> 已转录单元：Unit 1-6 全部 ✅
> 内容说明：教师用书栏目为 `Unit overview / Scenario / Active reading / Further exploration / Project / Extended reading / Self-reflection`，每栏下含 `Teaching suggestions`、`Reference answers`、语言点注释（Meaning / Notes / 词汇中文释义）以及课文参考译文；本文件按原书页序逐字转录。

# Unit 1 Get your foot in the door

## Unit overview

This unit focuses on the importance of developing soft skills at university to encourage Ss to start planning their career early, prepare themselves for their future career, and make contributions to society. The text in *Active reading* reveals the mismatch between British university Ss' personal skills and employers' requirements, illustrates some basic skills needed in the workplace and ways to acquire them. The text in *Further exploration* includes three overseas internship experiences shared by students from different countries, and shows what they've gained from their experiences. Then the audio presents the current university start-up trends and how Ss can get hands-on experience if they intend to found start-ups while at university. In *Project*, Ss are required to explain their own career plan, skills and experiences they have or lack, and the way to get better prepared. The essay in *Extended reading* analyzes the significance of cultivating the habit of lifelong learning to tackle challenges brought about by the rapid change of technologies and work processes. By learning this unit, Ss are expected to establish career goals, work hard to achieve them, and recognize their responsibility in making contributions for a better society.

（本页为单元结构流程图，各流程框内容如下）

**Scenario**
学校就业指导中心将举办一期职业发展交流会，学生向与会企业家和业内人士说明自己的初步职业规划、拥有和欠缺的技能、计划做的准备，并获取嘉宾的指导。

↓

**Active reading**（Are you the right person for the job?）
阅读文章，了解英国大学生的职业技能短板和解决方法，认识大学期间培养"软技能"的重要性及途径。

**Further exploration – Text**（Sharing overseas internship experiences）
阅读外国学生参加海外实习的经历，了解实习工作对提升职业技能、助力个人成长的重要意义。

**Further exploration – Audio**（The rise of entrepreneurship among university students）
聆听对话，了解大学生创业趋势以及在校期间如何为创业做准备。

↓

**Project**
介绍自己的初步职业规划，描述已经拥有和依旧欠缺的职业技能，并说明在校期间将如何提升个人技能以满足未来的职业发展需求。

↓

**Extended reading**（The mindset of a lifelong learner）
阅读关于终身学习习惯的文章，了解其如何帮助人们应对数字化与全球化给就业带来的挑战。

**Self-reflection**
回顾整个单元的学习，反思在内容、语言、结构、交际和行为上的收获。

↓

结合个人职业规划，分析自身职业能力的优势与劣势，掌握培养职业技能的方法，提升帮助他人、服务社会的意识，形成正确的职业观和终身学习的习惯。

## Scenario

The scenario in this unit can be analyzed from the following aspects:
**Setting:** Participating in a meeting held by your university's Student Career Center
**Topic:** Planned career path and preparation for it
**Identity:** Presenter – Students participating in the meeting
　　Audience – Entrepreneurs and other industry insiders
**Purpose:** To explain career plans and obtain guidance

**Teaching suggestions**
Since deciding on the potential career path is the most difficult part for the unit project, T may first emphasize the importance of making career plans early by analyzing the competitive job market. Then T may explain the basic career skills needed in most jobs to encourage Ss to think about how to prepare for their potential career. T can also ask Ss to reflect on their interest, strengths or weaknesses, family traditions, economic concerns or friends' advice to work out their career plan, and identify the skills and relevant experience they have or lack. Then Ss may share those ideas with partners or in groups to seek suggestions.

After the tryout, T can ask Ss to reflect on their difficulties in four aspects, i.e. content (what they have learned to do concerning the topic), language (the relevant expressions, sentence structures, and the language style they have learned), structure (how to organize the presentation), communication (how to tell personal anecdotes), and write them down in the table in the Student's Book. T may also do a small survey to see what the top three difficulties in doing this project are.

## Active reading

From *Scenario*, Ss have already known that they are expected to give a presentation on their career plan and explain how they will improve basic skills to achieve their goals. The text analyzes some important basic skills university students in the U.K. lack and offers effective ways to acquire and improve those skills. It can help Ss learn about basic skills needed in the workplace and possible ways to develop them. Besides, T can also use the text to increase Ss' awareness of the wider community and encourage them to make a difference in other people's lives and make the world a better place.

### Before you read

**Reference answers**
1 Other skills: Critical thinking skills; Management skills; Technical skills ...
2 Other ideas: Doing part-time jobs; Developing an entrepreneurial mindset; Improving emotional intelligence ...

## Are you the right person for the job?

### Text interpretation

本文着眼当下英国竞争激烈的大学生就业市场，揭示了目前大学生拥有的基本职业技能与雇主主要要求之间的差距，并提供了一些培养职业技能的办法。第一部分（第一至第三段）通过对比英国过去和现在的大学生活，指出拥有一个不错的学位不再意味着一定能找到一份好工作，而缺少基本职业技能是造成学生就业困难的主要原因。第二部分（第四段）以南安普敦大学历史系为例，介绍如何通过在学位课程中增加社交技能的训练，提升学生的基本职业技能。第三部分（第五至第八段）详细阐释了上述办法不仅有助于学生提高自己的就业能力，还能鼓励他们帮助他人，对社会做出应有的贡献。

本文列举了一些英国大学生欠缺的基本职业技能，并介绍了培养和提升这些技能的途径。基于课文，教师可首先引导学生认识到提升这些技能是"成人成才"的必由之路，对个人（职业）发展和建设中国式现代化都至关重要，人才是全面建设社会主义现代化国家的基础性、战略性支撑之一。作为新时期的大学生，立志成人成才是个人对建设人才强国的应有之义和必要之举，"功以才成，业由才广"，个人是否成才关乎国家和民族长远发展大计。由此鼓励学生反思自己需要提升的技能，并探索实现这一目标的有效方式。其次，教师可紧扣第六至第八段的文本内容，借助语篇中的词汇、句子进行隐性的课程思政，鼓励学生树立正确的职业观，在提高自己就业能力的同时，奉献报国、帮助他人、服务人民、回馈社会，将个人的梦想与追求与国家、社会发展相结合。
例 1："... that students should be aware of the wider community, and find ways to make contributions to it."（Para. 6）
　　鼓励学生关注社会，服务社会。
例 2："There are numerous ways in which students can make a difference to the world."（Para. 6）
　　鼓励学生为社会进步和发展做出个人贡献。
例 3：Typically, volunteering might mean helping the sick or elderly, entertaining needy or disabled children at holiday camps, or teaching in rural areas. It could also include working on agricultural or environmental projects.（Para. 7）
　　介绍不同的志愿者活动，鼓励学生踊跃服务人民、回馈社会。

本文总体上看是一篇说服性说明文，结构清晰，逻辑性强。作者首先呈现大学生缺乏基本职业技能的问题，再通过实例探讨该现象，最后提出解决问题的办法。本文较突出的写作手法是运用习语和

（页底截断，接下页）

隐喻等修辞方法，有效缓和了说明文的正式语气，增强了文章的生动性和说服力。教师可结合写作策略分析板块，引导学生理解和赏析这些修辞手法，并鼓励他们积累语言知识，在完成单元任务时积极使用这一表达方式。

### Language points

1 **You played your heart out on the football pitches and cricket fields, or paraded across the stage as a leading light of the university dramatic society. (Para. 1)**
　　play / work / sing etc. your heart out: put a lot of effort into playing, working, singing, etc. 尽情地玩耍、工作或唱歌等
　　*The students all played their hearts out at the graduation party.*
　　a leading light: an important, active, or respected person in a particular area of activity （某活动范围内）重要的活跃人物，受敬重的人物
　　*This football team lacks a leading light.*

2 **Whatever your interest, university life catered for it. (Para. 1)**
　　cater for: (BrE) provide people with everything they want or need 为……提供服务；满足……的需要
　　*We aim to cater for the need of customers of different ages.*

3 **And you would usually keep up with the work, too, by doing the required reading and dashing off the week's essay at the last minute. (Para 1)**
　　dash off: write or draw sth. quickly because you are in a hurry （因着急而）迅速写，迅速画
　　*Whenever he visits a place, he would dash off a sketch of the local people.*

4 **... graduates can no longer walk into the top jobs. (Para. 2)**
　　**Meaning:** ... university graduates cannot secure good jobs easily any more.
　　walk into: succeed in getting a job very easily 轻易获得（工作）
　　*Everyone was surprised when he walked into the top position.*

5 **... students are spending too much time studying, or worrying about making ends meet. (Para. 3)**
　　make ends meet: have only just enough money to buy the things you need 使收支仅能相抵；勉强维持生计
　　*To make ends meet, she works as a tour guide during the day and convenience store clerk at night.*
　　**Notes:** T may explain to Ss that many university students in the U.K. need to take part-time jobs to either pay off the money they have borrowed for university education or cover the cost of daily necessities.

6 **The managers also say that they are prepared to leave jobs unfilled ... (Para 3)**
　　**Meaning:** When an employee leaves their post unfilled, there is a vacancy (职位空缺). What the managers were saying is that if they could not find the right person for the job, they would rather employ no one.

7 **This includes making a presentation, writing a group thesis, and carrying out a public service. (Para. 4)**
　　carry out: do a particular piece of work, research, etc. 承担
　　*The building work was carried out by a local contractor.*
　　**Notes:** Other collocations include "carry out work / duties / research / an experiment / an investigation," etc.

8 **This sort of cooperative approach can help many students develop personal skills, or soft skills, which will help improve their prospects in their search for a job. (Para. 5)**
　　**Meaning:** By developing some basic career skills, university students will have more chances of finding a good job.
　　prospect: *n.* [C, usu. pl.] chances of success, esp. in a job or career 胜算；（尤指工作和事业方面）成功的可能
　　*We are highly optimistic about the future prospects of the company.*

9 **... a university is not an ivory tower of learning, cut off from the real problems of the world. (Para. 6)**
　　cut off: make a place difficult or impossible to enter, leave, or communicate with 使隔绝
　　*The city was cut off for hours after 10 inches of snow fell overnight.*
　　**Notes:** "An ivory tower" is a place or situation where you are separated from the difficulties of ordinary life and so are unable to understand them. The term is usually used to describe a college or university.

10 **On the contrary, university can be an agent for change for a better world. (Para. 6)**
　　**Meaning:** Instead of being isolated from the real world, university is actually an important place where students can work hard to make the world a better place.
　　agent: *n.* [C] (~ for / of change) sb. or sth. that affects or changes a situation 原动力，动因（指对事态起重要作用的人或事物）
　　*Technological advances are the chief agents of industrial change.*

11 **However, with courses making heavy demands on students' time, a popular option is to take a gap year before or after university. (Para. 6)**
　　**Notes:** The part "with courses ... time" is an absolute construction (独立主格结构) introduced by "with" which serves as an adverbial modifier for the main clause and, is often structured in a "with + noun / pronoun + verb-ing / verb-ed" pattern.

12 **Typically, volunteering might mean helping the sick or elderly, entertaining needy or disabled children at holiday camps, or teaching in rural areas. (Para. 7)**
　　**Meaning:** Typical volunteer work for university students may include offering help to the sick or the elderly people, giving performances at holiday camps to make poor or disabled children happy, or tutoring schoolchildren in rural areas.
　　entertain: *vt.* give a performance that people enjoy （通过表演）使快乐，给……娱乐
　　*In professional sports, the responsibility is on everyone to entertain the crowd.*

13 **For students who choose this path, one positive result is gaining a wealth of experience to be added to their CVs, ... (Para. 8)**
　　**Meaning:** Students who choose to do volunteer work will not only make a difference in other people's lives, but also reap an expected gain of valuable experience that can make their CVs more impressive.
　　a wealth of experience / knowledge / resources, etc.: a large number or amount of experience, knowledge, etc. 丰富的经验（或知识、资源等）
　　*The report contains a wealth of information about the IT industry.*

14 **But a word of warning is in order ... (Para. 8)**
　　**Meaning:** But a warning is suitable and necessary here.
　　a word of warning: used before a warning when giving advice 提醒一句
　　*Here's a word of warning: Do not overeat.*
　　be in order: be a suitable thing to do or say on a particular occasion 适宜；恰当
　　*Congratulations! I think a glass of champagne is in order, don't you?*

15 **... but I don't think you should be looking to bolster your CV through volunteering ... (Para 8)**
　　be looking to do sth.: be planning or expecting to do sth. 正打算（或期待）做某事
　　*We are looking to expand our business overseas.*

**Additional activity**
This language exercise is to help Ss learn more collocations related to "look" to expand their vocabulary bank.

Complete the sentences with phrasal verbs of "look."
look after　　look back　　look for　　look forward to　　look in
look into　　look through　　look to　　look up　　look up to
1 I will be near your mother's home tomorrow so I'll ______ and tell her you are doing well.
2 I am very busy ______ a job, but it isn't easy.
3 I can't remember, but you can ______ it ______ in the University Handbook.
4 It was hard work, but I am sure when you ______ you will think it was worth doing.
5 Will you ______ my assignment for me and see if you can spot any obvious mistakes?
6 My grandfather isn't very well, so I am going to visit and ______ him for a few weeks.
7 It's a big challenge – they are ______ me to score some baskets and save the team.
8 The police are ______ the robbery, and we hope they can find who did it.
9 Thank you for all your help. I really ______ meeting you next week at the conference.
10 She is a wonderful teacher, and we all ______ her and treasure our time with her.

**Reference answers**
1 look in　2 looking for　3 look ... up　4 look back　5 look through
6 look after　7 looking to　8 looking into　9 look forward to　10 look up to

**Additional activity**

The following activity will inform Ss of more volunteer work and encourage them to help those in need and make the world a better place.

Read the following list about different kinds of volunteer work. Then work in pairs and add at least two more to the list.

- Teaching in a Hope Primary school
- Taking part in a project to protect the environment

### Analyzing writing techniques

**1**
**Reference answers**
1-b; 2-a; 3-d; 4-c

**2**
**Teaching suggestions**
To help Ss fully understand the four figurative phrases in this exercise and practice using them when finishing the project, T may go further to draw Ss' attention to the cultural references of these phrases, or compare them with Chinese proverbs. For example, the phrase "people person" is used similarly to terms like "cat person (someone who likes cats)" or "morning person (someone who likes mornings)," which refer to someone who enjoys a particular thing. In this case, a people person is a person who likes other people. The phrase "two heads are better than one" can be easily understood and properly used if compared to the Chinese saying "三个臭皮匠，顶个诸葛亮". The phrase "put something to bed" first came from the publishing industry. The word "bed" used to refer to the flat portion of an old-style printing press that houses the type. In the old days when writers said "putting a newspaper or magazine to bed," they meant the final edits before publication. Later it came to mean "finish dealing with something" by extension. The phrase "know the ropes" is most probably derived from the days of sailing ships when sailors learned how to tie knots and handle ropes that were attached to sails. A sailor who was learning all these things was said to be "learning the ropes (学习做某事的诀窍)."

**Reference answers**
1-d; 2-c; 3-a; 4-b

### Sharing your ideas

**Teaching suggestions**
As the modern workplace often requires employees to change roles frequently, T may encourage Ss to think beyond the basic skills that they're already familiar with, and pay attention to the transferable skills they still need to improve. Skills, qualities, and experience Ss may talk about include but are not limited to:
- Interpersonal skills: communication skills, listening skills, flexibility and adaptability, emotional intelligence ...
- Technical skills: digital literacy, cloud computing, programming ...
- Decision-making skills: problem-solving skills, analytical skills, critical thinking skills ...
- Management skills: project management, motivating others, leadership, conflict resolution, goal setting ...
- Personal qualities: reliability, self-discipline, creativity, diligence, resilience, confidence ...
- Relevant experience: volunteering, taking an internship, being a class monitor, working in Students' Union or other student clubs, organizing or participating in campus activities, taking part in competitions ...

**Reference answers**
I am good at communicating with people. I am easy-going and enjoy talking with others, which has helped me make many friends, including the classmates in my group. This enables me to cooperate better with the others in group work, and even improves my leadership skills. In the workplace, many projects require people to work as a team. I believe my communication skills, cooperation skills, and leadership skills will be helpful in my future career.

Besides, I've had the experience of doing volunteering job in the University Sports Games. I was responsible for registration of athletes and recording their results. It enhanced my sense of responsibility and made me aware that every job is important, however ordinary it is. So when I get a job, I will take every work task seriously and do my best.

I think I still need to improve my English and critical thinking skills. With the increasing globalization, grasp of a foreign language will be more and more important. Critical thinking is another transferable skill that is crucial for career development, so I plan to join the debate club where I can learn how to view an issue from different perspectives and increase my ability in expressing myself.

**Suggestions for evaluation**
Ss' presentation can be evaluated according to the following criteria:
- whether Ss list both the skills, qualities, or experience they have and the ones they lack
- whether Ss explain clearly how these skills, qualities, or experience are related to their career development

## Further exploration

The task in *Scenario* asks Ss to present their career plans and exemplify how they're going to get prepared. The text in this part provides some university students' internship experiences to help Ss broaden their horizons and learn from others' experience. The audio takes a closer look at one possible career path, university start-ups, and offers advice to Ss who want to start their own business.

T can connect *Active reading* and *Further exploration* by reminding Ss that to achieve their goals and improve their skills, they need hands-on experience. T may guide Ss to think about what kind of experience they need and how to gain it. Ss should also reflect on how they can contribute to society and better prepare themselves for future careers.

## Sharing overseas internship experiences (Text)

### Language points

1 **I am a computer science major and Japanese minor. (Para. 1)**
　　minor: *n.* [C] the second main subject that you study in university for your degree （大学中的）副修科目
　　*The English majors and minors are given different assignments.*
　　**Notes:** Every university student has a major as the main focus of their study. Some students may choose to have a secondary area of specialization as their minor either to pursue personal interests, or to gain specific specialization and make themselves more competitive in the job market.

2 **As an intern at the university, my primary responsibility was helping Japanese students enhance their English skills by leading group chat sessions. (Para. 1)**
　　**I was responsible for rephrasing scientific research papers into layman's terms. (Para. 4)**
　　**... my main job was to do research on the labor policies and working conditions in developing countries. (Para. 6)**
　　**Notes:** This text provides different ways to describe one's job duties, such as "my primary responsibility was ..." "I was responsible for ..." and "my main job was to ..." Other useful expressions include: "I'm in charge of ..." and "My job duties regularly consist of ..."

3 **I started to reach out to people, be confident, and try to make friends. (Para. 5)**
　　reach out to sb.:
　　1) show people that you are interested in them and want to listen to them 愿意倾听
　　　　*Having a person, instead of a computer, reach out to John will help him combat the sense of isolation.*
　　2) offer help to sb. 为……提供帮助
　　　　*We are reaching out to the most vulnerable members of the community.*
　　3) ask sb. for help 请求……的帮助
　　　　*When you're in distress, you can always reach out to your friends and family.*

4 **This eye-opening experience allowed me to see the efforts people are making for a better future for all. (Para. 7)**
　　eye-opening: *a.* making you feel surprised or amazed 让人大开眼界的
　　*It was an eye-opening experience to see how my students used their talents to produce such a wonderful performance.*
　　**Notes:** An eye-opening experience refers to an experience that is unexpectedly enlightening. One can also use the noun phrase "an eye-opener" or "a real eye-opener" to describe something that surprises you and that you learn something new from it.

5 **The internship brought me new perspectives on what's going on around the world. (Para. 7)**
　　perspective: *n.* [C] a particular way of thinking or attitude about sth., esp. one that is influenced by sb.'s beliefs or experiences 角度；观点；想法
　　*He says their trip to a forest has given him a new perspective on life.*

**1**
**Reference answers**
1) enhance their English skills
2) facilitated
3) achieve their goals
4) cultural experience
5) scientific research papers
6) regarding their health and nutrition
7) important life lessons
8) labor policies
9) developing countries
10) direct the next phase

**2**
**Reference answers**
1 Because Hassan had never been to Japan before. This overseas internship gave him an opportunity to have close observation of Tokyo. He got to explore Tokyo's busy city streets and historical sites, which sparked his interest in Japanese culture.
2 Leonardo developed not only professional abilities as a writer and scientist, but also interpersonal skills when interacting with others. He learned to be more proactive, to step up and act promptly. He also learned to speak up, and realized that asking questions is a good way to get familiar with others and learn about the culture and the people around.
3 Chae-won's major is sociology, the study of society or of the way society is organized. Her internship allowed her to see the efforts people are making for a better future for all. She became interested in policies that affect the world and realized that she could use her major to help shape our society.
4 As a student majoring in business administration, I hope I could get an internship in some big trading companies, especially joint ventures. I know the sales department and marketing department of these companies usually hire a lot of interns and I hope I can have the opportunity to experience what it is like to work there.
Through this internship, I hope to find out how professionals work. I also hope I can sharpen my skills and broaden my knowledge in the field, and figure out what kind of work I like.

## （续）

**The rise of entrepreneurship among university students (Audio)**

### Scripts

**Host:** You're listening to "Campus Life." Today, we will be talking about entrepreneurship among university students. Joining us is Professor Zhang Lan, Director of the Student Entrepreneurship Center at our university. Welcome, Professor Zhang.

**Zhang:** Thanks for having me.

**Host:** Professor Zhang, what exactly does the center do to help young entrepreneurs?

**Zhang:** Well, the Student Entrepreneurship Center helps transform students' academic experience into career readiness. Our staff provides students with access to resources, points them to possible funding, and offers advice as students develop their paths to success. We also offer seminars and training courses such as business plan writing, and organize other campus-wide events to help them prepare.

**Host:** Those sound really useful. After helping so many students, what trends have you noticed in university start-ups?

**Zhang:** Quite a few. For example, more women are founding start-ups. More people outside of tech fields are developing creative solutions to meet new market demand. More students from rural areas are seeing opportunities in their hometowns – they are trying to address the needs of the local people.

**Host:** So university entrepreneurs are more diversified. But what do they have in common? What is the most important thing that students can learn from them if they want to start a business?

**Zhang:** A good entrepreneur needs to be passionate about solving real-world problems. This is how they come up with great business ideas. They are good at finding a problem or a need, and figuring out what product or service they can provide to solve that problem or meet the demand.

**Host:** Some students may have great business ideas, but they still lack hands-on experience. What can they do?

**Zhang:** A good way to gain experience is to apply for internships. They can learn how to start and run a business by observing how others are doing it, and by completing the work tasks they're assigned. If a student can land an internship or two in their freshman year, they can get a head start and acquire some necessary skills and invaluable experience for their future career.

**Host:** Thank you for sharing your insight. I hope more students will be encouraged to put their business ideas into practice.

### Background information

In 2014, China launched its mass entrepreneurship and innovation (大众创业、万众创新) initiative in order to promote technological innovation, encourage industrial growth, and boost employment. The Chinese government and universities are encouraging entrepreneurship (弘扬企业家精神) and helping students build their own start-ups. They have offered various innovation and entrepreneurship training programs (大学生创新创业培训项目) and organized innovation and entrepreneurship competitions (大学生创新创业大赛). Learning how to found their own start-up is an important way for each university student to enhance their sense of social responsibility, scientific research ability, innovation ability, communication ability and collaboration ability. The national policy has created excellent opportunities for the younger generation to dream big, work hard, and shape their own future, and move faster to help Chinese companies become world-class outfits.

### Language points

**1 Thanks for having me.**

**Notes:** This is a polite expression of gratitude, particularly when a guest has just arrived or is about to leave. In the audio, it is directed toward the host for given the opportunity to take part in the event.

**2 Well, the Student Entrepreneurship Center helps transform students' academic experience into career readiness.**

**Meaning:** The Student Entrepreneurship Center helps turn the knowledge and skills students have learned in university into what will make them successful in the workplace. Here, "career readiness" can be translated into 职业胜任力.

**3 More students from rural areas are seeing opportunities in their hometowns – they are trying to address the needs of the local people.**

**Meaning:** More students are trying to respond to and serve the needs of the local people.

**address:** *vt.* try to deal with a problem or question, for example by thinking carefully about it, or by doing things to improve a situation 处理；应对

*Scientists are trying their best to address the problem of global warming.*

**4 Some students may have great business ideas, but they still lack hands-on experience.**

**hands-on:** *a.* hands-on experience or training involves you doing sth. rather than just reading about it or watching other people do it 实际操作的；亲身实践的

*This program gives medical students hands-on experience in a hospital.*

**5 If a student can land an internship or two in their freshman year, they can get a head start and acquire some necessary skills and invaluable experience for their future career.**

**land:** *vt.* get sth. that you wanted, esp. a job or opportunity 得到，获得（自己想要的东西，尤指工作或机会）

*Bill has just landed himself a part in a Broadway show.*

**head start:** *n.* [C] an advantage that helps you to be successful 起步时的优势

*The reading course is designed to give young children a head start in learning a foreign language.*

**1**

**Reference answers**

1) career readiness
2) diversified
3) passionate
4) business ideas
5) internships
6) observing
7) completing

**2**

**Reference answers**

1) 1, 3, 4, 5, 6
2) 1, 2, 5

### Sharing your ideas

**Reference answers**

As a student majoring in civil law, I'm looking forward to becoming a lawyer and making a difference to people's lives, and eventually making our society a better place. I wish to work in big law firms where I can meet excellent people and learn from them.

To achieve that, I need to excel in my study and pass the qualification examination. Studying law is extremely difficult as there is so much to learn, but I have always been passionate about my major, which makes it a lot easier for me.

To be a good lawyer, I also need to work on my communication skills. A lawyer should be able to present cases and explain legal procedures and rights to clients in a clear, concise, and persuasive way, and compose well-written legal documents. Many of these rely on my speaking and writing skills. To develop these skills, I am going to take public speaking and writing courses in university, and keep improving my skills by taking internships in law firms.

**Suggestions for evaluation**

For this activity, the evaluation could focus on whether Ss can clearly state what they wish to do in the future, and whether they show a sufficient understanding of what the career requires. They should be able to describe where their strengths and weaknesses lie and what specific measures they will take to develop the skills they lack.

## Project

**Sample**

**(\*Words and expressions in red are from the input materials.)**

Good morning, distinguished guests and my fellow classmates. I am Wang Lin, a freshman majoring in software engineering. Now, I will take this opportunity to present my career plan, and I'd appreciate your comments and instructions.

My career plan is to found a start-up that creates apps especially for the elderly to make their life easier and better. One reason is that I am fascinated by the way that information technology has helped shape our society. Another is that I hope the technological advancement can make a big difference to the elderly's lives.

Last semester, I joined in a volunteer program, helping the elderly in a nursing home once a week. I noticed that some seniors were frustrated by the complex operating system of the social media apps when they tried to make contact with their families. I realized it's time to build some senior-friendly apps that cater for their needs. I put step-by-step operating instructions in the app and enlarged the font size. The seniors liked this app very much because they could use it without asking others for help, and the words are easy to read. This rewarding experience not only bolstered my confidence in starting my own business, but also brought me new perspectives on how technology could affect the elderly's lives.

However, I understand innovative thinking, keen observation, and a caring heart are not enough for establishing a start-up. I need to develop more soft skills such as leadership and communication skills to achieve my goal. Therefore, I would like to apply for an internship as a software engineer at an IT company to push myself to grow both personally and professionally.

I believe an internship experience will help me develop some personal skills essential for creating my start-up. For one, through cooperating with team members to complete a project, I will not only learn how to communicate ideas clearly and work well with others, but also learn from the project manager how to pick team members, organize the work, and check for early warning signs. For another, by committing myself to an assignment until completing it, I will become more persistent, so that I can stick to my goal no matter what challenges come my way.

To sum up, I believe building a start-up to create apps for the elderly is a good career choice for me, and an internship at an IT company could better prepare myself for my goal. Thank you very much for your attention.

**Suggestions for evaluation**

| Components | Criteria / Traits |
| --- | --- |
| **Content** | • Relevance: Presenting a career plan and ways to get better prepared<br>• Appeal: Sharing a personal experience to make the presentation entertaining and persuasive |
| **Organization** | • Structure: Giving the presentation in a clear and well-organized order<br>• Coherence: Using transitional words and phrases to make the presentation cohesive and coherent<br>• Clarity: Arranging the presentation with a logical layout and highlighting the key points<br>• Technique: Using a personal anecdote to engage the audience |
| **Language** | • Accuracy: Using expressions and sentence structures accurately and effectively<br>• Diversity: Using an adequate range of vocabulary and sentence structures<br>• Acquisition: Using as many as possible new words, expressions, and grammatical structures learned in the unit |
| **Delivery** | • Fluency: Delivering the presentation with a natural, colloquial flow<br>• Engagement: Keeping eye contact with the audience and using body language to engage the audience |

## Extended reading

In the previous parts, Ss have talked about the skills and experience they need to get ready for the future career. Ss should be aware that knowledge gained from classroom learning is far from enough. The text in *Extended reading* first clarifies the necessity of lifelong learning, describes the cycle of "learn, unlearn, and relearn," and then illustrates the importance of lifelong learning especially in this age of rapid technological development. After reading this essay, Ss may better understand the importance of soft skills in their personal and professional development.

**The mindset of a lifelong learner**

### Culture notes

**World Economic Forum**

The World Economic Forum (WEF) is an international non-governmental organization based in Geneva, Switzerland. Founded in 1971, the foundation views its own mission as "improving the state of the world by engaging business, political, academic, and other leaders of society to shape global, regional, and industry agendas."

The WEF is mostly known for its annual meeting at the end of January in Davos, Switzerland. The meeting discusses global issues for five days. In regards to the future of work, the 2020 WEF set the goal of providing better education, skills, and jobs to one billion people by 2030.

### Language points

**1 In fact, the technological changes in progress will create more jobs in different sectors if history is any indication. (Para. 1)**

**in progress:** *(fml.)* happening at this time 在进行中

*I turned on the television and found the ceremony was still in progress.*

**Notes:** Here, "if history is any indication" implies that what happened in history can be seen as an indication of what will happen tomorrow, that is, technological development may make many jobs out of date, but will create new jobs as well. This is one of the reasons why we have to keep learning: We'll need to adapt to the new jobs.

**2 The question then becomes not whether a machine will take your job, but whether you will rise to the challenge of learning to stay relevant and employed. (Para. 2)**

**Meaning:** This sentence is connected with the last sentence in Paragraph 1. Machines may take away some jobs, but at the same time may also create new jobs. So if you can cope with the challenge by learning new skills, you will still be competitive, and will not be out of work.

**rise to the challenge / occasion:** deal successfully with a problem or difficult situation, esp. by working harder or performing better than usual 成功应对挑战或困难局面

*John rose to the occasion and defeated his opponent.*

**Notes:** In the expression "stay relevant and employed," "stay," when followed by an adjective, means to continue to be in a particular state or situation. And "relevant" here means being valuable and useful to the job.

**3 …, you claim the power to face the future of work successfully. (Para. 2)**

**claim:** *vt.* gain, win, or achieve sth. 获得；赢得；取得

*After years of practice, he finally claimed a place in the National Table Tennis Team.*

**4 This is a cycle of "learn, unlearn, and relearn." (Para. 3)**

**unlearn:** *vt.* deliberately forget sth. you have learned, in order to change the way you do sth. 设法忘记，抛弃（已学会的东西）

*Sometimes scientists have to unlearn conventional theories in order to make new discoveries.*

**Notes:**

"Unlearn" is formed by adding to the verb "learn" a prefix "un-" (used to show an opposite of a particular action). So the word means an opposite process to learn. When you learn something, you get new skills, ideas, and so on; when you unlearn something, you forget them, and eliminate the effect of learning them. There are several other examples formed in this way, including "illiterate" in Paragraph 3, "deconstruct" in Paragraph 5, and "disinterest" in Paragraph 11.

In the word "relearn," "re-" means again, especially in a better way.

**5 There are a lot of metaphors for unlearning, such as chipping away at the old paint before you put on a fresh coat, or clearing away the weeds before planting something new. (Para. 5)**

**Notes:** Metaphors, by comparing one thing to something else, can help readers vividly visualize unfamiliar concepts or situations. The two metaphors given aim to help readers better understand the process of unlearning and implies a metaphor for relearning. In the metaphors, unlearning is compared to chipping away at the old paint, and clearing away the weeds, while relearning is compared to the subsequent actions, putting on a fresh coat and planting something new.

**6 When a professor, for the first time, delivers a keynote speech online to hundreds of people on the other side of the world without leaving home, he steps out of his technological comfort zone, unlearns "old ways" of doing things, and relearns the skill of working from home. (Para. 6)**

**Meaning:** When a professor gives a keynote speech to people from all around the world in an online conference, it may be his first time to use the technology to connect with the world from home. In this case, he gets rid of the old ways of giving speech – in a hall, in front of hundreds of people, using microphone and projector – and learns the skill for giving a talk in a teleconference.

**comfort zone:** *n.* [C] a situation or position in which a person feels secure, comfortable, or in control, esp. when they choose to stay in this situation instead of trying to work harder or achieve more 舒适区（尤指自我放松、不追求更高成就的状态）

*A common reaction to changes is retreating to a comfort zone, but a better approach is to step out of it and face the challenges.*

**Notes:**

1) A keynote speech is given to set the theme for an event. It is often given by experts in the field.

2) People may feel everything under control in the comfort zone, without anxiety, stress, or uncertainty. But if they always stay in the comfort zone, they will gradually lose the nerve to take challenges, and will not be able to reach their full potential. That is why young people are often encouraged to step out of the comfort zone to live a more fulfilling life.

**7 For workers set to remain in their roles, nearly half will need to retrain their core skills. (Para. 7)**

**set:** *a.* likely to do sth.; ready for sth. or to do sth. 有可能的；做好准备的

*(– to do sth.):* *We were set to leave the mountains for home before it got dark.*
*World population is set to reach 9.8 billion by 2050.*

*(– for):* *He was set for a full evening of cheerful entertainment.*
*The museum is set for a major expansion.*

**core:** *a.* most important; main or essential 最重要的；主要的；基本的

*Sports can help to teach core values such as honesty and respect.*
*We offer courses in core subjects such as mathematics and reading.*

**Notes:** The word "set" in this sentence is used as an adjective, modifying the word "workers."

**8 Increased digitalization and globalization lead to extreme flexibility when it comes to hiring new talent. (Para. 8)**

**when it comes to:** used to identify the specific topic that is being talked about 当涉及到……；当谈到……

*When it comes to listening to music, Mike always prefers the music which can calm himself down.*

**talent:** *n.* [C, U] people or a person with a natural ability to do sth. well 有才能的人；人才

*Big companies often have the advantage of attracting and retaining top talent.*
*He's considered one of the greatest talents in physics.*

**Notes:** The words "digitalization" and "globalization" are formed in the same way.

digit *n.* + -al → digital *a.* + -ize → digitalize *v.* + -ation → digitalization *n.* （+ -ization）

globe *n.* + -al → global *a.* + -ize → globalize *v.* + -ation → globalization *n.* （+ -ization）

The verb suffix "-ize" can be added to a noun or an adjective and transform it into a verb. Examples include "modernize," "privatize," "standardize," and so on. The noun suffix "-ization" is a combination of "-ize" and "-ation," a word-forming element making nouns of action, process, or state. Examples include "modernization," "privatization," "standardization," and so on.

**9 They shop for workers based on skills rather than official qualifications or job titles. (Para. 8)**

**Notes:** The expression "shop for" is used metaphorically in this sentence. When employers select workers, they evaluate and compare them, a process reminding us of shopping.

**10 Employers see skills in self-management, such as active learning, resilience, and flexibility, as growing in importance in the coming years. (Para. 8)**

**Notes:** Self-management is the ability to manage one's emotions, thoughts, and behaviors effectively in different situations. This includes controlling anger, managing stress, sticking to schedule, concentrating on work at hand, keeping fit, and so on.

The skills in self-management which the author thinks will have growing importance in the coming years include: active learning (the ability to motivate oneself to learn new things), resilience (the ability to cope with setbacks, and to bounce back from difficult experiences), flexibility (the capacity to adjust to changes, to deal with unexpected problems or tasks effectively).

**11 These truths apply to both personal and professional development. (Para. 9)**

**apply to:** have an effect on or concern a particular person, group, or situation 对……有效；对……适用

*The discount applies only to children under the age of eight.*
*Consumer protection laws apply to both online and offline businesses.*

**12 This translates not only into greater happiness and satisfaction, but also promotes the soft skills that are appreciated in the workplace. (Para. 10)**

**Notes:** The word "translate" basically means to change written or spoken words into another language. The meaning of the expression "translate into" is extended beyond the context of languages. If one thing translates into another, the second thing is the changed form or the result of the first.

Soft skills are the skills that are often acquired through hands-on experience rather than school courses or vocational training. They are transferable and not job-specific. The soft skills that are appreciated in the workplace may include communication, team work, creativity, critical thinking, leadership, time management, decision making, and so on.

**translate (sth.) into sth.:** if one thing translates into another, the second thing happens as a result of the first 引起；造成

*A small increase in local spending will translate into a big rise in council tax.*

**13 Professional development, on the other hand, is learning transferable skills that boost your value in the workplace. (Para. 11)**

**boost:**

*vt.* make sth. increase, or become better or more successful 使增长；使兴旺

*The share price of this company climbed again today, boosted by rising sales.*
*He wanted to boost students' performance in school and help them find jobs.*

*n.* [C, usu. sing.] sth. that gives sb. more confidence, or that helps sth. increase, improve, or become successful 帮助；激励；鼓舞

*Vitamin supplements can give your immune system a boost.*
*Her friends' support gives her a boost in confidence in the last round of the competition.*

**14 Technologies and work processes change rapidly these days, and disinterest in learning their usefulness translates into disinterest in helping your employer. (Para. 11)**

**Meaning:** Technologies and work processes are developing rapidly. Companies have to keep pace with the changes to thrive. If workers are not interested in learning about and using new technologies or get familiar with new work processes, they will be seen as not interested in contributing to the development of their companies, which could put them out of work.

**15 The outcome? You are first in line for layoffs. (Para. 11)**

**Meaning:** What is the result of your disinterest in learning about the new technologies and work processes? You will be the first one to lose your job.

**be in line for sth.:** be very likely to get or be given sth. 很有可能获得某物

*Farm workers are in line for a pay raise this year.*

**16 Lifelong learners invent and reinvent themselves whenever they find or create the opportunity. (Para. 12)**

**Meaning:** Whenever lifelong learners find or create for themselves the opportunity to learn, they will unlearn the old ideas, and start to learn the new things and update their skills. They keep changing themselves in this way to adapt to the changes around them.

**Reference answers**

1 C　2 C　3 B　4 D　5 A

## Self-reflection

In this part, T can ask Ss to refer back to "Try it out" in the Student's Book and reflect on what they have learned to complete the task. Ss can categorize their gains according to the same four aspects, i.e. contents, language, structure, and communication. They can also reflect on how learning the unit may help them establish their career goals, and encourage them to work hard to achieve them as well as make contributions to society. Then they can write down their specific gains in the Self-reflection table in the Student's Book.

## Translation of the texts

**Active reading**

**你适合做这个工作吗？**

1　过去，大学生活很轻松，你也不用担心找工作的事。你花时间在咖啡馆里与新结交的朋友聊到深夜，在足球场和板球场上尽情地挥洒汗水，或是作为大学戏剧社的大腕在舞台上昂首阔步。不管你有什么爱好，大学生活都能为你创造条件。而且你的学习还总能跟得上，能完成指定的阅读，并在最后一分钟匆忙写完那一周的文章。你唯一没有时间考虑的是过完这激动人心的三年后要做什么。不过这个问题你无需考虑，因为无论你选择什么职业，都有一大堆公司排着队来聘用你。

2　那是过去英国大学生校园生活的情形，现在情况已经改变了。一项对英国各大跨国公司的研究表明，即使拥有一个不错的学位，大学毕业生也不再可能轻而易举就得到最好的工作了。过去 20 年里，英国的大学数量增加了 40%多，超过 50%的年轻人接受高等教育。有了大量的大学毕业生，获得一个不错的学位对找工作就变得至关重要了。竞争很激烈，如果如今的大学毕业生花了比任何时候都要多的时间来准备期末考试或者兼职以偿还债务。

3　然而，这恰恰是问题之所在。英国两百多家公司的经理认为，学生花大量的时间在学习或挣钱维持生计上。他们本应该找时间去参加各种俱乐部，学习一些基本的技能，如团队合作和现场演示。这些经理还说，他们宁可让职位空缺，也不愿意聘请那些缺乏必要技能、无法在国际市场上取得成功的毕业生。

4　该怎么做才能解决这个问题呢？经理们相信，解决的办法就是在学位课程里增加社交能力的训练，而且有的大学已经开始这样做了。例如在南安普敦大学，历史系学生必须做一个为期十二周、六或七人一组协同工作的项目——通常与当地的生活有关。项目内容包括：做演示、写集体论文、做公众服务。服务内容可能包括给中、小学生讲课，或者就某一话题做一期广播节目。

5　这种合作学习法能帮助许多学生培养有助于改善就业前景的个人技能，或软技能。雇主们知道，拥有软技能的人通常能清晰地表达想法、很好地与他人合作并保持井井有条。这类人通常更擅长在截止日期前高质量地完成任务，证明他们是可靠的、值得信赖的。

6　南安普敦大学项目中同样有趣的一个理念是：学生应该关注比校园更广阔的社区，并设法为之做出自己的贡献。在当今越来越小的世界里，学生们越来越清楚地意识到大学并不是与社会现实问题完全脱钩的学术象牙塔。正相反，大学本身可以促使世界变得更加美好。学生也可以通过很多方式改变世界。例如，在学学位课程之前、期间或之后做志愿服务工作在学生中就很受欢迎。但是，由于大学课程需要学生花费大量的时间，一般人会选择腾出上大学前或大学毕业后的—年时间作为实践年。

7　通常，志愿工作指帮助病人或老年人，在假日营里为待困儿童或残疾儿童表演节目，或在农村地区支教。它还可能包括参与农业或环境项目。

8　对那些选择从事志愿服务的学生而言，还有个意外的收获：可以把他们获取的丰富经验写进个人履历里，而未来的雇主是不会注意不到这些经验的。不过提醒一句：你应该记住自己的首要目的是什么。曾经在巴布亚新几内亚工作了七年的沙恩·欧文指出："志愿者的工作能教给你宝贵的职业技能，但我认为你不应该只想着通过志愿者工作来给自己的履历表增添光彩——你做志愿工作的主要原因应该是你想帮助别人。"

**Further exploration**

**分享海外实习经验**

**哈桑（美国，芝加哥）**

1　我的专业是计算机科学，辅修日语。我喜欢参与到东京大学学生的生活当中并为他们的生活带来改变。作为一名东京大学的实习生，我的主要职责是指导日本学生的小组讨论活动，帮助他们提高英语技能。

2　我觉得我最大的成就之一就是与这些学生建立联系。能够促进他们的学习并帮助他们实现目标，这样的感觉真的很棒。

3　这次实习让我有机会去探索东京繁忙的城市街道和历史古迹。它激发了我对日本文化的兴趣，给了我一次有益的文化体验，尤其是因为我之前从未到过日本。

**莱昂纳多（古巴，哈瓦那）**

4　我是一名生物学专业的学生，辅修数学。我在牙买加的一家食品和营养研究所的实习经历促进了我在个人和专业方面的发展。我的工作是将科学研究论文改写为通俗的语言。我很开心能够帮助普通人了解有关他们健康和营养的研究。

5　这次实习不仅提升了我的写作和科研的能力，还鼓励我更加积极主动，迈开步伐，及时行动。我意识到，如果不询问就无法得到答案，也无法了解我周围的人和它文化。我开始主动与他人接触，变得更自信，并尝试结交朋友。这些都是我从这段经历中学到的重要人生经验。

**彩媛（韩国，首尔）**

6　主修社会学的我有幸在瑞士日内瓦的国际劳工组织（International Labour Organization）获得了一个暑期实习机会。在那里，我加入了"消除童工国际计划"（IPEC），主要工作是研究发展中国家的劳工政策和工作条件。我向主管汇报了我的研究发现，并很高兴地得知我的部分发现被用于指导一些 IPEC 援助项目下一阶段的工作。

7　这一令人眼界大开的经历让我看到了人们为全人类的美好未来所做的努力。我开始对影响整个社会的政策产生了兴趣，也看到了这些政策如何影响我们的个人生活。这次实习让我从新的视角观察世界各地正在发生的事，并让我意识到我所学的专业在帮助塑造我们的社会这方面大有可为。

**Extended reading**

**终身学习者的心态**

1　那些关于机器"偷走"我们工作的科幻故事似乎正在变成现实。随着今天智能电子技术和人工智能技术的发展，这看起来更加现实。机器将抢走数百万人的工作的说法可能只是部分正确，我们无法确定确切的数字。事实上，按照历史的发展迹象来看，正在发生的技术变革将在不同的领域创造更多的就业机会。

2　那么问题就不是机器是否会抢走你的工作，而是你是否会迎接挑战，学习如何与时俱进，保住自己的工作。当你成为一个终身学习者，专注于不断学习工作所需的新技能时，你就获得了成功面对未来工作的能力。

3　做一个终身学习者意味着什么？有人说："未来的文盲不再是不识字的人，而是没有学会如何学习的人。"我们必须能够看到旧的思想何时过时，并知道如何、何时替换它们。这是一个"学习、摒弃、再学习"的循环。

4　学习一些你真正感兴趣的东西总是令人愉快的。有些东西学起来要比学其他东西花费更长的时间（达到精通可能需要一辈子的时间），但我们确实每天都在学习。

5　然而，摒弃完全是另一回事。摒弃是对思维方式、行为方式和反应方式中的一部分发起挑战，进行解构。关于摒弃有很多比喻，比如在刷新油漆之前要先把原来的刮掉，或者在开始种植之前先清除杂草。它们都指向同一件事：以前的思想、信念和假设必须被移除，新的思想、信念和假设才能蓬勃发展。

6　摒弃可以是一个真正的转变，可以完全改变一个人的观点。然而，事情还不止于此。我们学习，摒弃，然后重新学习。在这个瞬息万变、飞速发展的世界里，这个终身学习的循环是如此重要。当一位教授第一次足不出户，向身处世界另一端的数百人发表线上主题演讲时，他走出了自己的技术舒适区，摒弃了做事的"老方法"，重新学习并学到了居家工作的技能。

7　现在，终身学习尤其重要，比以往任何时候都重要。在过去十年中，劳动市场发生了巨大的变化，并且仍在快速变化着。根据最近的一份报告，公司对自动化的应用将彻底改变任务、工作和所需技能。未来五到十年里，雇主会逐渐将工作任务大致平均地分配给人类和机器。对于打算留在岗位上的员工来说，近一半的人需要对自己的核心技能进行再培训。

8　不断发展的数字化和全球化使得招聘新人才有了极大的灵活性。公司比以往任何时候都更多地选择兼职人员、基于项目的独立承包商或自由职业者。他们根据技能来挑选员工，而不是资历或职位头衔。雇主们认为自我管理方面的技能在未来几年将变得越来越重要，如主动学习能力、适应力和灵活性。这些技能是对现有技能的补充，如批判性思维能力和解决问题能力。

9　熟练掌握一项技能并不能确保长期就业。如果你在精通一项技能之后放弃学习，你在劳动力市场的竞争力仍然会下降。要保持工作中的胜任力，就有必要让学习新技能成为一种持续的习惯。这对于个人发展和职业发展都适用。

10　把个人发展看作是学习过程，学习所有可以强化你的头脑、提升自信的东西。这不仅会带来更强的幸福感和满足感，还会提升在职场中倍受欣赏的软技能。

11　另一方面，职业发展是学习可转移技能，提高你在职场中的价值。如今，技术和工作流程变化迅速，对学习使用它们漠不关心就等于对帮助雇主不感兴趣。结果呢？你会是第一个被解雇的人。

12　随着计算机、人工智能系统、机器人和其他机器越来越聪明、能力越来越强，人类职员要想保持胜任工作、留在岗位上，就必须同样越来越聪明、有能力。终身学习者不管什么时候，只要能发现机会或创造机会，都会塑造、再塑造自己。这与跳槽无关。这是为了确保你能继续为目前的工作创造利益。即使你不得不去找一份新工作，这也会让你成为有价值的人才。

# Unit 2 Strength from within

## Unit overview

This unit focuses on empathy and other important emotional abilities, and introduces ways to build healthy interpersonal relationships. The text in *Active reading* analyzes how empathy originates and develops, emphasizes the significance of empathy to interpersonal relationships, and introduces ways to improve empathy. The text in *Further exploration* presents a personal account about smiling in different cultures and explains how smiling can boost mood and improve social interactions. Then the audio introduces a simple strategy to cope with social anxiety and explains how it works. In *Project*, Ss are expected to give an oral response to a voicemail message to offer help for forging strong interpersonal relationships. The essay in *Extended reading* presents an alternative perspective to see emotional issues and deal with them. By learning this unit, Ss are expected to improve their ability to empathize with others, cope with their own difficult emotions, and develop better social relationships.

> **Scenario**
> 学校心理援助中心以回复热线留言形式为国内外学生提供心理援助服务。留学生 David 在留言中诉说他在人际交往中的苦恼和困扰。作为中心志愿者，学生将给 David 回电，帮他解决问题。

**Active reading**（How empathy originates and develops）
阅读介绍同理心的产生及后天培养的说明文，认识同理心对个人及人际交往的重要意义，学习提升同理心的有效方法。

↓

**Further exploration – Text**（Keep smiling）
阅读通过微笑改善人际关系的故事，理解微笑能提振个人情绪的原因，了解微笑是一种有效促进社交的方式。

↓

**Further exploration – Audio**（How to turn social anxiety into excitement）
聆听对话，学习缓解社交焦虑的方法及其对促进社交行为的重要作用。

↓

**Project**
站在求助者 David 的角度思考，帮助他分析遭遇社交困扰的原因，解释同理心的产生和提高方法，鼓励他在人际交往中善用同理心，并有效策略改善人际关系。

↓

**Extended reading**（The joy of sadness）
阅读关于终身学习习惯的文章，了解其如何帮助人们应对科技发展给就业带来的挑战。

↓

**Self-reflection**
回顾整个单元的学习，反思在内容、语言、结构、交际和行为上的收获。

↓

从有效管理个人情绪和对他人的困难感同身受两方面了解提升情商、增进人际交往的有效方法，形成正确的社交观，增强同理心和应对负面情绪的能力。

### 本单元课程思政元素备选清单

| 素材 | 课程思政参考点 | 二十大精神 |
| --- | --- | --- |

## Scenario

> The scenario in this unit can be analyzed from the following aspects:
> **Setting**: Volunteering at the Mental Health Support Center of your university
> **Topic**: Building better relationships
> **Identity**: Caller – David, an international student seeking help with relationship issues;
> Respondent – a student volunteer at the Center
> **Purpose**: To help students improve empathy and build better interpersonal relationships

**Teaching suggestions**

Many freshmen have problems in getting along with others in a new environment. T may first encourage Ss to recall their previous experiences, thinking about what problems they have and how they coped with it. Then Ss can share their experiences and strategies to build better interpersonal relationships in pairs or in groups, so that they may learn more from each other about the various reasons that may cause relationship problems and practical approaches to deal with them. This could help Ss examine David's concern from his perspective even if they don't know much about how empathy originates or how to improve it.

After the tryout, T can ask Ss to reflect on their difficulties in four aspects, i.e. contents (what they have learned to do concerning the topic), language (the expressions and sentence structures they have learned), structure (how to organize the response), communication (how to show empathy to better offer help), and write them down in the table in the Student's Book.

## Active reading

> In *Scenario*, Ss are required to explain how empathy originates and develops in order to help David understand that everyone has this emotional ability, and that he doesn't need to be anxious about social interactions. The text in this part not only traces the origin and development of empathy in early childhood, but also offers various ways for adults to improve their empathic ability and analyzes the importance of empathy in building strong social connections. It can help Ss understand the important role empathy plays in living a healthy life, e.g. its importance for both physical and psychological well-being, and learn more ways to improve their empathy, e.g. listening carefully to other people's feelings and thoughts.

### Before you read

**Reference answers**

1 Empathy: 1, 3, 4, 6
2 Sympathy: 2, 5

## How empathy originates and develops

### Text interpretation

本文第一段通过具体研究事例提出文章主题：同理心是每个人与生俱来的能力。第二至第四段以婴幼儿成长阶段为线索，阐释不同年龄段同理心的发展情况。第五段和第六段分别介绍儿童和成年人如何提升同理心。第七段强调同理心对人际交往和个人身心健康的重要价值。

学习文章时，教师可基于语篇中的词汇、句子进行隐性的课程思政教学。首先引领学生了解同理心不仅是一项与生俱来的能力，而且可以通过后天努力得到提高。认识到提升同理心能提高情商、改善人际关系、增强社交能力，有利于促进心理健康、提升个人幸福感。如文章结尾总结到，"The ability to empathize ... is important for both physical and psychological well-being"。基于此共识，教师可进一步引导学生，个人（人民）身心健康和幸福感也是民族昌盛和国家强盛的重要标志，是健康中国的重要组成部分。所以提升同理心于己、于国都有重大意义。进而鼓励学生尝试文中介绍的提升同理心的方法，并思考更多的有效途径，促使学生形成积极正确的社交观。

另外，文中多处提及同理心重在关爱他人、对他人的疾苦感同身受，如 "comfort a crying friend"（Para. 2）和 "have compassion for and relate to loved ones, co-workers, and even strangers"（Para. 7）。这一品质与中华优秀传统文化中的"修睦""善邻"和社会主义核心价值观中的"友善"高度契合。同理心能使家人、朋友、同学、同事，甚至陌生人之间都能互相关心、互相帮助，这也是实现人与人、人与社会和谐相处的重要条件之一。

本文体裁可归于科普类说明文。为使读者理解文中的心理学术语和概念，作者列举了大量具体研究实例。教师可结合写作技巧板块帮助学生学习使用具体事例解释抽象概念。文章语言正式，长句和复杂句也较多，教师应给予充分讲解，以便学生更好地理解同理心及相关概念。

### Culture notes

**Martin L. Hoffman**

Martin L. Hoffman (1924 – 2022) was an American psychologist and a professor emeritus at New York University. His career largely focused on the development of empathy, and its relationship with moral development. His research also touches on areas such as empathic anger, sympathy, guilt, and feelings of injustice.

**E. B. Titchener**

Edward Bradford Titchener (1867 – 1927) was an American experimental psychologist who is best known for developing structuralism, an important school of thought in psychology. He is also remembered for introducing the term "empathy" into

（页底截断，接下页）

the English language in 1909. According to Titchener's structural psychology, empathy was not a matter of understanding other minds, but rather a projection of imagined bodily movements and accompanying feelings into an object. Now the term generally refers to two constructs. The first involves the process of placing oneself into another person's shoes, referred to as perspective-taking or role-taking. The second involves the experiencing of emotional reactions to the other person's situation.

**Marian Radke-Yarrow**

Marian Radke-Yarrow (1918 – 2007) was an American child psychologist known for studying topics such as prejudice, altruism, and depression in children. She worked in academia in the early years and was one of the few women who headed a laboratory at National Institutes of Health (NIH) in the U.S., leading the developmental psychology laboratory at the National Institute of Mental Health from 1974 to 1995.

**Carolyn Zahn-Waxler**

Carolyn Zahn-Waxler is a developmental psychologist and a senior research scientist at the University of Wisconsin-Madison. Her most cited studies discuss how empathy develops in the first few years of life, indicating that children are able to understand distress in others and display empathetic behavior as young as one year old, and this increases substantially during the second year of life. Her work also focuses on how the relationship between parent and child as well as the general environment in which the child grows up can either increase or decrease these types of behavior in children.

### Language points

1 **The moment Hope, just nine months old, saw another baby fall, tears** welled up **in her own eyes. (Para. 1)**

**well up**: if a liquid wells up, it comes to the surface of sth. and starts to flow out （液体）涌溢，开始流淌

*Dark blood was welling up quickly from the wounded soldiers.*

**Notes**: In this sentence, "the moment" introduces an adverbial clause of time roughly meaning "when" or "as soon as." Similar expressions include "the minute" and "the instant."

2 **She crawled off to be comforted by her mother, as though it were she who had been hurt. (Para. 1)**

**Notes**: The adverbial clause of manner introduced by "as though" uses the subjunctive mood (虚拟语气) to suggest that Hope was not hurt, the condition contrary to the past fact. This clause also includes a typical "it"-cleft (it 强调句型) that puts "she," Hope, into focus, while "were" instead of "was" is used to indicate the subjunctive mood.

3 **... when Paul kept crying, Michael fetched Paul's security blanket for him. (Para. 1)**

**Notes**: A security blanket is a blanket or piece of cloth that young children carry to comfort themselves or help them feel more confident. Besides a security blanket, some parents give their baby a toy or a thing as a source of comfort when the baby is in distress, for example, a dummy (a plastic or rubber object for a baby to suck).

4 **Both these small acts of caring were observed by mothers trained to record such incidents of empathy in action. (Para. 1)**

**Meaning**: Mothers who were trained to record such acts of the baby recorded the acts of empathy.

**in action**: sb. or sth. that is in action is doing the job or activity they are trained or designed to do 在工作；在运转

*I'd like to see the new computer system in action.*

5 **... infants are upset when they hear another infant crying – a response some researchers** see as the earliest form of empathy. **(Para. 1)**

**Meaning**: Infants become unhappy when they hear another infant crying, which is a reaction regarded by some researchers as the earliest form of empathy.

**see ... as ...**: think about or consider sb. or sth. in a particular way, or as having particular qualities 把……看作……；视……为……

*Mary sees the project as a new challenge to her career.*

6 **On seeing his mother cry, one baby wiped his own eyes, though there were no tears. (Para. 2)**

**Meaning**: As soon as a baby saw his mother cry, he started to wipe his own eyes even though there were no tears.

7 **He sought a word that would be** distinct from **"sympathy," which can be felt without sharing the same feelings. (Para. 3)**

**seek**: vt. (sought, sought) (fml.) try to achieve or get sth. 寻求；争取

*If you are worried about your health, you should seek medical advice.*

**distinct**: a. separate and different in a way that is clear 有区别的；不同的；单独的

*The European Union is made up of nations with distinct cultural, linguistic and economic roots.*

8 **Motor mimicry fades from toddlers' behavior at around two and a half years of age. (Para. 4)**

**Meaning**: This imitation of others' movements or actions gradually disappear when the young children are about two and a half years old.

9 **She then tries to stroke his hair, but he** pulls away **... (Para. 4)**

**pull away**:

1) move away from sb. who is trying to hold you or touch you 闪避；躲开

*The caregiver reached out to help the girl stand up, but she was in too much pain and pulled away.*

2) if a vehicle or driver pulls away, they start to move （车辆或司机）开走

*The police signaled the driver to pull away from the middle of the road.*

10 **At this point in their development, toddlers begin to differ from one another in their overall sensitivity to other people's emotional upsets. (Para. 5)**

**Meaning**: At around the age of two and a half, children become different from each other in their development regarding how sensitive they are to others' emotions of sadness, worry, or anger.

11 **Some, like Jenny, become keenly aware while others** tune out. **(Para. 5)**

**Meaning**: Like Jenny, some children become strongly aware of other people's emotions, while others stop paying attention.

**tune out**: (infml.) stop paying attention 不注意；不理睬；不过问

*Some kids have attention spans of about 10 minutes before they tune out.*

12 **They found that children are more empathic when they are made to see the effect of their behavior on others: ... (Para. 5)**

**Meaning**: If parents could make young children realize how their behavior affects the emotions of others, they would be more empathic.

**Notes**: Both "empathic" and "empathetic" describe the ability to understand and relate to other people's feelings or experiences. They are often used interchangeably in a variety of contexts. The major difference, however, may be a general trend in the use of these words. "Empathic" is often used in scientific or technical contexts, while "empathetic" is more commonly used in everyday language and informal writing.

13 **While it is easier to train a person from childhood to be empathic, it is also possible for adults to increase their levels of empathy. (Para. 6)**

**Notes**: Both the main clause and the adverbial clause have a dummy subject "it" (it 做形式主语). The infinitive subject of the adverbial clause, "to train a person from childhood to be empathic," has been moved to the end of the sentence to become a delayed subject. In the main clause, the infinitive subject "to increase their levels of empathy" is the delayed subject. The original sentence can be rewritten roughly as "Although training a person from childhood to be empathic is easier, increasing adults' levels of empathy is also possible."

14 **For example, we may** empathize with **people anxious about tests by listening carefully to their frustrations and fears. (Para. 6)**

**empathize with sb. / sth.**: be able to understand sb. else's feelings, problems, etc., esp. because you have had similar experiences 有同感；产生共鸣

*His ability to empathize with people made him an excellent marriage counselor.*

15 **The ability to empathize allows us to** have compassion for **and** relate to **loved ones, co-workers, and even strangers. (Para. 7)**

**have / feel / show compassion for**: have or show a strong feeling of sympathy for sb. who is suffering, and a desire to help them （感到或表示）同情，怜悯

*We should always have compassion for those in difficulties and help them whenever we can.*

**relate to**: be able to understand a situation or the way that sb. feels and thinks 理解；认同

*She's a great teacher because she always relates to her students, which allows her to adapt her teaching methods to match their needs.*

> **Additional activity**
> **Work in groups and complete the following activity.**
>
> **Step 1**
> Divide Ss into groups of four or so. Ss in each group are given the following roles: Martin L. Hoffman, E. B. Titchener, Jenny's mother, and Marian Radke-Yarrow.
>
> **Step 2**
> Ask each student to read the passage again, paying particular attention to the sentences related to their role.
>
> **Step 3**
> Groups discuss how each of them will act out their part, and how each student should speak in their role, for example, they may have to change their way of speaking by using first-person pronouns.
> e.g. *I am Martin Hoffman from New York University. From my research, I have noticed that a one-year-old brought his own mother over to comfort a crying friend, ignoring the friend's mother, who was also in the room ...*
>
> **Step 4**
> Each group acts out the passage and one group presents their role-play to the class.

**3**

1) More and more Chinese parents can empathize with their children when disciplining them, rather than merely criticize their wrong behavior.
2) People with high levels of empathy tend to give highly accurate responses to others' emotional upsets.
3) She has experienced the pain of being misunderstood by others, so she has compassion for those in the same situation.
4) As a developing country itself, China can relate to the difficulties that other developing countries are facing.
5) There are many self-regulation strategies that can ease anxiety, for example, listening to the *guqin* can help you calm down.

### Sharing your ideas

**Reference answers**

I totally understand David's concerns about interpersonal relationships, especially since international students have to face culture shock. I want him to know the problem is not that he doesn't have empathy, because we are all born with the ability to empathize with others. What's more, there're many ways for adults to increase their levels of empathy, so there's no need to lose confidence or get anxious.

Virtually from the day we were born, we start to show empathy toward others' upsets. Research has found that infants may cry when they hear another infant crying or try to comfort infants in distress. These are early forms of empathy. This ability develops as we grow up and can be enhanced by appropriate parental disciplines, which is important for interpersonal relationships because it allows us to connect with others on a deeper level by understanding their thoughts and feelings.

Although it is easier to train children to be empathetic, adults can also improve empathy in various ways. For example, we can practice active listening to understand how others think and feel. To be a better listener, we should put away our phones when having a conversation so that we can fully listen and pay more attention to the speaker's facial expressions and gestures to better understand them.

**Suggestions for evaluation**

For this activity, the evaluation could be carried out based on the following criteria:

- Whether Ss can explain clearly the origin and development of empathy
- Whether they can empathize with David by examining his anxiety and analyzing the possible reasons behind his point of view

## Further exploration

> In the task in Scenario asks Ss to offer strategies to help David build better relationship. Therefore, Ss should first help David manage his social anxiety to boost his confidence in interacting with other students. The text in this part can help Ss understand how keep smiling can evoke pleasant feelings and improve social interactions. The audio explains how saying "I am excited" can help people cope with social anxiety.
> T can first ask Ss to connect *Active reading* and *Further exploration* by summarizing their gains in *Active reading*, then ask Ss whether they have experienced social anxiety, how they coped with it, and if they want to learn two more simple ways to deal with the problem. This will spark their interests and prepare them to develop strategies to help David.

## Keep smiling (Text)

### Language points

1 **A couple of months ago, I read an interesting article in a science journal about how people from different cultures** reveal **their emotions through facial expressions. (Para. 1)**

**reveal**: vt. make sth. known to sb. 揭示；显示；透露

*His e-mails reveal a different side of his personality.*

**Notes**: The time adverbial "a couple of months ago" indicates that the author is going to introduce the topic with a story, which is an effective way to attract readers' attention.

2 **India was quite near the bottom of the list – 124th,** to be precise, **while Denmark was at the top. (Para. 1)**

**to be precise**: used for saying that the information you are giving about sth. is more exact than what you have said before 确切地说

*To be precise, the total cost of the project was $25,670, not $26,000 as estimated.*

**Notes**: Here, "to be precise" serves as a parenthesis which is separated from the rest of the sentence by commas.

3 **I remember reading somewhere that we use a lot more muscles when we frown than we do when we smile. (Para. 3)**

**Notes**: The objective clause compares the number of muscles people use when they frown and smile. In the comparative construction, the auxiliary verb "do" is used instead of "use" in the constituent following "than" as a substitute expression to avoid repetition.

4 **But we still need to make an effort to smile in social situations – it's a** learned **behaviour, not a reflex action. (Para. 3)**

**Meaning**: Even though smiling is a simple behavior, we still need to put energy to smile when socializing with others because smiling in social situations is not instinctive, but a skill developed through training.

**learned**: a. developed by training or experience; not existing at birth 通过训练（或经历）形成的；学到的；非天生的

*Effective communication is a learned skill that requires practice and patience to master.*

5 **But the curious thing is that even as I lift those muscles at the side of my mouth, it makes me happy. (Para. 3)**

**Notes**: The conjunction "even as" is similar in meaning to "while" or "at the same time as," and "the curious thing" indicates a contrast or a surprising connection between two things happening simultaneously. This sentence places emphasis on the contrast or potential opposition between the behavior and the feeling, highlighting the unexpected emotional response of the author while forcing himself to smile.

6 **By adopting a happy facial expression, we can "trick" our mind into entering a state of happiness. (Para. 3)**

**trick sb. into (doing) sth.**: deceive sb. in order to get sth. from them or to make them do sth. 骗某人做某事

*Sweeteners can trigger our body's taste receptors and trick them into believing it's actually sugar.*

**Notes**: In this sentence, the word "trick" is used as a rhetorical device known as a metaphor. The author uses it to compare the action of adopting a happy facial expression to a clever, intentional act of deception, as if one is pulling a trick on the mind. The metaphor is effective in conveying the idea that smiling can give us a positive psychological implication, so we can get ourselves into a state of happiness by making a smiling face. It also creates a vivid image in the reader's mind and emphasizes the idea that managing one's facial expression can have a powerful impact on one's mental state.

7 **Sitting next to me was a young man who moved his bag to make more room for me. (Para. 4)**

**Notes**: This sentence is a typical example of full inversion. The subject complement, "was sitting next to me," is put before the subject, "a young man who moved his bag to make more room for me," to achieve a good sense of balance because of the long subject.

8 **The next thing I knew, we had** settled into **our seats and were chatting together about ourselves (in English). (Para. 4)**

**the next thing I knew**: (infml.) used when sth. surprising happens very suddenly 接下来；等我反应过来时

*I heard a voice. The next thing I knew, the power went out and everything went dark.*

**settle into**: make yourself comfortable in a place because you are going to stay there for a long time 安定下来；安心呆在（某处）

*After traveling for weeks, it was nice to finally settle into my hotel room.*

### How to turn social anxiety into excitement (Audio)

### Scripts

**Host:** You are listening to "Campus Life." Today, we will talk about how to cope with social anxiety. Joining us is Professor Julia Brooks, a psychologist. Welcome.

**Brooks:** Thanks for having me.

**Host:** Social anxiety is very common in our lives. Many people are afraid of going to social events or talking to strangers. Some even feel nervous when starting a daily conversation with people they know, and don't know how to respond properly. If you're one of them, you've probably heard this advice: Take a deep breath and calm down before starting to talk. Is that effective, Professor Brooks?

**Brooks:** That might be exactly the wrong thing to do. Instead, you might want to try saying three little words to yourself: "I am excited."

**Host:** I am excited?

**Brooks:** Right. We have conducted studies about this. We had people say "I'm excited" before attending a social event or starting a conversation, and when people said that, they reevaluated their social anxiety as excitement in these social situations. Then they actually communicated better with others, and their interpersonal relationships started to improve.

**Host:** It sounds too simple to be true! How does it work?

**Brooks:** Actually what you're really doing is getting yourself out of a threat mindset, where you're focused on all the things that could go wrong. Instead, you get into an opportunity mindset, where you're

（页底截断，接下页）

（接上页）

thinking about all the good things that could happen.

**Host:** So before they went to a social event, such as a co-worker's birthday party at a karaoke bar, they told themselves that they were excited about celebrating with the others or meeting new friends, and they gradually fit into the party?

**Brooks:** That's right. They gave themselves positive implications and felt more comfortable about social situations, which helped them interact with others better.

**Host:** I still find it a little hard to believe. Why does this strategy work better than taking a deep breath?

**Brooks:** Well, that's because anxiety and excitement have a lot more in common than you might think. They're both aroused emotions. In both your pulse becomes faster, and the body prepares for action. It's much easier to get from anxiety to excitement than it is from anxiety to calm.

**Host:** I see. It really is a simple but effective strategy to overcome social anxiety. Thank you for coming in today, Professor Brooks.

**Brooks:** It's my pleasure.

### Language points

**1 Joining us is Professor Julia Brooks, a psychologist.**

**Notes:** In this inverted sentence, the subject complement "joining us" is put before the subject "Julia Brooks" to emphasize the behavior. The noun phrase "a psychologist" is the appositive to provide the reader with more information about the subject.

**2 Instead, you might want to try saying three little words to yourself: "I am excited."**

**Notes:** "You might (or may) want to do something" is used to suggest politely that someone take a certain action. It is often used when you want to create a friendly tone and avoid any misunderstanding that could lead to a negative response. For example, you could say "You might want to spend more time studying and less time playing video games."

**3 ... when people said that, they reevaluated their social anxiety as excitement in these social situations.**

**(re)evaluate ... as ...:** categorize or conceive of sb. or sth. (again) in a certain way in order to judge or grade them appropriately （重新）评价；将……（重新）评估为……

*After receiving feedback from her boss, she reevaluated her approach as too rigid and made adjustments.*

**4 Actually what you're really doing is getting yourself out of a threat mindset, where you're focused on all the things that could go wrong.**

**mindset:** n. [C] a way of thinking about things 思维方式；心态；思路

*The book accurately captures the mindset of a teenage girl.*

**Notes:** The sentence here consists of a non-restrictive attributive clause introduced by relative adverb "where" to further explain that if you have a threat mindset, you pay more attention to the negative results or problems you may experience. Besides, the subject of the main clause is a subject clause beginning with relative pronoun "what."

**5 ... they told themselves that they were excited about celebrating with the others or meeting new**

（页底截断，接下页）

（接上页）

**friends, and they gradually fit into the party?**

**fit into sth.:**
1) be accepted by the people in a group or organization 与……相处融洽
*The manager was glad to see that the new employee fitted into the team very well.*
2) be part of a group or system 成为……的一部分
*Some of the patients we see do not fit neatly into any of the existing categories.*

**6 They gave themselves positive implications and felt more comfortable about social situations, which helped them interact with others better.**

**Notes:** When giving yourself positive implications, you're using positive thinking to approach life's challenges with a positive outlook. It doesn't mean seeing the world through rose-colored lenses by ignoring the negative aspects of life, but viewing yourself and your abilities in a positive light.

**7 Well, that's because anxiety and excitement have a lot more in common than you might think.**

**have ... in common:**
1) (~ with sth.) if objects or ideas have sth. in common, they share the same features 与……有相同的特点
*Despite the fact that they were written by different authors, the two books have a lot in common with each other.*
2) (~ with sb.) have the same interests, ideas, etc. as sb. else 与某人（兴趣、想法等方面）相同
*Larry have almost nothing in common with Tim. How did they become friends?*

**8 It's much easier to get from anxiety to excitement than it is from anxiety to calm.**

**Meaning:** Anxiety and excitement share lots of same features. Therefore, when you are anxious, making yourself feel excited is much easier than making yourself become calm.

**Notes:** The sentence uses a comparative construction with two dummy subject "it." The real subject of the former is "to get from anxiety to excitement," and the original delayed subject of the latter is "to get from anxiety to calm." However, "to get" in the second part is omitted to avoid redundancy.

**1**

**Reference answers**

1) social events
2) nervous
3) I am excited
4) excitement
5) threat
6) opportunity
7) comfortable
8) calm

**Reference answers**

1) His initial reaction is one of disbelief. He thinks that the strategy sounds too simple to be true.

（页底截断，接下页）

（接上页）
