# Unit 4 Horns of a dilemma

## Unit overview

This unit focuses on the significance of moral awareness and aims to help Ss gain moral inspiration from literary works, consider the moral aspects when making decisions, and practice moral principles in their life. The text in *Active reading* is a well-known short story by American writer O. Henry, in which the main character made the right decision when faced with a dilemma. The text in *Further exploration* shows how analyzing the character's dilemma in a renowned literary work can provide moral inspiration. Then the video reminds Ss to always be aware of morality, and listen to their inner voice for moral hints. In *Project*, Ss are required to make comments on the decision of the main character in *Active reading*, and talk about the moral inspiration they get from reading the story. The essay in *Extended reading* illustrates the two choices we may have – engagement or avoidance – when we engage dilemmas, moral dilemma engagement might be a better approach to make the world better. By learning this unit, Ss are expected to establish moral values, increase moral awareness, and behave morally.

（本页为单元结构流程图，内容如下）

**Scenario**
英语学习论坛的在线读书分享会将聚焦欧·亨利的短篇小说《二十年后》，分析人物在面临两难处境时所做的决定，并讨论其中的道德启示。学生将参与此次读书分享会，并于分享会开始前在论坛上发布自己写的读后感。

↓

**Active reading**（After twenty years）
阅读欧·亨利的短篇小说《二十年后》，厘清故事脉络、人物关系、情节冲突，分析主人公做出的决定。

→ **Further exploration – Text**（The Moral dilemma of Jean Valjean）
阅读对《悲惨世界》中人物道德行为的评论，学习如何分析故事中的困境，评论人物的选择，总结获得的道德启示。

→ **Further exploration – Video**（Moral awareness）
学习视频材料，了解提高道德意识、听从内心道德指引的重要意义。

↓

**Project**
分析《二十年后》故事中的人物关系及人物处境，评论人物在两难处境中做出的决定，从中获得道德启示。

↓

**Extended reading**（Should we avoid or engage moral dilemmas?）
阅读关于在道德困境中如何选择的说明文，分析直接面对和逃避道德困境的不同结果。

↓

**Self-reflection**
回顾整个单元的学习，反思在内容、语言、结构、交际和行为上的收获。

↓

结合经典文学著作阅读，树立正确道德观，认识提升道德意识的重要性，时刻提醒自己注意行为道德，并在生活中践行道德准则，在面临道德困境时做出正确的选择。

**本单元课程思政元素备选清单**

| 素材 | 课程思政参考点 | 二十大精神 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Active reading (After twenty years) | 1）自觉尊法、学法、守法、用法；<br>2）情与法冲突时，要明辨是非，维护法律至高无上的尊严。 | 通过本单元的学习，学生能够自觉弘扬社会主义法治精神，做社会主义法治的忠实崇尚者、自觉遵守者、坚定捍卫者。同时，弘扬中华传统美德，明大德、守公德、严私德，提高自身道德水准和文明素养。 |
| Further exploration – Text (The moral dilemma of Jean Valjean) | 在法律和道德面前，人人平等。每个人都应该为自己的行为负责。面临道德抉择时要坚持真理和正义，永远不能放弃道德原则。 | （与上格合并） |
| Further exploration – Video (Moral awareness) | 视频素材强调道德意识的重要性，教导学生倾听内心良知的召唤，在面临道德问题时做出正确的选择。这一观点与中国传统文化思想不谋而合。例如，"修身齐家治国平天下"的理念中，道德修养是修身的重要内容；明代阳明心学中，"致良知"是核心要义。"良知"即是先天道德观念，知善恶是非，而"致良知"就是依良知做事，存善去恶。 | （与上格合并） |
| Extended reading (Should we avoid or engage moral dilemmas?) | 面临道德困境时，应该积极应对，不要逃避，以自己的行为影响周围的人，促进社会良好风尚的形成。 | （与上格合并） |

## Scenario

The scenario in this unit can be analyzed from the following aspects:

**Setting:** Posting a reflective journal for an online reading-sharing session

**Topic:** The moral lesson of *After twenty years*

**Identity:** Poster – Students
　　　　　Viewer – Other participants of the reading-sharing session

**Purpose:** To share the moral inspiration gained from the story and learn to make the right decision when faced with moral dilemmas

**Teaching suggestions**

T can ask Ss to read through the text in *Active reading* before class to get a rough understanding of the story. In class, T can help students clear up the main plots of the story by asking suggestive questions. For example: What are the identities of Jimmy and Bob? What dilemma is Jimmy faced with? What does he do in the end? Ss can conclude the moral inspiration they gain from the story by answering these questions and share their thoughts about Jimmy's decision.

After the tryout, T can ask Ss to reflect on their difficulties in four aspects, i.e. content (what they have learned to do concerning the topics), language (the expressions and sentence structures they have learned), structure (how to organize a reflective journal), and communication (how to write a reflective journal in an instructive way), and write them down in the table in the Student's Book. T can also do a small survey to see what are the top three difficulties Ss have in doing this project.

## Active reading

In *Scenario*, Ss are expected to write about the moral inspiration they get from the short story *After twenty years*. In the story, the main character Jimmy chooses to have his friend Bob, the wanted criminal who comes back from the West to keep his appointment with Jimmy, arrested. It reveals a moral rule that we should always be loyal to the law, even if it conflicts with personal relationships. Ss can learn O. Henry's writing strategy, i.e. producing a surprise at the end while dropping hints to make the ending believable. Ss can use the hints to analyze Jimmy's moral dilemma and comment on his decision.

### Before you read

**1**

**Reference answers**

✓ *The gift of the Magi*
✓ *The cop and the anthem*
✓ *The last leaf*

O. Henry's other short stories include: *The ransom of red chief*, *A retrieved reformation*, *The furnished room*, and so on.

**2**

✓ Writing mostly about ordinary life (In his writings, he uses dialogues from his own childhood and his experience with people of various classes.)
✓ Using humorous language (He uses devices like metaphor, irony, puns, and exaggeration to make his writing humorous and amusing.)
Using short, one- or two-syllable words (This is often considered to be a typical feature of Hemingway's writing style.)
✓ Giving stories surprising endings (The endings are surprising, but reasonable.)
✓ Combining tragedy and comedy (This is also called "tearful smile." Some stories have sad endings, but they still contain hope, warmth, and true feelings, which make readers smile bitterly.)

O. Henry's other writing styles:
- Often containing local culture of North Carolina, where he was born
- Exaggerating characters' traits and making the stories interesting
- Often using allusions, particularly to ancient classics and Shakespearean plays

### After twenty years

### Text interpretation

本文是美国作家欧·亨利的短篇小说，发表于 1906 年。故事的主人公 Jimmy 和 Bob 是好朋友。他们年轻时各奔前程，20 年后如约再见时，Bob 是通缉犯，而 Jimmy 是一名警察。Jimmy 以其正直的个性和对法律的忠诚，在 Bob 没认出他的情况下，请一位便衣警察逮捕了 Bob。第一部分（第一至第二段）描述故事发生的背景——寒冷的夜晚，Jimmy 在少有人迹的街上巡逻。第二部分（第三至第十七段）情节推进，Bob 与 Jimmy 碰面，但没有认出 Jimmy，只当对方是一个"陌生的"巡逻警察，并向对方解释他来赴 20 年前的约定。第三部分（第十八至第二十七段）情节进一步发展，一名便衣警察装作 Jimmy 来与 Bob 会面，Bob 也没有发现对方的真实身份。第四部分（第二十八至第三十二段）是故事的高潮，同时也是小说的结局。Bob 意识到眼前的人不是 Jimmy，便衣警察挑明身份，告诉 Bob 他已被逮捕，并交给他 Jimmy 留下的字条。Bob 读了 Jimmy 给他的字条，幡然醒悟刚才遇到的巡逻警察正是 Jimmy。结局在意料之外，又在情理之中。

小说中 Jimmy 意识到从千里之外赶来与他赴约的老朋友正是被通缉的罪犯时，便面临着情与理的内心冲突。最终 Jimmy 选择了忠于法律，呼应了小说开头对他的描写：和平的守护者。故事以旁观者的角度讲述，没有对人物进行褒贬评述，给读者留下思考和回味的空间。

基于对小说内容和人物的理解，教师可引导学生思考每一位公民的社会责任，做社会主义法治的忠实崇尚者、自觉遵守者、坚定捍卫者。每一位公民都应尊法、学法、守法、用法，用实际行动捍卫法律的尊严，并努力影响周围每一个人。当法律与情感发生冲突时，要保持头脑清醒，明辨是非，牢记法律面前人人平等。小说中 Jimmy 珍视与 Bob 的友谊，没有忘记二十年前与老朋友的约定，但是当发现 Bob 是被通缉的罪犯时，他坚定地选择了捍卫法律。在情与法的冲突中，Jimmy 没有迷失方向，做出了正确选择。

同时，教师也可以引导学生塑造正确的价值判断。小说中，Bob 无疑发了大财。如果以金钱作为价值标准，他似乎是成功的，文中描写也处处透露着他的自豪感。但是金钱真的可以作为衡量人生价值或成功与否的首要标准吗？如果为了金钱不择手段，置法律与道德于不顾，这样的人生还是有价值的吗？与 Bob 形成鲜明对照的 Jimmy 在平凡的岗位上尽职尽责，维护法律和道德的底线。这样的人应该受到我们的尊重。

小说以时间顺序展开，包开端、发展、高潮、结局四个部分。写作手法上体现了欧·亨利的典型写作特征——出奇不意的结局。叙事中作者设置了悬念，同时也有充足的铺垫，使得读者在阅读时充满新奇感。读到结尾处时，既有意外带来的惊讶，也有"原来如此"的释然。学生可以结合理解练习厘清小说发展脉络，理解人物关系和冲突；在填空练习中夯实关于人物特征分析的词汇表达，并在产出任务中有效使用。

### Culture notes

**1 The West**

The West is the term most often used to describe the area of the U.S. lying to the west of the Mississippi River. However, in the past the frontier line moved further west as the country was settled. So some regions such as the Mid-West, which includes the city of Chicago, and the South, are only considered "western" today because of their frontier heritage. People used to seek their fortune in the West because it had cheap land and a lot of opportunities to get rich by mining or farming.

**2 Drug store**

In the U.S., a drug store is a shop that sells medicine as well as many everyday items and perhaps food and drinks. It is like a pharmacy, but with a wider range of goods for sale.

**3 Chicago**

Chicago, Illinois, is the third-largest city in the U.S. In the early 20th century, it was known for having many well-dressed gangsters and criminals. Bob, with his diamonds and egotism, is recognizable as one of these gangsters.

### Language points

**1 The policeman on the beat** moved up the avenue impressively. (Para. 1)

**on the beat:** walking around an area that a police officer has responsibility for and must walk around regularly （警察）在巡逻
*The authorities intend to increase the number of police on the beat.*

**2 ..., but chilly gusts of wind with a taste of rain in them had well nigh de-peopled the streets.** (Para. 1)

**Meaning:** Because of the awful weather, with cold wind and coming rain, there were almost no people on the street.

**well nigh:** almost 几乎
*It would be well nigh impossible to get there in an hour.*

**Notes:** The prefix "de-" is often used to show that something is removed or reduced. Here "de-peopled the streets" means reduced the number of people on the streets, or made the streets deserted.

**3 ..., the officer, with his stalwart form and slight swagger, made a fine picture of a guardian of the peace.** (Para. 2)

**Meaning:** The officer, strong and walking in a slightly proud way, looked like a guardian who protected peace.

**4 The vicinity was one that kept early hours.** (Para. 2)

**Meaning:** People in this area closed stores early in the evening.

**keep regular / strange etc. hours:** if you keep regular, strange, etc. hours, the times at which you do things (esp. getting up or going to bed) are regular, strange, etc. 做事（尤指作息）时间有规律（或怪异等）
*Keeping early hours helps to maintain a healthy lifestyle and improve productivity during the day.*

**Notes:** The word "hours," when used in the plural form, often refers to how long you spend each week doing your job and when you do it. The phrase "keep early hours" means to stop working early every day. Other commonly used expressions include: keep late / regular / long / strange hours. For example:
*He has kept late hours since he took this job.*
*To effectively manage your workload, it is necessary to keep regular hours for work and breaks.*
*It is hard to find time to develop hobbies for those who keep long hours.*
*Musicians and artists often keep strange hours to fuel their creativity.*

**5 Well, I'll explain if you'd like to make certain it's all straight.** (Para. 4)

**Meaning:** Well, I'll explain if you want to make it certain that what I said was true.

**straight:** a. honest and truthful 坦诚的；真实的
*Having a straight talk is the best way to eliminate misunderstanding.*

**6 We figured that in twenty years each of us ought to have our destiny worked out and our fortunes made, whatever they were going to be.** (Para. 7)

**Meaning:** We believed that 20 years later, we would have made some money, and our destiny and life path would have been set, whether good or bad.

**work out:** find the answer to sth. 找到……的答案；解决
*We have worked out a way of increasing the quality of our products.*

**make one's fortune:** gain a very large amount of money and become very rich 发财；发迹
*Henry Ford made his fortune with the Model T.*

**Notes:** In this sentence, "have sth. done" means to cause something to be in a state of being finished. The past participle can be changed into an adjective or a present participle to mean "cause something in a particular state." Here is an example:
*I have to have everything ready tonight for tomorrow's conference.*

**7 But after a year or two we lost track of each other.** (Para. 9)

**lose track (of):** no longer know where sb. or sth. is or what is happening 不了解……的动态；与……失去联系
*I moved so many times when I was young that I lost track of many of my childhood friends.*

**Notes:** The phrase "keep track (of)" is an expression with opposite meaning to "lose track of."

**8 You see, the West is a pretty big proposition, and I kept hustling around over it pretty lively.** (Para. 9)

**Meaning:** You see, you can do a lot of business in the West, and I was always busy making my fortune there.

**9 I've had to compete with some of the sharpest wits going to get my pile.** (Para. 13)

**Meaning:** I've had to compete with many smart people in order to make some money.

**sharp:** a. clever or quick to notice things 聪明的；精明的；敏锐的
*An excellent journalist should have a sharp mind.*

**wits:** n. [pl.] your ability to think quickly and clearly and to make good decisions 理解力；头脑；智力
*The young man has achieved success by his own hard work and wits.*

**Notes:** Here "the sharpest wits" refers to the smartest men with sharp minds, and "pile" means a large amount of money.

**10 A man gets in a groove in New York. It takes the West to put a razor-edge on him.** (Para. 13)

**Notes:** Bob means that living in the West is demanding, and you have to be alert to the possible dangers all the time. He himself has to be alert to "the smartest wits" who rival with him for money. People in New York tend to live a routine life. Bob thinks Jimmy should have some exciting experience, like the life in the West, to spur him. A razor is something used to shave, and a razor edge is supposed to be very sharp. If a finger is on a razor edge, it is in a danger of being cut and bleeding. So "on the razor edge" metaphorically means "in a difficult situation where any mistake may be very dangerous." In this sentence, "put a razor-edge on him" refers to becoming alert and competitive after surviving a difficult situation.

**11 Going to call time on him sharp?** (Para. 15)

**Meaning:** Are you going to leave if your friend doesn't turn up at 10 o'clock?

**call time (on sb. / sth.):** say or decide that it is time for sth. to finish 宣布结束；决定结束
*The referee decided to call time on the match due to the severe weather conditions.*

**12 ..., the man who had come a thousand miles to fill an appointment, uncertain almost to absurdity, with the friend of his youth, smoked his cigar and waited.** (Para. 18)

**Meaning:** The appointment is uncertain, almost absurd, because it was made 20 years ago, between two friends who have been thousands miles away and lost track of each other. The man who traveled long distance from the West to New York to fill this appointment smoked his cigar and waited.

**Notes:** In this sentence, "uncertain almost to absurdity" is an adjective phrase, used to modify "an appointment." The appointment was so uncertain that it almost sounded absurd.

**13 How has the West treated you, old man?** (Para. 22)

**Notes:** "Old man" is an old-fashioned, informal expression used by older men of the middle and upper classes as a friendly way of addressing another man.

**14 The man from the West, his egotism enlarged by success, was beginning to outline the history of his career.** (Para. 27)

**Meaning:** The man from the west was very proud of his success, and began to tell his friend his experience in the West.

**Notes:** Here "his egotism enlarged by success" is an absolute construction; "his egotism" is the logic subject, followed by a past participle phrase.

**15 At the corner stood a drug store, brilliant with electric lights.** (Para. 28)

**Meaning:** There stood a drug store at the corner. It was bright because electric lights were in it were on.

**Notes:** The former part of this sentence is in an inverted order. The normal order should be "A drug store stood at the corner ...," and the adjective phrase following it modifies "a drug store."

**16 Chicago thinks you may have dropped over our way and wires us she wants to have a chat with you.** (Para. 31)

**Meaning:** Chicago police think it is likely that you have arrived here and have sent us a telegram saying that they want to have you arrested (for the crime you have committed).

**drop over:** visit sb. casually or unexpectedly 顺便访问
*The kids drop over and see the retired teacher from time to time.*

**Notes:** In this sentence, "... may have done..." means it is likely that something has happened, and "she" refers to Chicago.

**17 Going quietly, are you?** (Para. 31)

**Meaning:** This is an informal way of telling the man from the West to cooperate and not to try to resist. In Chinese it roughly means "放老实点，知道吗？"

### Getting the ideas

**1**

1) policeman
2) 10 o'clock
3) scar
4) restaurant

**2**

**Teaching suggestions**

When retelling the story, Ss can use the table about how the story develops in Activity 1 as a help. They can do this in pairs, and take turns to finish the story.
Alternatively, T can give Ss some questions as follows. Ss can retell the story by connecting the answers to the questions.
1 When and where does the story happen?
2 Who is Jimmy and what is he doing at the beginning of the story?
3 Where does Jimmy see Bob? Do they recognize each other?
4 What does Bob tell Jimmy, the patrolman?
5 Who comes up to Bob 20 minute after Jimmy left? Who does Bob think he was?
6 What do Bob and the tall man do after they met?
7 How does Bob find out that the tall man is not Jimmy?
8 What is the ending of the story?

**Reference answers**

1 In a cold night, Jimmy, a policeman, is patrolling the street. He finds a man leaning in the doorway of a store, and comes up to him. When the man strikes a match and lights his cigar, Jimmy recognizes he is his old friend Bob, but also a criminal wanted by Chicago. Bob doesn't recognize that the policeman in front of him is Jimmy, and tells the policeman his appointment with his old friend made 20 years ago to meet here at 10 o'clock.

Jimmy leaves. About 20 minutes later, a tall man comes up to Bob, and gives Bob an impression that he is Jimmy who comes for the appointment. Bob is very happy, and walks down the street with him, talking about his experiences in the last 20 years.

At a corner, the bright light from a drug store makes Bob realize that this man is not Jimmy. To Bob's surprise, the man tells him that he has been under arrest. The man gives Bob a note from Jimmy. Only then does Bob come to know that the policeman he talked to was actually Jimmy, who asked a plain-clothes man to arrest him.

2 When Jimmy finds out that his friend Bob is a wanted criminal, he might have three choices.

The first choice is to avoid the moral dilemma. He can pretend that he doesn't recognize Bob as a criminal and "miss" the appointment. But in doing so, he will break his oath to defend the law while hurting his friend's feelings and leaving the man to continue to do the wrong. Clearly, this is not a good choice.

The second choice is to partly engage the moral dilemma. He can tell Bob the truth face-to-face and leave the decision to Bob whether to run or to confess. However, this is not in accordance with the law enforcement procedure. Bob is more likely to run, and he will still hurt Bob's feelings.

The third choice is to fully engage the moral dilemma. He can tell Bob the truth face-to-face and arrest Bob by himself. This is a very straightforward way to handle the situation. He will be doing his job as a dutiful police officer, but may do a great damage to his friendship with Bob at the same time.

None of the choices above seems a good one. He could not allow himself to sacrifice the justice of the law to let Bob leave, even though Bob is his friend, who has been so loyal to their friendship that he comes all the way from the West to keep their appointment. Then, he couldn't arrest his friend by himself. So in the story, Jimmy comes up with a "smarter" choice. He asks a plain-clothes man to arrest Bob, not too straightforward while protecting the law and order.

### Analyzing writing techniques

**Reference answers**

**The hints toward Bob's identity as a criminal:**
- As the policeman walked up to him the man spoke up quickly. "It's all right, officer," he said, reassuringly. "I'm just waiting for a friend. (Paras. 3-4)
- The light showed ... a little white scar near his right eyebrow. His scarfpin was a large diamond, oddly set. (Para. 6)
- The waiting man pulled out a handsome watch, the lids of it set with small diamonds. (Para. 10)
- "... I've had to compete with some of the sharpest wits going to get my pile. ..." (Para. 13)

**The hints that the first police officer might be Jimmy:**
- When about midway of a certain block the policeman suddenly slowed his walk. (Para. 3)
- "Until five years ago," said the policeman. "It was torn down then." (Para. 5)
- "... But I know Jimmy will meet me here if he's alive, for he always was the truest, staunchest old chap in the world. He'll never forget. ..." (Para. 9)
- The policeman twirled his club and took a step or two. (Para. 14)

**The hints that the tall man might not be Jimmy:**
- About twenty minutes he waited, and then a tall man in a long overcoat, with collar turned up to his ears, hurried across from the opposite side of the street. (Para. 19)
- "... You've changed lots, Jimmy. I never thought you were so tall by two or three inches." (Para. 23)
- The other, submerged in his overcoat, listened with interest. (Para. 27)

1 This company has always adhered to business ethics, so it is still in existence after a century of ups and downs and and is still popular with many customers today.

### Sharing your ideas

**Reference answers**

**From Bob's perspective:**
If I were Bob, I might think at first that Jimmy has betrayed our friendship. I might feel that our friendship should have been stronger than the law. But deeply I know that Jimmy has done the right thing and he is not to blame. I may also feel a sense of regret and guilt for my actions, knowing that I have disappointed my old friend. So on second thought, I may thank Jimmy for making the right decision. He is actually helping me correct my mistakes and restart my life with a clean slate.

**From Jimmy's perspective:**
If I were Jimmy, I might have struggles but I would have to arrest Bob, despite our former friendship. As a police officer, it is my duty to uphold the law and bring criminals to justice. Although it may be difficult to arrest an old friend, I must do what is necessary to protect our society.

**From reader's perspective:**
I think Jimmy's decision is both right and clever. Everyone is equal in front of the law. All those who break the law must be brought to justice. But in Jimmy's situation, he not only fulfills his duty, but also finds a smart way to handle the situation. Asking others to do the job for him is a good way to fulfill the police's duty and avoid awkwardness. If I were him, I would do the same. If I were not able to find another policeman at that time, I would arrest him by myself. Or I would persuade and accompany him to go to the police station to confess his crime.

**Suggestions for evaluation**

For this activity, Ss' answer can be evaluated according to the following criteria:
- whether they can put themselves in different positions and analyze Jimmy's decision from different perspectives
- whether they know what to do if they were facing the same dilemma

## Further exploration

The task in *Scenario* asks Ss to write a reflective journal about the moral lesson from *After Twenty Years*. The text in this part can give Ss an example of how to write a reflective journal about moral inspiration by analyzing the character's situation. The audio explains the importance of keeping moral awareness and suggests an effective way to do so – listen to our inner voice.

T can show Ss how to organize a reflective journal taking the text in *Further exploration* as an example, and tell Ss to refer to this pattern in their own writing.

### The moral dilemma of Jean Valjean (Text)

### Culture notes

**Victor Hugo**

Victor Hugo (1802—1885) was a French Romantic writer. He is considered to be one of the greatest French writers of all time.

**Les Misérables**

It is a French historical novel by Victor Hugo, first published in 1862. It is considered one of the greatest novels of the 19th century.

### Language points

**1 One character who stands out is Jean Valjean.** (Para. 1)

**stand out:**
1) be much more impressive or important than others 杰出；出色
*China stands out as the leader in high-speed rail technology.*
2) be easy to see or notice because of being different 引人注目；显眼；突出
*Her bright clothes always make her stand out in a crowd.*

**2 Branded a violent criminal, Valjean is refused by all employers and cannot make a living.** (Para. 2)

**brand:**
1) vt. describe sb. or sth. as a bad person or thing, esp. when they do not deserve this 给……冠以恶名
*We should not brand a person as a failure just because of one mistake they made.*
2) n. [C] a type of product made by a particular company, that has a particular name or design 品牌
*I think there is less brand loyalty in computers than there was a few years ago, since there are more choices on the market.*

**3 With no other hope, he takes up another identity, and eventually rises to prominence, ...** (Para. 2)

**Meaning:** He gave himself a new name, in the hope that he could overcome the obstacle brought about by his past criminal record, and begin his career from the beginning. Eventually, he was successful.

**rise to prominence / fame / power, etc.:** become important, famous, powerful, etc. 变得更加成功（或重要、强大等）
*He rose to power in the company when it was going through a financial crisis.*
*He rose to fame after starring in a successful movie.*

**4 Another man has been mistaken for Valjean, and will be sent to prison for a crime Valjean committed under his original name.** (Para. 3)

**mistake sb. / sth. for:** think wrongly that one person or thing is sb. or sth. else 把……错当成
*I apologize for my absence yesterday. I mistook the date for next week.*

**5 If Valjean remains silent about the mistake, he will be home free, his past buried forever as another man is sent to prison in his place.** (Para. 3)

**home free:** (AmE) (infml.) having finished the worst or most difficult part of sth. 成功完成最困难的部分
*After months of hard work, the team finally secured the contract and knew they were home free.*

**bury:** vt.
1) try to prevent people from finding out about sth. that has happened 掩盖
*The company attempted to bury the scandal, but the truth eventually came to light.*
2) put sb. who has died in a grave 埋葬；安葬
*We gathered at the cemetery to bury our beloved friend and bid our final farewell.*

**6 If he speaks up to reveal the truth, the man who has been mistaken for him will be set free, but Valjean's new life will fall down around him as he is sent to prison once more.** (Para. 3)

**speak up:** say sth., esp. to express your opinion 说话（尤指发表意见）
*I encourage everyone to speak up during the meeting and share their thoughts on the proposal.*

**Notes:** Literally, "fall down" means a building is falling apart or in a very bad condition. Here, the author （页底截断，接下页）

（接上页）uses it in a metaphoric way to emphasize that Valjean's new life will be destroyed if he tells the truth.

7 **The complexity of Valjean's problem hides a simple moral proposition … (Para. 5)**

**Meaning:** Although Valjean's situation may be complex and difficult, the fundamental moral question it raises is actually quite simple. It implies that underneath the intricate details lies a clear and basic message about right and wrong, ethics, or values that can be learned or understood from Valjean's moral dilemma.

8 **Valjean's awareness is perhaps the central moral message of *Les Misérables*. (Para. 6)**

**Meaning:** In the story, Valjean realizes that moral rules apply to everyone equally and he makes the right decision. This is the core value that Hugo tries to convey to his readers.

9 **The fact that the moral order applies equally to all is one that should regularly be recalled, most of all by those who hold power, fame, or wealth – the very things that so easily tempt us to minimize our moral obligations in the first place. (Para. 6)**

**Meaning:** People should keep reminding themselves that the moral order applies equally to all. This is especially important for those who are powerful, famous, or wealthy, because their privileges and advantages can easily make them neglect their moral responsibilities.

**Reference answers**

1) identity
2) criminal
3) mistaken for
4) two choices
5) his past
6) new life
7) suffer
8) moral order
9) moral obligations

**2**
**Reference answers**

1 The author provides background information on the novel and one of its main characters, Jean Valjean, in the first two paragraphs to set the stage for the moral dilemma that Valjean later faces. The paragraphs introduce Valjean's character and give context to his initial struggles as an ex-convict trying to reintegrate into society. This information is important because it establishes Valjean's reputation and background, highlighting the challenges he will face in his daily life when he is forced to confront a difficult moral decision. By presenting the complexity of Valjean's situation early on, the author sets up the central conflict of the novel and prepares the reader for the moral implications that will be explored in the rest of the story.

2 The focus of Part 2 is on the specific moral dilemma that Jean Valjean faces, the choices he has, and the pros and cons of each decision. These paragraphs discuss the ethical implications of Valjean's decision, including the impact it will have on himself as well as others around him.

In my reflective journal, I will examine the different values or principles at play in the decision-making process. For example, I will reflect on the importance of abiding by the law versus the desire to protect
（页底截断，接下页）

（接上页）oneself or others. Additionally, it may be helpful to explore the personal biases or assumptions that might impact one's decision-making process and consider ways to mitigate their influence. Finally, I will reflect on how this moral dilemma can be applied to other situations or contexts in my own life, and how I might approach similar challenges in the future.

3 The function of Para. 5 is to reveal the central moral message of the novel. The author places this paragraph before revealing Valjean's decision because it highlights the importance of this moral principle in the story and encourages readers to consider it as they follow Valjean's journey. By emphasizing this message upfront, the author reinforces the idea that no one is above the moral order, including powerful or wealthy individuals like Valjean, and establishes that this is a key theme readers should keep in mind when considering his decision.

## Moral awareness (Video)

### Scripts

1 This video explores the first step to being your best self, which means developing moral awareness.

2 Without moral awareness, people might accidentally make the right choice or they might accidentally make an unethical choice. This happens because they focus on other aspects of the decision-making process and ignore any ethical considerations.

3 Studies on selective attention prove that people generally see what they expect to see. If you focus too much on pleasing your boss, on getting along with your co-workers, on meeting sales or bonus targets, you may not even see an ethical issue which is right there in front of you.

4 The phenomenon that is called "ethical fading" can blind all of us to wrong ethical decisions if we're not careful.

5 It is our responsibility, as people who wish to live ethical lives, to keep ethics in our frame of reference. We can do so by reminding ourselves every morning in the shower that we wish to be good people, and that to meet that goal, we must always strive to act ethically, just as we must constantly strive to gain more knowledge and skills regarding the technical aspects of our jobs.

6 Behavioral ethics teaches that we must practice listening to our moral intuition, "to our gut." This is more effective than weighing both sides of the issue or trying to find excuses for a position that intuition tells us is wrong.

7 Our gut instinct is not always right, but we would be silly to ignore it. Some psychologists say this: "When faced with a moral decision, take a few seconds to pause and listen to your inner voice. Is there a hint of guilt or shame? Does something worry you? If so, don't ignore it." This is your moral awareness coming to the surface.

**Interviewee 1:**

8 Sometimes our gut feelings are a good indication that something wrong is going on, and it is hard to decipher when you should be listening more to your gut and when you should be doing more rational thinking.

**Interviewee 2:**

9 If you have a feeling that it's not the right thing, make sure you keep working until it is.

### Language points

1 **Without moral awareness, people might accidentally make the right choice or they might accidentally make an unethical choice. (Para. 2)**

**Meaning:** If people lack moral consciousness, they may end up making the right decision accidentally or an unethical choice without realizing it.

**accidentally:** ad. happening by chance; not planned 意外地；偶然地
*He accidentally dropped the cup of coffee while trying to put it on the desk.*

**unethical:** a. morally wrong 不道德的
*The researchers found that unethical behavior was closely related to greed.*

2 **The phenomenon that is called "ethical fading" can blind all of us to wrong ethical decisions if we're not careful. (Para. 4)**

**Meaning:** If we do not notice the moral aspect of decisions, we will not be able to see that we are making an immoral decision.

**blind sb. to sth.:** make sb. no longer able to think clearly or behave in a sensible way 使思维混沌；使失去判断力
*Parents' indulgence of their child may blind them to the child's improper behavior.*

**Notes:** The word "fade" basically means colors or lights become paler or less bright. "Ethical fading" is a process where the moral "color" – people's moral awareness – of an ethical decision is fading. In other words, people are not aware of the moral implication in their decision.

3 **It is our responsibility, as people who wish to live ethical lives, to keep ethics in our frame of reference. (Para. 5)**

**Meaning:** If we wish to live our lives based on moral principles and values, we should take moral aspects into consideration in every choice and decision.

4 **…, and that to meet that goal, we must always strive to act ethically, just as we must constantly strive to gain more knowledge and skills regarding the technical aspects of our jobs. (Para. 5)**

**strive to do / for sth.:** try very hard to achieve sth. 努力；力争
*He strives for a balance between his work and life.*
*He strives to strike a balance between his work and life.*

5 **Behavioral ethics teaches that we must practice listening to our moral intuition, "to our gut." (Para. 6)**

**Notes:** Behavioral ethics is the study of how people behave when confronted with ethical dilemmas. Research in behavioral ethics finds that most ethical choices are made intuitively, not a result of careful analysis of the situation. Unethical decisions are often made because of outside pressures, like the pressure to conform, and by situational factors that they do not even notice.

6 **This is your moral awareness coming to the surface. (Para. 7)**

**Meaning:** When something "comes to the surface," it becomes easy to notice. This sentence means that when your intuition tells you that doing something may be immoral, you have noticed your moral
（页底截断，接下页）

（接上页）awareness.

**Reference answers**

2) ethical
3) be good people
4) act ethically
5) moral intuition

**Reference answers**

### Sharing your ideas

**Reference answers**

- Both Jimmy Wells and Jean Valjean face moral dilemmas that force them to question their values and make difficult choices. While both characters strive to do the right thing, the nature of their dilemmas differs. For Jimmy, his dilemma involves choosing between his duty as a police officer to uphold the law and his loyalty to his friend, whom he must arrest because he has become a criminal. In contrast, Valjean's dilemma concerns choosing between revealing his real identity and staying silent, as he must decide whether to lose everything he has built, making those who depend on him suffer or allow an innocent man to be punished for his crime. Ultimately, both characters are forced to grapple with the ethical implications of their decisions and work to balance competing values in order to do what is right.
- Moral awareness plays a crucial role in the decision-making of both Jimmy Wells and Jean Valjean. For Jimmy, his moral awareness is driven by his sense of duty as a police officer and his commitment to upholding the law. Similarly, for Valjean, his moral awareness is driven by his conscience and his sense of responsibility. In both cases, moral awareness helps guide their decisions and prompts them to do what they believe is right, despite the difficulties or consequences they may face.
- Last semester, I took a volunteer job at the Student Union. I was arranging the University Hall for a celebration activity on Teachers' Day with other volunteers, and found that one of them was stealing pens from the venue. I was struggling with whether to report this kind of misconduct to our supervisor. On the one hand, I felt a sense of obligation to speak out against wrongdoing. On the other hand, I feared that this would damage my relationship with other volunteers. Eventually, I chose to have a talk with that volunteer, persuading him to return the pens and never do it again. Because I knew this is the right thing to do, and I wanted to help my schoolmate realize his problem and fix his mistake. Fortunately, he realized his fault and did as I suggested. We have become friends since then.

**Suggestions for evaluation**

For this activity, the evaluation could focus on the following aspects:

- whether Ss can distinguish the moral dilemmas faced by Jimmy Wells and Jean Valjean
- whether they understand the role moral awareness plays in decision-making and analyze the character's motivation from a moral perspective
- whether they can draw moral inspiration from literary works and relate to their own experiences of making moral decisions

> **Additional activity**
>
> If Ss don't know where to start when answering the last question in the second "Sharing your ideas", T may use the following activity to help them brainstorm.
>
> **Work in pairs and discuss what you would do in the following situations.**
>
> 1 You are standing in a queue in a supermarket, and suddenly someone jumps the line.
> 2 You are standing on a crowded bus. At one stop, an old man gets on and stands beside you. The young man who is seated in front of you is dozing.
> 3 Your friend tells you that he has a good way to cheat in the coming exam.
>
> **Reference answers**
>
> 1 I would politely point out that everyone is waiting in line and ask them to go back to the end of the line, or alert a staff member or security personnel and let them handle the situation. After all, queuing up is a basic moral rule that everyone should follow. It's important to remember that jumping the line is considered rude and unfair to others who have been waiting patiently. However, it's also important to handle these situations with respect and dignity, and not resort to aggressive or violent behavior.
>
> 2 This is really an awkward situation. It's a Chinese traditional virtue to respect and take care of the senior, and the senior have the priority to take a seat on a bus. But young people nowadays are very busy, and dozing on the bus may be an opportunity for them to take a rest. So I may look around to see whether other seated passengers around would like to offer their seats to the old man. Or I could try my best to help him stand safely.
>
> 3 Cheating in an exam is a violation of rules and should definitely be banned. It puts honest students at an unfair disadvantage. And the grade got by cheating is misleading to teachers. If my friend has the intention to cheat in an exam, I would talk them out of doing so. I would tell them about the harm to others and the punishment they would get.

## Project

**Sample**

Recently I read O. Henry's novel *After Twenty Years*, and found that it is an inspiring story. In the story, Jimmy is a policeman. He and his old friend Bob parted ways twenty years ago and made an appointment to meet again at the same place and same time when they parted. Jimmy is a true and staunch friend, as Bob puts it. He arrives at the place at due time, and finds that Bob is already there. But he also realizes that Bob is a wanted criminal. At this point, he faced a moral dilemma. Should he ignore Bob's identity as a criminal or uphold the law and arrest him?

From my point of view, Jimmy might have three choices. First, he can pretend that he doesn't recognize Bob
（页底截断，接下页）

（接上页）as a criminal and "miss" the appointment. But he will break his oath to defend the law and leave Bob to continue to do the wrong. The second choice is to tell Bob the truth face-to-face and let Bob decide what to do. But it's highly possible that Bob will choose to run. The third choice is to arrest Bob by himself. This is a very straightforward way to handle the situation and may do a great damage to their friendship.

Jimmy could not fail his duty and let Bob leave. Then, he couldn't arrest his friend by himself. So in the story, he asks a plain-clothes man to arrest Bob, not too straightforward while protecting the law and order. I think his decision is both right and clever. Asking others to do the job for him is a good way to fulfill the police's duty and reduce the potential negative impact on their relationship. If I were in Jimmy's shoes, I would do the same, or I would convince Bob to confess to the authorities and accompany him to the police station.

Jimmy's awareness is perhaps the central moral message of *After twenty years*. The complexity of Jimmy's problem hides a simple moral proposition: The law and moral order apply equally to all. Anyone who breaks the law must be brought to justice. When it comes to friendship or any close relationship, it is far too easy for us to focus on our relationships so that we ignore any ethical considerations in decision-making. But Jimmy knows well that the law and morality always take priority. He follows his inner voice, his sense of duty as a police officer, and his commitment to upholding the law. His moral awareness prompts him to do what is right, despite the difficulties or consequences they may face.

When we are faced with a dilemma, we should take a few seconds to pause and listen to our inner voice. If there is a hint of guilt or shame, we should do more rational thinking, and make sure that we make a right decision.

**Suggestions for evaluation**

| Components | Criteria / Traits |
| --- | --- |
| **Content** | • Relevance: Following given instructions of analyzing the character Jimmy and summarizing his dilemma; addressing the assigned task by commenting on Jimmy's decision and drawing moral inspiration<br>• Appeal: Including fresh ideas, being informative and persuasive |
| **Organization** | • Structure: Writing the reflective journal in a well-organized structure and arranging paragraphs with a clear layout<br>• Coherence: Using transitional words and phrases to make the reflective journal cohesive and coherent |
| **Language** | • Accuracy: Using expressions and sentence structures accurately and effectively<br>• Diversity: Using an adequate range of vocabulary and sentence structures<br>• Acquisition: Using as many as possible of the new words, expressions and grammatical structures learned in the unit |

## Extended reading

> In the previous parts, Ss have talked about moral decisions in literary works. The focus is on gaining moral inspiration by analyzing the characters' traits and the dilemma they are faced with. This part provides an expository essay to help Ss make further exploration of the themes of this unit. The essay first raises a question – whether we should avoid or engage moral dilemmas, then illustrates the possible consequences of both choices through an imagined situation, and finally encourages readers to engage moral dilemmas to make the world better. After reading this essay, Ss may better understand the importance of moral awareness and their responsibility to keep the moral order.

## Should we avoid or engage moral dilemmas?

### Language points

1 **Should we avoid or engage moral dilemmas? (Title)**

**engage:** vt. enter into contest or battle with 开始与……交战
*The troops engaged the enemy as they advanced into the mountains.*

**Notes:** In the text, "engage moral dilemmas" is used metaphorically, which means to confront the dilemmas, in contrast to avoiding confrontation with them.

2 **Parental advice includes … having a responsible adult supervising at all times. (Para. 1)**

**Meaning:** … one advice for parents is to make sure that their children are always under the supervision of responsible adults.

3 **This strategy concerns whether we should avoid moral dilemmas or whether we should readily engage them. (Para. 2)**

**concern:** vt. be about sth. 与……有关；涉及
*Most of her letters to her parents concerned activities she took part in college.*

4 **If you employ the moral avoidance strategy, you are guaranteed not to make the wrong choice, but also guaranteed not to make the right one. (Para. 3)**

**employ:** vt. (fml.) use sth. such as a skill, method, etc. for a particular purpose 运用；使用
*The researcher employed sophisticated statistical analysis in the experiment.*

5 **On the other hand, engaging moral dilemmas opens up the possibility that you might make the right choice, as well as the wrong one. (Para. 3)**

**open (sth.) up:** become or make sth. possible, available or able to be reached （使某事物）成为可能，可得到，可达到
*The online courses have opened up new opportunities of education for young people not in college.*

6 **If Jan begins to gossip, Betty can convey her concerns about saying unkind things behind the backs of their friends. (Para. 5)**

**concern:** n. [C, U] a feeling of worry, or sth. that worries you 担心；忧虑
（页底截断，接下页）

（接上页）*He expressed concern about the environment in his speech.*

7 **But Betty is concerned that she will not be strong enough to follow through with her plan, and might engage in the gossip to avoid confrontation. (Para. 5)**

**follow through (with sth.):** finish sth. that you have started 把……进行到底；完成（开了头的事）
*If you followed through with this book, you will get better understanding of the author's theory.*

**engage (sb.) in:** take part in sth; make sb. take part in sth. （使）从事，参加
*He has engaged in regular exercise since he was five.*
*The two professors were engaged in a debate on the nature of light.*

**Notes:** In the sentence, the author uses the expression "be concerned that" to express Betty's worry. We can also use "be concerned about / for sth." to convey the same message. Here are some examples:

1) *I'm very concerned about Betty's health. She looks pale.*
2) *Your teacher is concerned for your progress in getting your work done.*

8 **Here, Betty must weigh the values of different potential approaches. (Para. 7)**

**weigh:** vt. consider sth. carefully before making a decision 认真考虑；权衡；斟酌
*We should weigh all the factors involved before we start the project.*

9 **I'll call the argument the "Above the Fray" argument. (Para. 9)**

**argument:** n. [C] a reason or set of reasons that sb. uses to show that sth. is true or correct 论据；理由；论点
*Now that we've heard all the arguments for or against the proposal, let's vote on it.*

**Notes:**
1) The word "argument" are often used with "for" "against" or an attributive clause guided by "that."
2) Here "above" means "too good for something." For example, "above suspicion" means the reputation of someone or something is too good to be suspected; "above criticism" means the perfection of someone or something is too good to be challenged. "Above the Fray" argument means if you keep yourself away from moral dilemmas, it is impossible for you to make bad decisions. Then it seems that you behave so well that no one will put you into a dispute about whether you are responsible.

10 **It seems believable that, like the rest of our intellectual capacities, our characters begin with undeveloped capacities that we can refine with practice. (Para. 10)**

**capacity:** n. [C, U] the ability to understand or to do sth. 领悟（或理解、办事）能力
*All living creatures possess a capacity to learn to survive.*
*Artificial intelligence may have the capacity for diagnosis in the near future.*

**refine:** vt. improve sth. by making small changes to it 改进；改善；使精练
*She constantly refines her painting techniques in the utilization of color.*

**Notes:** The word "capacity" is often used with "for (doing) something" or "to do something." Here "intellectual capacities" include memory, reasoning, analyzing, learning, planning, and so on.

11 **Empathy is of utmost importance when navigating moral disputes. (Para. 11)**

**Meaning:** It is very important to understand other people's feelings when we are trying to find a good way to deal with moral disputes.

（接上页）**navigate:** vt. find the right way to deal with a difficult or complicated situation 找到正确方法（对付困难复杂的情况）
*The agents help new home-buyers navigate the complex process of purchasing a property.*

12 **We can only put ourselves in the shoes of others if we have some sense of what it is like to wear those shoes. (Para. 11)**

**put oneself in sb.'s place / position / shoes:** imagine how sb. else feels in a difficult situation 处在（某人的）角度考虑
*To understand the challenges of being a parent, put yourself in their place and try to manage their daily routine.*
*Before criticizing a colleague's work performance, put yourself in their position and think about the obstacles they may be facing.*
*If you want to understand why someone is angry, put yourself in their shoes and consider what might have triggered their emotions.*

**Reference answers**

1 D
2 A
3 B
4 C
5 C

## Self-reflection

> In this part, T can ask Ss to refer back to the "Try it out" in the Student's Book and reflect on what they have learned to complete the task. Ss can categorize their gains according to the same four aspects, i.e. content, language, structure, and communication. They can also reflect on how learning the unit may improve their moral awareness. Then they can write down their specific gains in the self-reflection sheet in the Student's Book.

## Translation of the texts

**Active reading**

**二十年后**

1 正在巡逻的警察沿街而行，神态威严。威严的神态是习惯性的而不是给别人看的，因为也没几个人在看。时间还不到夜里十点钟，但是，夹带着一丝雨意的阵阵寒风已使街道近乎空无一人了。

2 警官边走边推门。他以错综复杂的精妙动作熟练地摆弄着警棍，时不时转身把警觉的目光投向安静的街道，他健壮的身形和微微大摇大摆的样子形成一幅和平守护者的美妙图像。这一带街区的人是习惯早睡早起的。时不时能看到一家雪茄店或一间通宵营业的便餐馆还亮着灯；但大多数商铺都早已关门了。

3 走到某一街区的中段时，警察突然放慢了脚步。在一家黑了灯的五金店门口倚着一个男人，嘴里叼着一根没点着的雪茄。警察一走上前去，那人急忙开了腔。

4 "这边没事儿，警官，"他说这话好叫警官放心。"我只是在等朋友。这是二十年前定的约会。听起来有点儿滑稽，对吧？呃，如果你想弄清楚这是不是实话，我可以跟你解释。大约二十年前，这家店原是个餐馆——'大乔'布拉迪餐馆。"

5 "直到五年前，"警察说。"五年前被拆了。"

6 门口那人划了一根火柴点燃雪茄。火光照出一张苍白、方下巴、目光犀利的脸，右眉边上有一处微小的白色疤痕。他的领带针是一块大钻石，镶嵌的方式很奇特。

7 "二十年前的今晚，"那人说，"我在'大乔'布拉迪这里和我最好的哥们儿、世上最棒的伙计吉米·威尔斯一起吃饭。我和他在纽约一起长大，就像哥们儿俩。我当时十八岁，吉米二十岁。第二天早上我就要动身去西部发财了。你没法儿把吉米拽出纽约；他以为这是地球上唯一的一块地儿。呃，那天晚上我们约定，我们将在二十年后的同一时间在这里再见面，无论我们混成什么样子，或者得从多远的地方来。我们估计二十年后，我们应该都已经知道了自己的命运，发了财。至于好是坏都无所谓了。"

8 "听起来很有趣，"警察说。"不过在我看来，两次会面之间隔了相当长一段时间。自从离开后，你没有听到过你朋友的消息吗？"

9 "呃，有的，我们通过一段时间的信，"那人说。"可是一两年后，我们就失去了联系。你知道，西部很大，我一直忙得团团转。但我知道如果吉米还活着，他一定会到这儿来见我的，因为他一直是这世界上最忠实、最可靠的老伙计。他绝不会忘的。今晚我从千里之外赶到这个地方，如果我的老伙伴露面的话，一切都值得了。"

10 那个等候的人掏出一只漂亮的怀表，表盖上镶嵌着小钻石。

11 "差三分钟十点，"他大声说。"我们是十点整在这餐馆门口分手的。"

12 "你在西部混得相当不错，对吧？"警察问道。

13 "当然啦！我希望吉米混得有我一半儿好。他是那种闷头苦干的人，不过是个好人。我得跟一帮打我钱财主意的最精明的人竞争。人在纽约容易墨守成规。去西部才能让他变得机敏。"

14 警察转动警棍，又踱了几步。

15 "我得上路了。希望你的朋友能准时到来。如果他没按时来，你会离开吗？"

16 "我不会离开！"另一位说。"我会至少再给他半个小时。如果吉米还活在世上，到时候他会来的。再见，警官。"

17 "晚安，先生，"警察说着，沿巡逻路线继续前行，边走边推着店门。

18 此时下起了毛毛雨，寒意袭人，原先若有若无的微风也变成凛冽的寒风。还在街上行走的少数行人也都竖起了大衣领子，将手插进了口袋，阴郁而沉默地匆匆走开了。五金店门口，从千里之外赶来赴约的那个人抽着雪茄等待着，他与少年时代朋友的这场不确定的约会几乎到了荒诞的程度。

19 他等了大约二十分钟，这时一个身穿长大衣、领子竖起到耳朵的高个子男人匆匆从街对面走来。他径直走向正在等待的人。

20 "是你吗，鲍勃？"他犹疑地问。

21 "是你吗，吉米·威尔斯？"门口的人大声问道。

22 "天哪！"后来的人欢呼起来，紧紧抓住对方的双手。"是鲍勃，确定无疑。我就知道只要你活着，我肯定会在这儿找到你的。好，好，好！——二十年很长。老餐馆没了，鲍勃；我多希望它还在，那样我们就可以在这儿再吃一顿饭了。你在西部怎么样，老伙计？"

23 "好极了！它给了我想要的一切。你变了很多，吉米。我可没想过你还能再长高两三英寸。"

24 "哦，我二十岁以后又长了一点儿。"

25 "在纽约混得不错，吉米？"

26 "一般。我在市政府部门有个职位。来吧，鲍勃；咱们去一个我知道的地方，好好叙叙旧。"

27 两人挽着胳膊，沿街走去。来自西部的人，功成名就，有点膨胀，开始谈论自己的发达史。另一位缩在大衣里，饶有兴趣地听着。

28 街角有一家杂货店，灯火辉煌。他们走到这亮光里时，不约而同地转头打量对方的面孔。

29 西部来的人突然停住，松开了他的胳膊。

30 "你不是吉米·威尔斯，"他厉声说。"二十年是很长，但再长也不足以把一个人的高鼻梁变成塌鼻梁。"

31 "有时候会把一个好人变成坏人，"高个子男人说。"十分钟前，你已经被捕了，'滑头'鲍勃。芝加哥警方认为你可能会来我们这里，电报通知我们说她想跟你聊聊。乖乖跟我们走，好吧？这才算聪明。现在，在我们去警局之前，这儿有一张纸条，有人让我交给你。你可以在橱窗这儿读。是巡警威尔斯写的。"

32 来自西部的人打开交给他的纸条。开始时他的手还是稳的，但到读完的时候他的手却在颤抖。纸条很短。

鲍勃：我按时到了约定的地点。你划火柴点雪茄的时候，我看到了一张被芝加哥警方通缉的人的脸。不知怎么的，我自己下不了手，于是我转了一圈，去找了个便衣来干这活儿。

　　　　　　　　　　　　　　　　　　　　　　　　　　　　　　　　吉米

## Further exploration

**冉·阿让的道德困境**

1 维克多·雨果的著名小说《悲惨世界》以其丰富的情节、人物和主题而闻名。冉·阿让是其中一个突出的角色。在入狱近二十年后获释，他经历了一次惊人的转变。

2 最初，冉·阿让从监狱过渡到正常生活是不可能的。冉·阿让被贴上了暴力罪犯的标签，遭到所有雇主的拒绝无法谋生。在没有其他希望的情况下，他改名换姓，最终出人头地，成为了一家制造企业的老板，甚至是小镇镇长。

3 正当冉·阿让为新生活而奋斗时，一件事情的发生让他陷入了道德困境。另一个人被误认为是冉·阿让，他将因冉·阿让以原名犯下的罪行而被送进监狱。只要冉·阿让对这个错误保持沉默，他将他的过去就会被永远埋葬，因为另一个人将代替他被送进监狱。但如果他站出来说出真相，那个被误认为是他的人将被释放，他开辟的新生活将分崩离析，因为他将再次被送进监狱。

4 此外，冉·阿让关心的不仅仅是自己，好多人都靠他生活。如果他被捕，这些人也会跟着遭殃。如果他说出真相，那么受到惩罚的不止他一人。

5 冉·阿让问题的复杂性隐藏着一个简单的道德命题：一个人作为社会杰出成员的特殊地位不应该也不能够成为他们不为自己的行为负责的借口。

6 冉·阿让最终承认，没有人可以摆脱道德秩序的义务。冉·阿让的觉悟也许是《悲惨世界》的核心价值信息。道德秩序平等地适用每个人这一事实应该时常被提醒，特别是那些享有权力、名望或财富的人，正是这些东西很容易诱惑我们第一时（原图不清）就减少自己的道德义务。

## Extended reading

**我们应该逃避还是面对道德困境？**

1 父母通常会教育孩子避开道德危险。父母的建议包括避开某些同龄人群，遵守规则，确保在任何时候都有负责任的成年人监督等。父母往往认为，这样他们就可以让孩子远离那些可能做出错误决定的情境。

2 很多人认为这是非常直接的、也是最好的方法——不仅对孩子，对成年人也是如此。许多哲学家建议人们避免介入可能导致道德败坏的处境。当我们计划道德生活，思考我们想要培养什么样的性格时，我们每个人都需要采取一种个人策略。这一策略涉及遇到进退两难的道德困境时，我们是应该逃避，还是应该欣然面对。

3 选择面对还是逃避道德困境就像参与一场游戏，因为你无法事先确定每种选择的结果。如果你采用道德回避策略，你能保证不会做出错误的选择，但也保证不会做出正确的选择。另一方面，面对道德困境会让你有可能做出正确的选择，也有可能做出错误的选择。

4 要理解这两种方法如何运，考虑一下下面的例子。贝蒂的朋友简邀请她共进午餐。最近，贝蒂对与简共度时光有些疑虑。简喜欢八卦她们共同的朋友，贝蒂觉得她每次参与这样的谈话都是在做不道德的事情。

5 她在处理这种情况的两种不同方法之间犹豫不决。第一种方法，她可以和简共进午餐。如果简开始说长道短，贝蒂可以表达她对在朋友背后说闲话的担忧。她希望这样的对峙能让简反思自己的行为，并可能使她以后不再说闲话。但贝蒂担心自己不够坚定，无法坚持完成自己的计划，而且还有可能和简一起说闲话，以避免冲突。

6 贝蒂的第二种选择是拒绝简的午餐邀请，并且不再与简来往。在这种情况下，贝蒂不可能让简不再说朋友的闲话，但她可以确定她自己不会参与这种行为。

7 这种情况下，贝蒂必须权衡两种潜在方法的价值。贝蒂认为避免不道德行为的好处超过了可能劝阻简将来别说闲话的好处。她选择了避开道德困境，取消了午餐约会。

8 想象一下凯特，她和贝蒂有同样的感受。凯特也被简的爱八卦所困扰，但她认为可能阻止简的不良行为比自己可能参与说闲话更有价值。她和简共进午餐并和她对峙。结果是，由于凯特面对了道德困境，并做出了正确的选择，简的行为发生了改变。

9 哪些论点支持避免道德困境呢？我把这个论点称为"置身于纷争之外"。人们对自己的决定所造成的道德伤害负有道德责任。如果他们想保持体面的声誉，避免承担道德责任，他们就会避免做出错误的决定。道德困境是可能做出错误决定的情境。因此，我应该避开道德困境。

10 支持面对道德困境的论点有哪些呢？首先，道德上的直接面对给了我们培养道德品质的机会。就像其他智力能力一样，我们的品行开始于未开发的能力，通过实践趋于完善，这似乎是可信的。正是通过让我们自己接触各种各样的经历，我们才能塑造自己的品格。

11 第二个论点也与道德品质的发展有关。在应对道德纠纷时，同理心至关重要。道德困境几乎总是涉及不同个人或群体之间的利益冲突。只有当我们知道他们在其处境中是何感觉，才能设身处地地为他人着想。如果你不知道另一个人或群体正在经历什么，你就不会把他们的利益当回事。因此，培养强烈的同理心需要与世界接触。

12 面对道德困境可能是一种更好的方法，因为当我们面对困境时，我们有机会让世界变得更美好。只有一开始把自己置于需要做决定的情境中，我们才能做出正确的决定。

# Unit 5 Time off

## Unit overview

This unit focuses on the healthy way of spending leisure time and aims to help Ss reflect on the negative impact of inactive lifestyles and encourage them to engage in active leisure activities. The text in *Active Reading* describes an inactive lifestyle and the risks it would result in. The text in *Further Exploration* describes some fun leisure activities involving words. Then the video shows an example of how students can improve their university experience by joining a campus club. In *Project*, Ss are asked to have a discussion about the impacts of an inactive lifestyle on people, share their concerns about it, and give suggestions on interesting and active leisure activities. The essay in *Extended Reading* talks about whether we should take leisure as a means to improve work efficiency, or we should pursue what we really want. By learning this unit, Ss are expected to develop a healthy lifestyle and enrich their university life by taking part in active leisure activities.

（本页为单元整体结构流程图）

**Scenario**
网络平台节目"青年会谈"下周将邀请来自世界各国的大学生，讨论时下过多使用数字设备的现象，关注其可能引发的问题，并介绍一些有趣有益的休闲活动。学生将参与此次会谈。

**Active reading**（Leisure inactivities – how to relax and do nothing）
文章以幽默手法描写闲暇时间的无所事事，分析这种生活习惯的特点及其影响。

**Further exploration – Text**（A love of words）
文章描写了与文字相关的闲暇活动，通过阅读文章了解一些人人皆可参与的有益、有趣的活动。

**Further exploration – video**（Queen's dance club）
学习视频材料，了解参与校园社团活动为大学生活带来的益处。

**Project**
参与小组讨论，描述无所事事的生活状态，关注其不利影响，并介绍一些有益身心的休闲活动，对合理安排闲暇时光提出建议。

**Extended reading**（Why your leisure time is in danger）
阅读关于现代生活中休闲时间受到威胁的文章，探讨休闲是为了更好工作，还是做自己喜欢的事。

**Self-reflection**
回顾整个单元的学习，反思在内容、语言、结构、交际和行为上的收获。

**（单元产出目标）**
结合个人经历，描述无所事事的生活方式，关注其不利影响，认识到合理安排课余时间，参加有益身心的活动对丰富生活和保持健康的重要性。

**本单元课程思政元素备选清单**

| 素材 | 课程思政参考点 | 二十大精神 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Active reading (Leisure "inactivities": How to relax and do nothing) | 1) 鼓励学生利用课余时间培养兴趣爱好、参加社团活动和体育锻炼等，修养身心、陶冶情操、强健体魄；<br>2) 提醒学生不沉迷于手机、网络、游戏，不虚度时光，让生活充实而有意义。 | 通过本单元的学习，学生能够提升精神面貌，培养积极向上的生活态度。激扬青春，不负时代，不负华年。 |
| Further exploration – Text (A love of words) | 帮助学生了解不同类型的休闲方式，鼓励学生在闲暇时间选择有趣有益的休闲活动。 | |
| Further exploration – Video (Queen's Dance Club) | 鼓励学生利用课余时间参加社团活动，丰富大学生活，结识新朋友，丰富知识和技能，锻炼身心素质。 | |
| Extended reading (Why your leisure time is in danger) | 从不同的视角了解人们对如何度过休闲时间的不同看法，思考如何平衡学习与休闲，劳逸结合。 | |

## Scenario

（教学引导框）

The scenario in this unit can be analyzed from the following aspects:
**Setting:** Participating in an online TV program "Youth talk"
**Topic:** Impact of an inactive lifestyle
**Identity:** Interlocutor – Students' real identity; Recipient – other invitees of the program
**Purpose:** To show concern about the inactive lifestyle, and introduce interesting and active leisure activities

**Teaching suggestions**
I can help Ss organize their discussion by considering the questions: What do people do in an inactive lifestyle? What are their impacts on people? What leisure activities do you recommend? Ss can think about their own personal experiences in their discussion. For example, do they or their friends spend much time on digital devices? What do they do on digital devices? What are the consequences if they use them too much? What positive activities do they do in their spare time?
After the tryout, I can ask Ss to reflect on their difficulties while doing the tasks in four aspects, i.e. content (what they have learned to do concerning the topic), language (the expressions and sentence structures they have learned), structure (how to organize a passage) and communication (how to communicate with people), and write them down in the table in the Students' Book. I can also do a small survey to see what the biggest challenge was in doing this project.

## Active reading

In Scenario, Ss are expected to have a discussion about the impact of inactive lifestyles on people and recommend some interesting and active leisure activities. The text describes a typical example of an inactive lifestyle – being a couch / mouse potato. It also mentions some harms that idling time away in an inactive lifestyle may bring about. It can help Ss better understand the themes of the unit, e.g. what an inactive lifestyle is like, and what risks it has. Ss can learn a writing strategy, i.e. expressing opinions in a humorous way.

### Before you read

**Teaching suggestions**
Ss can do this activity in pairs.
Photo A is an image of a couch potato while photo B is a woman playing on a tablet computer. T can call Ss' attention to the details of the photos, such as where the man / woman is, what he / she is doing, and so on. Remind Ss to connect the photos with the title of the text to understand and describe the photos.
Ask Ss to think about their personal experiences when answering the second question. After Ss' discussion in pairs, T can randomly select several Ss to share their answers with the class. While doing this, T can observe whether Ss' understanding of the "active" and "inactive" way is consistent, and summarize the meaning of the inactive lifestyle used in this unit – staying at home and not engaging in any physical or mental activities in the spare time.

**Reference answers**
Description of the photos:
In photo A, a man is lying on a couch, watching TV. In photo B, a woman is sitting on a couch and playing on a tablet computer.
- From the title of the text, I think the text is about how to spend leisure time. It seems that it will talk about an inactive way of spending leisure time. But I am curious about what "inactivities", or "doing nothing" refers to. Is it about having a rest? Or sleeping?
- I often ask several friends to play basketball in my spare time. After sports, I will feel refreshed. I think this is an active way to spend my leisure time.
- In my spare time, I would like to read books I am interested in. Sometimes I may sit there reading for a whole afternoon. Is this an inactive way?
- I actually spend a lot of time on my tablet. I like to watch TV series or other videos. Often several hours will pass before I realize it. It must be an inactive way of spending my leisure time.

### Leisure "inactivities" – How to relax and do nothing

**Text interpretation**
本文以幽默的手法描述了当代很多人休闲时间无所事事，以看电视、上网的方式打发时间，以轻松诙谐的方式告诫读者以这样的方式虚度时光，无益于身心健康。第一部分（第一至第二段）为引言部分，引入主题，举例说明何为"闲暇无为（leisure inactivity）"。第二部分（第三至第十段）描述20世纪末出现的闲暇无为的生活方式——做一个"沙发土豆"，描述了"沙发土豆"的特点，以及这种生活方式带来的影响。第三部分（第十一至十三段）描述21世纪新兴的"鼠标土豆"，他们沉迷网络，浪费时间。第四部分（第十四段）为结尾段，以反语方式提醒读者，做"沙发／鼠标土豆"浪费时间，不利于身心健康。
文章没有以说教的口吻提倡读者选择有益的休闲活动，而是看似称赞无所事事的生活方式的好处，实则调侃这样的生活方式带来的不利影响，如缺乏体育运动，缺乏与外界的交流，注意力持续时间变短，不经过思考随意发表言论，浪费时间，耽误工作、学习、锻炼等。
在分析文中描述的生活方式的不利影响后，教师可引导学生反省自己的生活方式是否有益于身心健康。
作者在第十段提醒读者长期缺乏运动带来的健康风险，如"telly belly"。教师可引导学生进一步思考这种"inactive lifestyle"的其他负面影响，如体质虚弱、容易患病、影响心理健康，等等。同时，教师可鼓励学生讨论改善生活方式的方法，如推荐合适的体育运动以及运动的频率、强度和时间等，由此帮助学生形成良好的健康习惯和生活方式。
在阅读第十一至十三段时，教师可引导学生思考沉迷于手机、网络、游戏的不利影响。如第十一段中提到的"lack of interest in the outside world"体现了沉迷于虚拟世界可能造成自我封闭，疏于与外界联系，不利于心理健康。此外，本段最后提到的"leaving badly spelt opinions around the virtual world"说明沉迷网络的人容易养成懒于思考，不经考虑就发表言论的坏习惯。第十三段则举例描述沉迷网络的其他后果，提醒学生不要虚度时光，无所作为。
学习课文后，教师应鼓励学生培养积极向上的生活态度。年轻人如同朝阳，应该朝气蓬勃。紧张学习之余，可以培养兴趣爱好、参加社团活动和体育锻炼等，修养身心、陶冶情操、强健体魄，使自己精神面貌奋发昂扬。
青年兴则国家兴，青年强则国家强。年轻充满朝气，青春孕育希望。青年人要激扬青春，不负时代，不负华年。
本文写作手法轻松幽默，有讽刺、夸张，描写生动形象，读来让人会心一笑。学生可以结合预习活动、理解练习充分理解作者的言外之意。文中包含了许多针对虚度时光、无所事事及其带来的后果的描写，教师可引导学生结合词汇练习、翻译练习及数字平台的填空练习熟悉夯实语言知识，并在产出任务中有效使用这些词汇和表达。

**Culture notes**
The traditional form of **cricket** as described in the text did last a very long time and winning was not regarded as especially important. The idea was that playing itself was a sufficient reason – it is a fulfillment. Modern versions of cricket are shorter and more competitive.
**British Potato Council** is an official organization to help this very large sector of British agriculture.

**Language points**
**1** In its traditional version, nothing happens for five days and then the players often decide to call it **a draw**. (Para. 2)
**call it a draw:** if two opponents in a game call it a draw, they agree that neither of them has won 打成平局；不分胜负
**Notes:** The author is exaggerating for humorous effect when he says nothing happens for five days on the pitch. Also, note that the whole passage is written in a humorous tone.

**2** And everyone, players and spectators, feels utterly **fulfilled** by this non-event. (Para. 2)
**Meaning:** Both the players and the audience feel satisfied with watching the game of cricket for five days and seeing the game end in a draw.
**fulfilled:** *a.* happy and satisfied because your life is interesting and you are doing useful or important things 满意的；满足的
*After completing the task, I feel really fulfilled.*
**Notes:** If something is a non-event, it was disappointing or dull. Sports are supposed to be exciting. But in the case of traditional cricket, it seems nothing happens, without winners or losers. So the author humorously refers to it as a non-event.

**3** It's a good example of what we can call a leisure **inactivity**. (Para. 2)
**Notes:** Basically, "inactivity" refers to the state of not doing anything, not moving, or not working. It is the noun form of "inactive (not doing anything, not active)". In this text, the author uses it in his own way, seemingly making it opposed to "activity". In fact, the "inactivities" mentioned in the text – cricket, watching TV, playing online games, and so on – are all activities, but involve less physical or mental effort. The author calls them "inactivities" to imply the inactive lifestyle connected with them.

**4** … you know it is coming … a couch. Don't sit on a potato. (Para. 5)
**Meaning:** A couch potato, of course, will sit on a couch. But don't sit on a potato, although there is "potato" in "couch potato." The author is being humorous and making a joke about couch potatoes' intelligence.

**5** In fact, watching TV without a remote control becomes perilously similar to physical exercise, and probably should be avoided. (Para. 6)
**Meaning:** A couch potato in the true sense should do nothing physically. If you watch TV without a remote control, you would have to move to the TV to change channels, which will involve physical activity. And by doing this you will risk your status as a couch potato, hence such physical activity should be avoided.

**6** Remember that you have the attention span of a Maris Piper. (Para. 7)
**Notes:** Maris Piper is the name of a kind of potato. A potato, of course, has no attention span. Like avoiding physical activity, the author here is showing the feature of a couch potato in an ironic way – they don't concentrate on anything for long.

**7** So **there you are**! Couch potatoes are healthy. (Para. 9)
**there you are (also there you go):** *(infml.)* used to mean "I told you so" 就是这样
*There you are! I knew you would win!*

**8** … and is referred to by professional couch potatoes as "telly belly." (Para. 10)
**Meaning:** When speaking about the risk resulting from lack of exercise, professional couch potatoes give it the term – telly belly.
**Notes:** "Telly belly" is a rhyming phrase where the two words end with the same vowel. It is coined by the author, meaning a fat belly resulting from watching TV for a long time and lack of exercise.
Rhyming not only makes phrases or sentences sound good, but it also makes them more accurate, persuasive, and easy to remember. Examples of rhyming phrases include: *easy-peasy; hustle and bustle; survive and thrive; brain drain;* and so on. Many proverbs and sayings also employ rhyming. For example: *A friend in need is a friend indeed. No pain, no gain. You snooze, you lose.*

**9** Interestingly, this is a highly fashionable style known as a "muffin top." (Para. 10)
**Meaning:** A couch potato's stomach bulges over the top of their trousers, just like the edge of a muffin bulging over the top of a cake tin.

**10** Mouse potatoes are addicted to gaming online with their fellow vegetables around the world. (Para. 11)
**Notes:** "Vegetables" refers to mouse potatoes. This sentence means that mouse potatoes spend a lot of time on online games with other mouse potatoes around the world.

**11** In fact, it **risks** a threat by some rival mouse potato in a brighter time zone across the world. (Para. 11)
**Meaning:** When you leave your computer and go to bed, other mouse potatoes may be still playing because they are in a different time zone where it is still not night. They will win the game.
**risk:** *vt.* get into a situation where sth. unpleasant may happen to you 冒……的风险（或危险）
*Those who violate the regulations of using the laboratory risk severe penalties.*

**12** For time off, they usually perform extensive Internet searches or participate in online chat rooms, casually leaving badly spelt opinions around the virtual world. (Para. 11)
**Meaning:** When they take a break, they do a lot of searches on the Internet and take part in online chatting. They may leave their opinions on the Internet, which are full of spelling mistakes.
**Notes:** "Time off" basically means a period of time when you are officially allowed not to be at work or studying. But for mouse potatoes, it seems that playing games is a full-time job, so doing other things, like Internet searches or online chat, is like taking a break.

**13** …, they can even travel the world and visit countries by watching webcam streams. (Para. 12)
**Notes:** A webcam stream is a continuous live stream of video captured through a webcam, a digital camera that is connected to the Internet.

**14** This is called "mouse miles," and who knows, maybe one day everyone can exchange them for more potato chips at all good computer stores. (Para. 12)
**Meaning:** When a mouse potato gets enough mouse miles, maybe they can exchange them for potato chips at computer stores.
**Notes:** There is no such thing as "mouse miles." The author humorously associates it with airline miles. One can earn airline miles (also known as frequent flyer miles) when one buys an air ticket, and the accumulated miles can be used for free or discounted flights, or other rewards, such as hotel stays.

**15** So with a minimum of effort you too can have your own leisure inactivity. Become a couch potato! Earn that telly belly! Train hard as a mouse potato, and earn mouse miles! Get started today! There's so much time, and so little to do. (Para. 14)
**Notes:** The whole passage is written in a humorous and light-hearted tone. At the same time, the author is trying to warn readers of the harm of the inactive lifestyle in an ironic way, saying one thing but meaning the opposite. The last paragraph is an example of irony.

### Getting the idea

**1**
**Reference answers**
1) couch potatoes
2) features
3) inherent risks
4) mouse potatoes

**2**
**Reference answers**
Couch potatoes: a
Mouse potatoes: b, d
For both: c, e, f
**Others:**
Couch potatoes: changing channels every ten seconds; having a telly belly
Mouse potatoes: staying up late when playing games; taking no responsibility for the opinions they leave on the Internet
For both: having a shorter attention span

**3**
**Reference answers**
1 Some people who are tired from a whole day's work or study may not want to do anything on weekday evenings or weekends. Others who have not found anything they are interested in may also become couch potatoes or mouse potatoes, spending time in an easy way.
2 First, how people spend their time off may depend on their hobbies. Then people's work may also influence how they spend their leisure time. If they are physically exhausted in their jobs, they may want to listen to music or read in their spare time; if their work is more mental than physical, they may want to do sports in their spare time. Moreover, people's personalities may also be a factor. Outgoing people may like activities where they can socialize with others, such as joining a club. Introverted people may like individual activities better, like painting, or playing musical instruments.

### Analyzing writing techniques

**Reference answers**
1 If they don't have a remote control, they would have to make physical effort and get out of the couch to change channels. This is against the principle of "inactivity" of couch potatoes, so it should be avoided. This sentence is in an ironic tone. The author actually wants to warn the couch potatoes that they lack physical exercise.
2 No. There is no such thing as "mouse miles." The author humorously associates it with airline miles. You can earn airline miles if you fly often, and the airline miles you accumulate can be used for rewards like free flights or hotel stays. "Mouse miles" result from using the computer too much, but it is entirely useless. The author here means that overusing the computer in this way is a waste of time.
3 We often say "*There's so little time, and so much to do.*" The author put the sentence in an ironic way, to make the opposite more noticeable. When we are amused by the ridiculous version "so much time, so little to do," we will unconsciously remind ourselves of "so little time, so much to do." We all have much to do, such as our work, study, hobbies, sports, housework, and so on. But time is always limited. If we do nothing, we are wasting our life.

### Sharing your ideas

**Teaching suggestions**
T can ask Ss to do this activity in pairs, and ask them to think about the impacts of inactive lifestyles based on the mind map in the Student's Book.

**Reference answers**
1. Inactive activities involve little mental or physical effort. Individuals who have an inactive lifestyle may struggle with concentration and focus during their studies or while working. This can lead to decreased productivity and poor performance.
2. The lack of physical exercise may result in health problems such as weight gain. Moreover, being immersed in the virtual world for too long will separate them from the real world. They will neglect personal interaction and even are unaware of current events. Furthermore, excessive screen time and limited social interaction can lead to an increased risk of anxiety and depression
3. It doesn't matter to spend a little time inactively. But spending too much time being inactive will bring no good. In fact, the inactive way of spending spare time cannot bring us real relaxation. We will not feel refreshed. Instead, it often results in regret for wasted time.

**Suggestions for evaluation**
For this activity, Ss' answer can be evaluated according to the following criteria: Whether the impacts of the inactive lifestyle on people's mental health, physical health, social life, work, and study are presented, whether Ss can think of its impacts on other aspects, and whether Ss express their attitude clearly.

## Further exploration

The task in *Scenario* asks Ss to have a discussion about the impact of an inactive lifestyle on people and suggest good ways to spend leisure time. The text in this part can help Ss generate ideas for interesting activities and show them how to briefly introduce the rules of the activities and their benefits. The video gives another option for leisure activity especially for university students – joining a club.
T can connect *Active Reading* and *Further Exploration* by summarizing the negative impact of an inactive lifestyle and asking Ss to think about what "active" things we can do in our spare time. After learning the text in this part, T can guide Ss to offer solutions to the inactive lifestyle discussed in *Active Reading*.

### A love of words (Text)

**Culture notes**
**Specialist magazine:** There are many magazines, printed or digital, specialized in hobbies. For example, *The Woodworker* is about creative craft, like crafting toys, bookshelves, plates, and so on. *British Railway Modelling* offers expert modeling advice, the latest product reviews, news, and developments in the world of railway scale modeling; *A Needle Pulling Thread* explores different techniques such as quilting, knitting, cross stitch, embroidery and more.
**Wordle:** Wordle is a web-based word game created by software engineer Josh Wardle. The name "Wordle" is a combination of "word" and the creator's last name "Wardle."
**Lingo:** Lingo is an American television game show with many adaptations in other countries. The word "lingo" comes from the Latin root "lingua", which means language.

**Language points**
**1** It's certainly true that among their many **pastimes**, the English adore words, above all reading them and playing with them. (Para. 2)
**Meaning:** Undoubtedly, the English have a strong fondness for words as one of their many leisure activities, particularly when it comes to reading and playing word games.
**pastime:** *n.* [C] sth. that you enjoy doing when you are not working 消遣；休闲活动
*Reading novels and watching movies are my favorite pastimes during the weekends.*
**Notes:** Different ways to talk about "pastime" include "recreational activity", "leisure activity", "avocation", "distraction", etc.

**2** There are **approximately** 185,000 books published each year in the U.K. (Para. 2)
**Approximately:** *ad.* used to show that sth. is almost, but not completely, accurate or correct 大概；大约
*The construction project is expected to be completed approximately within six months.*

**3** The most typical hobbies … have at least one, if not several, specialist magazines devoted to them. (Para. 2)
**Notes:** The phrase "if not several" is used in the sentence to indicate that some of the hobbies mentioned may have more than one specialist magazine related to them. The phrase conveys the idea that while it is certain that each hobby has at least one specialist magazine devoted to it, some of them may have more than one. It signifies that the number of specialist magazines per hobby may vary and could be more than one.

**4** Many people love it because it **bears a striking resemblance to** the classic television game "Lingo." (Para. 2)
**bear a resemblance to sth.:** be similar to 与……相似
*Both children bear a very close resemblance to their father.*

**5** You may think that you would have to be fluent in or even have an excellent **command** of the English language to enjoy playing with words as much as the English do. (Para. 4)
**command:** *n.*
1 [U, sing.] (~ of) knowledge of sth., esp. a language, or ability to use sth. 对某事物的掌握，使用某物的能力（尤指语言）
*Employees should be able to demonstrate a basic command of reading, writing, and arithmetic.*
2 [U] the control of a group of people or a situation 控制；管辖；指挥
*The ship is under the command of Captain Blake.*

**6** But many of the word games are very simple and **good fun**, … (Para. 4)
**good / great fun:** used to say that an activity is fun and enjoyable 有乐趣的
*For those who love nature, hiking could be good fun.*
