**3**

**Reference answers**

1 Specialist magazine covers news and knowledge of a particular hobby, and often offers advice and instructions on skills.
I am interested in sports, especially basketball. I don't read specialist magazines, but I sometimes read official accounts specialized in sports.
2 Two kinds of clues are mentioned. One is somewhat direct and given in the form of synonyms; while the other is indirect, using various linguistic and cultural references.
3 To enjoy the word games, the only skill needed is to read and write a little.
4 Yes. Chinese people also enjoy word games. Many Chinese word puzzles make use of Chinese idioms and poems. In recent years, some TV programs featuring competitions on knowledge of Chinese characters, idioms, and ancient poems are very popular.
No, word games are not as popular in China as in the U.K. I think that is because traditionally, Chinese word games were played among scholars, who had expertise in writing poems and understanding idioms. The demand for a good master of idioms and the Chinese language makes word games difficult for ordinary people.

**Queen's Dance Club (Video)**

### Scripts

**Nicole Teplin (QDC Co-President)**

My favorite part of QDC is a sense of community. We offer classes for people of all different levels and backgrounds, and people from different faculties and years all come together based on their shared love of dance.

We offer 30 different classes, so each class meets once a week. During the class they do a warm-up, and they'll learn some technique(s) and do across-the-floor and stuff like that and learn combinations, but closer to recital time they'll start learning their dances.

There's (There're) four different levels of classes. We start a beginner and we go (to) advanced so we have a beginner level, an intermediate level, a pre-advanced level, and an advanced level. So we offer all different styles in those levels; we offer ballet classes, jazz, tap, lyrical, contemporary, hip-hop ... and then we have some specialty classes. So if they've never taken a dance class before, it's a really fun mix of exercise and dance, and it's more simple moves but it's really high-energy.

**Marina Pei (QDC Co-President)**

So another really important component of Dance Club is the social aspect of it because we really pride ourselves on the community that we've created. So outside of the classes that our students take, we try to organize different socials and events that they can attend to meet other dancers and kind of bond over the fact that they all love dance. Participating in the dance club is really gonna improve your university experience. It's great for mental health. It's also great for keeping up your physical activity. So if you're interested in signing up in the fall semester, we have online registration that goes on for about a week. Our recommendation would be to come out and try all the trial classes the week before registration, so you can kind of figure out what styles you might be interested in and what level you want to dance at, and like I said it's super easy to sign up. So what's unique about Queen's Dance Club is that one of our core values is really inclusivity, and I think it's well reflected in what we do in all the different classes that we offer. No matter what your background is in dance, whether you're coming from no dance experience at all and you just want to learn for the first time or if you're a competitive dancer that wants to get back into it, we have a dance class for everyone.

### Background information

Queen's Dance Club is a student-run recreational club. Queen's refers to Queen's University, a public research university in Kingston, Ontario, Canada. It was established by a Royal Charter issued by Queen Victoria in October 1841.

### Language points

**1 My favorite part of QDC is a sense of community. (Para. 1)**

**Notes:** A sense of community is the feeling of connection, unity, and belonging individuals in a group or society experience.

**2 ... and it's more simple moves but it's really high-energy. (Para. 3)**

**Meaning:** The class includes simple exercises of body movement, but it's full of vigor.

**Notes:** "Moves" refers to acts of moving the body or part of the body.

**3 ..., we try to organize different socials and events that they can attend to meet other dancers and kind of bond over the fact that they all love dance. (Para. 4)**

**Meaning:** We try to organize various parties and activities, where our members can meet other dancers, and they can establish friendships due to their common love of dance.

**kind of:** (spoken) slightly or in some ways 稍微；有点儿

*The two friends kind of had a misunderstanding with each other and were not as close as before.*

**bond:** v. to develop or create a relationship with sb. 建立（与某人的）关系

*Teammates who were strangers bonded instantly after the party.*

**Notes:** Socials are parties where people interact with each other (联谊会); events are planned public or social occasions (公开活动).

**4 It's also great for keeping up your physical activity. (Para. 4)**

**Meaning:** It also helps you maintain your physical activity levels.

**keep sth. up:** make sth. continue at its present level or amount, instead of letting it decrease 继续保持（维持）某事物

*They keep up their friendship through frequent telephone calls and lunch dates.*

**5 ..., one of our core values is really inclusivity, and I think it's well reflected in what we do in all the different classes that we offer. (Para. 4)**

**Meaning:** One of our most important values is inclusivity – we include people of different levels and backgrounds and people from different faculties and years in our club. The inclusivity is shown in the various classes we offer.

### Sharing your ideas

**Reference answers**

I think practicing Chinese calligraphy is a good way to spend my spare time. In the beginning, you need to learn ways to hold the brush, ways to write each stroke, and some knowledge of the structure of Chinese （页底截断，接下页）

（接上页）characters. We have a Chinese calligraphy club at our university, where we can learn basic skills and also communicate with other calligraphy lovers. Once you have grasped the basic techniques, you can practice on your own whenever convenient. When you focus on each stroke in your Chinese calligraphy practice, you will find yourself becoming patient and calm, and enjoying the inner peace throughout the process. You can also better appreciate Chinese aesthetics and the harmony in the combination of thin and thick strokes.

## Project

**Sample**

**(\*Words and expressions in red are from the input materials.)**

As you said, with the convenience of smartphones and other digital devices, it is too easy for us to indulge in an inactive lifestyle. I have this problem too. Sometimes after class, I am **desperate to chill**. I will find myself **a comfortable place** to sit, or sometimes I will lie down. Then I may keep that position for a long time while I read the news, watch videos, and play games on my phone. It often takes one or two hours before I notice how much time has passed. I agree with you that an inactive lifestyle is not good for people. Firstly, the things you read on the phone are mostly fragmented and entertaining. It only takes you several minutes and requires little mental effort to read. If you always read in this way, you will find that you have a **short attention span**, and it is hard for you to focus on long and serious materials. In addition, an inactive lifestyle makes physical exercises **undesirable**. As a result, you will feel uncomfortable in your neck, back, and eyes. In the long run, a **lack of physical exercise** may bring even more health problems. Moreover, when you are addicted to the virtual world, you will be deprived of spending time with your family and friends, or exploring the city where your university is. Watching **webcam streams** can never replace the feeling of being physically present somewhere. What's most important, indulging in an inactive lifestyle is a waste of time. You will not get relaxation from it. And after several hours in the virtual world, you will regret that you could have done much. You may read books, go to **gym classes**, review your lessons, and so on. **There is so much to do, and so little time**. If we choose to do nothing, we are wasting our life.

When you put down your smartphone, you will find there are many good ways to spend leisure time. For example, you can practice Chinese calligraphy. You could **start off by joining** the club at our university, where you can learn the basic skills of using brush and writing strokes. Then you can practice it anytime. At first, you may feel it is hard to manipulate the brush, but once you stick to the practice, you will enjoy it. Practicing Chinese calligraphy **is great for** improving your concentration and patience. It gives you a sense of inner peace. It is also great for better understanding Chinese aesthetics and the harmony in the combination of strokes.

**Suggestions for evaluation**

| Components | Criteria / Traits |
| --- | --- |
| **Content** | • Relevance: keeping relevant to the assigned topic in the discussion<br>• Appeal: Including fresh ideas, showing a sense of audience, being informative and entertaining |
| **Organization** | • Structure: Giving a discussion with main points covered<br>• Coherence: Making sure that ideas build upon each other in a logical progression<br>• Clarity: Using relevant and credible evidence to support the main arguments |
| **Language** | • Diversity: Using an adequate range of vocabulary and sentence structures<br>• Appropriacy: Using appropriate tone when talking to group members<br>• Acquisition: Using as many as possible of new words, expressions, and grammatical structures learned in the unit |
| **Delivery** | • Fluency: Delivering the discussion with a casual tone and moving the discussion forward by giving responses<br>• Engagement: Ensuring active engagement from all participants and making sure all participants ask thoughtful questions or provide meaningful responses |

## Extended reading

In the previous parts, Ss have learned to talk about how to spend leisure time. The focus is on the negative impact of an inactive lifestyle on people. This part provides an expository essay to help Ss make further exploration of the themes of this unit. The essay argues that making use of leisure to increase productivity is not what "leisure" is supposed to be, and encourages people to pursue what they really want in leisure time, instead of using leisure as a means to an end. Ss can better understand the importance of leisure time and how to get real relaxation.

**Why your leisure time is in danger**

### Culture notes

**Josef Pieper**

Josef Pieper (1904 – 1997) was a German philosopher.

**Bertrand Russell**

Bertrand Russell (1872 – 1970) was a British philosopher and logician. He had a considerable influence on mathematics, logic, set theory, linguistics, artificial intelligence, cognitive science, computer science, and various areas of analytic philosophy.

### Language points

**1 "A time like the present," he admitted, "seems, of all times, not to be a time to speak of 'leisure.'" (Para. 1)**

**Meaning:** "A time" means a period of time of history. Here, Josef Pieper means that the current time (post- （页底截断，接下页）

（接上页）war) is a period of recovery when people are busy rebuilding their countries from the ruins of the war. All the hard work seems to make it not a good time to talk about "leisure."

**2 But such periods of recovery, Pieper argued, were also an opportunity for societies to reconsider their collective goals, ideals, and values. (Para. 1)**

**Meaning:** Josef Pieper means that the time of rebuilding also gives us an opportunity to rethink what we really want. What kind of societies do we want to create? Do we build a society with or without proper leisure? Josef Pieper is reminding people of the value of leisure. He believes that leisure is the basis of culture, which should not be sacrificed due to work.

**3 ... have been calling for a four-day workweek, paid parental leave, and tighter limits on mandatory overtime. (Para. 3)**

**Notes:** In the U.S., employees who have completed at least 12 months of Federal service, are entitled to a 12-week paid leave on the birth of their babies.

**4 The well-meaning argument illustrates a tricky paradox. (Para. 4)**

**Notes:**
1) "The well-meaning argument" means the argument which means well – it has good intentions, although the effect is not necessarily good.
2) "A tricky paradox" refers to the quality of leisure. Leisure is of great value – but it is only useful when we don't use it to improve employee mood and increase productivity. When it is useful for work, it is not useful as leisure anymore.

**5 Leisure is doing things for their own sake, to pursue what one wants. (Para. 4)**

**Notes:** If you do things for their own sake, you enjoy the experience of doing them, without considering the advantages they may bring. For example, you play the piano because you enjoy yourself when doing this, not because you want to showcase your skills to others.

**6 ... both agree that time off fuels human creativity and innovation. (Para. 5)**

**fuel:** vt. make sth. happen, grow, increase etc., or encourage sb. to do sth. 促进；激起；鼓励

*Rising expectations about health can fuel further demand for health care.*

**7 Then, sometime around 1985, the trend reversed. (Para. 7)**

**Meaning:** Then at a time around 1985, average working hours began to increase and leisure decreased, as opposed to what happened in the mid-1900s, when average working hours decreased and leisure increased.

**reverse:** vt. change sth., such as a decision, judgment, or process so that it is the opposite of what it was before 推翻；彻底转变

*Cities are expanding and using up more land. Our aim is to reverse this process and protect our open spaces.*

**8 The same pattern now shows up in emerging economies. (Para. 7)**

**Notes:** An emerging economy, or emerging market, refers to the economy of a developing nation that is becoming more engaged with global markets as it grows.

**9 Later these calls were embraced by pragmatists looking at input-output tables. (Para. 9)**

**embrace:** vt. (fml.) eagerly accept a new idea, opinion, etc. 欣然接受；乐意采纳

*Young people are always eager to embrace the latest technology.*

**Notes:**
1) A pragmatist is a person who deals with problems or situations by focusing on practical approaches and solutions. The word "pragmatist" is often contrasted with the word "idealist", which refers to a person who acts based on high principles or ideals.
2) Input-output tables are used for macroeconomic analysis, which includes a series of rows and columns of data that quantify the supply chain for all sectors of an economy.
Here, looking at input-output tables metaphorically means weighing the input of time (expanding leisure time) and its effect on productivity.

**10 ..., for then leisure may be put before work instead of after work – where it belongs. (Para. 10)**

**Meaning:** (If we emphasize the humanitarian side of expanded leisure time,) leisure will be seen as more important than work. But in fact, work should still be more important than leisure. This implies that the factory gave workmen more leisure time in order to make them work better.

**11 ..., his workers showed up "so fresh and keen that they are able to put their minds as well as their hands into their work." (Para. 10)**

**Meaning:** ..., his workers came to the factory energized and eager to start work. They not only worked hard physically, but also focused their minds on the work.

**fresh:** a. [not usually before noun] full of energy because you are not tired 精力充沛的

*I had a good sleep last night, and feel fresh now.*

**keen:** a. wanting to do sth. or wanting sth. to happen very much 渴望的；热切的；热衷的

*My parents have always been keen on traveling since they were young.*

**12 If leisure is justified by its contribution to other social ends, it stands to lose any perceived worth as soon as it comes into conflict with those goals. (Para. 11)**

**Meaning:** If leisure is only believed valuable when it is used as a means to meet other societal goals, it may easily lose all its value when it clashed with those goals.

**stand to do:** be in a situation where you are likely to do sth. 很可能做某事

*You stand to get a promotion due to your excellent performance in the previous year.*

**13 The result is unused vacation days. (Para. 11)**

**Meaning:** (When leisure time conflicts with work, work will be given priority.) The result is that even if the company provides vacation time, employees may choose not to use them.

**14 Yes, this leisure time might generate great benefits for our knowledge economy. ... But as individuals, we gain from preserving a space for doing things for their own sake. (Para. 12)**

**preserve** vt. save sb. or sth. from being harmed or destroyed 保护；保存（使免受破坏） （页底截断，接下页）

（接上页）*The local government has taken effective measures to preserve buildings of historical significance.*

**Meaning:** Yes, it is true that leisure time can improve work efficiency, thus producing great benefits for our knowledge economy. But I don't think this should be considered as an ultimate goal of leisure time. Every individual should be able to keep their own leisure time and use it in their own way, when they can do what they really want to do.

**Notes:**
1) "Yes, ..." followed by "but...," is a polite way of introducing a different viewpoint or objection to a statement. You may partly accept the truthfulness of the statement following "yes," but you don't agree with it on the whole. Then you put forward your argument or perspective, which is after "but" to express opposition, contrast, or alternative reasoning.
2) The knowledge economy is the marketplace for the production and sale of scientific and engineering discoveries. It is a result of the rapid expansion of knowledge and the increasing reliance on computerization, big data, and automation.

**Reference answers**

1 D　2 D　3 B　4 D　5 B

## Translation of the texts

**Active reading**

**闲暇无为——或如何歇着无所事事**

1　人们在闲暇时间干些什么呢？很多人喜欢温和的休闲活动，如观鸟、钓鱼、园艺。他们甚至可能只是观看一项运动，给人一种有事可做的印象。

2　其中一个例子是板球。这是一种规则怪异的奇特游戏。传统规则下，如果五天之内毫无进展，玩家往往就会决定双方战平。而无论是玩家还是观众，人人都对这毫无结果的比赛感到心满意足。这是个我们现在可以称之为闲暇无为的好例子。

3　与几十年前相比，现代闲暇无为中的互动甚至更少。在 20 世纪末，一个新的休闲物种进化成功，他们认为躺在沙发上看电视是他们能做到的最令人兴奋的活动了。这就是沙发土豆。

4　那么，谁想当沙发土豆呢？好吧，事实上，许多人都想，理由也是可以理解的。也许今天是你一周中的休息日，也许你刚下班或放学。也许你累坏了，只想放松。

5　怎样才能变成沙发土豆呢？那很容易。在一个舒适的地方坐下，例如一张躺椅、一个豆袋椅，或……你知道那就是……一张沙发。可别坐在土豆上。确保你拥有所需的一切：零食（尤其是土豆片——严肃的沙发土豆不会吃别的）、饮料、杂志和电视。

6　最重要的装备当然是遥控器了。没有无需离开沙发就能换频道的自由，沙发土豆就配不上这个称号。事实上，没有遥控器看电视有变成近似体育锻炼的危险，或许是应当避免的。

7　现在，就开始看电视吧。每隔十秒钟换一次频道，然后继续换。别太用心，尽量不要对任何节目太感兴趣，因为这会威胁到你的沙发土豆身份。（记住，你的注意力持续时间只有玛丽斯·派铂马铃薯的那么长。）

8　但是，对于每种新时尚，都会有反对者。曾有报道称，英国农民对"沙发土豆"这个词的用法感到愤怒，因为它败坏了土豆的形象。英国土豆协会称，土豆是天然有益于健康的，他们甚至组织抗议活动，要求把"沙发土豆"一词从《牛津英语词典》中删除。

9　你瞧我没说错吧！沙发土豆是健康的。

10　当然，对于真正的沙发土豆来说，固有的危险还是有的。也许最大的危险来自缺乏锻炼，被专业沙发土豆称为"电视肚"。然而，有些人则视自己的电视肚为专注闲暇无为的标志，骄傲地把它系在裤腰之上。（有趣的是，这是一种极为时尚的穿法，叫做"松糕顶"。）

11　可是时间在推移，到了 21 世纪，我们现在又有了沙发土豆的翻版，特指电脑在线活动。这叫做"鼠标土豆"。鼠标土豆在电脑显示器前花费太多的时间，和他们的表兄沙发土豆一样缺乏对外界的兴趣。鼠标土豆痴迷于与世界各地的蔬菜同伴玩在线游戏。对他们来说，离开电脑是万万不行的。事实上，离开电脑会使你受到世界上较亮时区的鼠标土豆对手的威胁。休息期间，他们通常在互联网上进行广泛搜索或加入在线聊天室，在虚拟世界随意发表错字百出的观点。

12　对于更具冒险精神的鼠标土豆来说，他们甚至可以通过观看网络直播，去周游世界，造访列国。通过记录你的鼠标旅行的距离，你还可以算出你在电脑上花了多少时间。这叫做"鼠标里程"，或许有朝一日，在所有的好的电脑商店里你可以拿它换更多的土豆片。

13　当你终于决定关掉电脑时，你可能会想到你还有很多事情没有做。也许你几个月前买的那本书还没有翻过，它就放在你的电脑旁边。也许你报名参加的健身课已经被遗忘了很久了。带着懊悔，你告诉自己（就像你的表哥，沙发土豆一样），"没事的，还有明天呢。"

14　就这样，费最小的劲，你也可以拥有自己的闲暇无为。变成一个沙发土豆吧！养一个那样的电视肚吧！作为鼠标土豆刻苦训练，挣鼠标里程吧！今天就开始！时间那么多，而要做的事情那么多。

**Further exploration**

**热爱文字**

1　英国一份日报的读者曾被问到，当离家外出的时候，他们想念什么；回家的时候，他们期望什么。除了"家人"、"朋友"和"最爱吃的食物"之外，许多人都提到了"周日版报纸"和"填字游戏"。

2　这确实是真的。在许多爱好中，英国人特别喜欢文字，尤其喜爱阅读文字、玩文字游戏。读书是最流行的休闲活动之一。英国每年大约有 185,000 种图书出版。此外，三分之二以上的人经常读一份日报，包括线上的和线下的。更重要的是，最流行的业余爱好——钓鱼、集邮、收集火车头号码、观鸟、散步、插花、编织和养鸽——都拥有至少一种甚至数种专业杂志。事实上，许多人很可能花更多时间"阅读"爱好，而不是"践行"爱好。

3　报纸上、广播上、电视上、甚至网络上的文字游戏、字谜和相关的比赛非常受人欢迎，这也无疑是真的。最流行的网络文字游戏之一是"Wordle"。很多人喜欢它，因为它与经典电视游戏"Lingo"极为相似。这两个游戏中，玩家都有几次机会猜出正确的单词。每一次猜测之后，他们都会知道每个字母是否在正确的位置，是在单词的某个位置，还是根本就不在这个单词中。很多情况下，报纸都有一个文字游戏专栏，例如"词中词"竞赛，其目的是用一个词的字母拼写出尽量多的词来。其中人们最熟悉的文字游戏是填字游戏，其提示词从待猜词的同义词到以非常间接的方式表达该单词的语句，这些语句使用各种语言和文化知识做提示。显然，有些人选择报纸不是为了看其高质量的新闻报道，而是为了其填字游戏。

4　你可能以为，你需要熟练掌握英语，甚至精通英语才能像英国人一样玩文字游戏。但是，许多文字游戏都非常简单而且好玩；事实上，你所需的唯一技巧只是拥有一手读写能力。

**Extended reading**

**为什么你的闲暇时间受到威胁**

1　当欧洲从第二次世界大战中复苏时，哲学家约瑟夫·皮珀开始思考闲暇。"自古以来，"他承认，"现在这样的时代似乎不是谈论'闲暇'的时候。"但皮珀认为，这种复苏时期也是社会重新考虑其集体目标、理想和价值观的机会。

2　今天，我们又一次有机会重新思考我们想要怎样生活。

3　在过去的几个月里，许多专家和商业专栏作家一直在呼吁实行每周四天工作制、带薪亲子假、更严格地限制强制性加班。这些思想家中的人为这些归还我们的时间的提议辩护，承诺这类提议将有助于全面繁荣。他们的论据是，休息充分的员工工作效率更高，这是"对老板的回报。"

4　这个善意的争论说明了一个微妙的悖论。闲暇时间是有用的——但仅限于它仍然是闲暇时间。一旦这段时间被视为改善员工情绪和促进成长的手段，那么休闲就失去了使其如此强大的特质。正如皮珀所写，"闲暇不是为了工作而存在的。"闲暇是出于喜欢事情本身而做某些事，追求自己想要的东西。

5　这个命题看似简单，实则很难做到，因为闲暇时间恰恰是极其富有成效的。皮珀和哲学家伯特兰·罗素都认为，休闲时间能激发人类的创造力和创新能力。罗素认为，它"几乎贡献了我们所说的整个文明"。它培植了艺术，发现了科学；产生了各种著作，发明了哲学，并改进了社会关系。"

6　我们很多人都知道这一点：当我们在大自然中徒步远足时，或在淋浴时，或只是做白日梦时，灵感的火花就会突然闪现出来。

7　正当公司和政策制定者认识到休闲的价值时，员工们已经决定他们不需要休闲了。20 世纪中期，随着各国日益富裕，平均工作时间减少，休闲时间增加。随后，在 1985 年前后，这种趋 （页底截断，接下页）

（接上页）势发生了逆转。休闲时间开始减少，影响到了富裕国家中生活最优裕的人群。同样的模式现在出现在新兴经济体。最富有和受教育程度最高的人群比他们 20 年前工作时间更长。

8　我们渴望"充分利用"我们的空闲时间，所以经常把晚上、周末和假期用来自我提高。纯粹的休闲现在感觉就像纯粹的放纵。

9　人们是怎么把休闲看作达到目的的手段的？在对休闲矛盾性质的思考中，增加休闲时间的呼声首先来自思考人类尊严的理想主义者。后来，这些呼吁被研究投入产出表的实用主义者所接受。

10　上世纪 20 年代，一家工厂进行改革，将每周工作时间从 6 天缩短到 5 天，其负责人随后在接受采访时解释说："我们有一种观念，认为工人的休息不是'浪费时间'就是阶级特权。现在是时候摆脱这种观念了。"基于这些郑重的言辞，人们可能会误以为他是一个提倡美好生活的人。他很快就纠正了这种印象。"当然，"他接着说，"较短的工作日和较短的工作周有人道主义的一面，但过分强调这一方面很可能会让人陷入麻烦，因为那样的话，休闲可能会被置于工作之先，而不是工作之后——它应该在工作之后。"他发现，工人们多休息一天，上班的时候会"精力充沛、积极热情，能够全身心地投入到工作中。"

11　理想主义者和实用主义者之间心照不宣的联盟有什么不好呢？如果休闲的合理性是由它对其他社会目标的贡献来证明的，那么一旦它与这些目标发生冲突，它就会失去所有已发觉的价值。两者之间的冲突最终总会以有利于工作的方式得到解决。其结果就是休假日不去休假。即使员工们休假了，他们也会有一种冲动，想在海里游泳的间隙登录工作电子邮件。

12　人们不需要解释为什么想有闲暇时间。是的，闲暇时间可能会为我们的知识经济带来巨大的好处。它可能会无意中产生一些精彩的代码，前所未有的创新，以及文化的繁荣。政策制定者可能需要了解这些好处。但作为个人，我们为自己喜欢做的事保留一个空间，这对我们有一处。

# Unit 6 Science empowers

## Unit overview

This unit is about the power of science and technology in transforming our lives and improving our understanding of the working of the world. The text in *Active reading* shows some technological miracles China has achieved over the past few decades. The text in *Further exploration* explains the roles science plays in addressing societal and environmental challenges and the ways to better embrace science. Then the video highlights the importance of raising science literacy among the general population. In *Project*, Ss are expected to give a talk on how scientific and technological advancements have changed their lives and the way they understand the world, and how they can better embrace science. The essay in *Extended reading* provides scientific explanations for the phenomena that strike people as bad luck. After learning this unit, Ss are expected to be able to describe the influence of scientific and technological advancements on improving people's lives and identify the ways to embrace science and improve science literacy.

（本页余下为空白）

## Scenario

**Scenario**

学生所在学校的英语广播电台将举办名为"科学与我的"谈话节目，讨论科技进步对人类社会发展产生的积极影响。学生将参加此节目，请结合自己的体会和认识，讲述科技是如何改变了自己的生活和对世界的理解以及如何更好地拥抱科学。

**Active reading**
（Living in China's age of technological miracles）
阅读文章，了解中国在过去的几十年来取得的科技成就以及这些成就对提高人民生活水平和生活质量做出的重要贡献。

**Further exploration – Text**
（How the world can better embrace science）
学习如何通过沟通、合作和教育来更好地传播和普及科学，提高科学素养以应对未来社会和环境面临的挑战。

**Further exploration – Video**
（Science literacy）
通过视频学习，明白提升科学素养的目的不是让每个人都成为科学家，而是为了改变人们看待世界的方式。

**Project**
结合自身情况，阐述科学和技术进步是如何改变了自己的生活和对世界的认识以及如何更好地拥抱科学。通过分享个人经历或观点，让听众认识到科学和技术的重要性。

**Extended reading**
（Can bad luck be explained?）
阅读文章，了解科学对厄运之谜的解答，明白生活中困扰人们的很多事情背后都有科学解释。

**Self-reflection**
回顾整个单元的学习，反思在内容、语言、结构、交际和行为上的收获。

对科学和技术进步对人类生活的影响有深入的认识，能够更好地讲述中国取得的科技成就，了解普及科学和提升科学素养的重要性。

### 本单元课程思政元素备选清单

| 素材 | 课程思政参考点 | 二十大精神 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Active reading<br>（Living in China's age of technological miracles） | 了解中国在科技方面取得的卓越成就，体悟科技发展对增进民生福祉的重要作用，感受中国实施创新驱动发展战略，建设科技强国的决心和努力。 | 通过本单元的学习，学生能够认识到：<br>1）中国式现代化在科技发展方面取得的伟大成就及其对增进民生福祉的重要作用；<br>2）青年人应当加强培养创新思维、提升科学素养，努力向高技能人才的方向发展。 |
| Further exploration – Text<br>（How the world can better embrace science） | 认识到普及科学知识、加强各界合作、促进科技发展的重要意义。社会可持续发展的基础就是要不断向民众普及科学知识，将新的科学概念，新的事物更加理性、生动地讲解给民众，从而真正实现科学素养的提升。 | （同上栏） |
| Further exploration – Video<br>（Scientific literacy） | 激发青少年好奇心、想象力、探求欲，培育具备科学家潜质、愿意献身科学研究事业的青少年群体，鼓励学生在生活中勇于发现，不断探索新知。 | （同上栏） |
| Extended reading<br>（Can bad luck be explained?） | 认识到应将科学思维作为认识世界的工具，用科学知识解释现实生活，从实际出发，探求事物发展的内在规律。培养科学理性思维，进而用实际行动传播科学科学生活理念。 | （同上栏） |

## Scenario

The scenario in this unit can be analyzed from the following aspects:
**Setting:** Participating in a talk show held by the English radio station at your university
**Topic:** How scientific and technological advancements have changed your life and the way you understand the world and how you can better embrace science
**Identity:** Presenter – Students
Recipient – Audience listening to the talk show
**Purpose:** To show your appreciation of the transformative power of science and technology

**Teaching suggestions**
It is easy to take the convenience brought about by science and technology for granted. It is also easy to see science simply as a means to improve living standards, and fail to appreciate its value in making informed, thoughtful, and open-minded citizens. In this unit, Ss are expected to tell how technology has changed their

（页底截断，接下页）

lives, how science shapes the way they understand the world, and how they can better embrace science. T can ask Ss to imagine a life without the application of science and technology and without science literacy. For example, what would life be like without smartphones, modern means of transportation, or mobile payments?

After the tryout, T can ask Ss to reflect on their difficulties in four aspects, i.e. content (what science and technology have done to their lives), language (the relevant expressions, sentence structures, and the language style they have learned), structure (how to organize a talk in a clear and concise way), communication (how to add a personal touch to a talk), and write them down in the table in the Student's Book. T may also do a small survey to see what the top three difficulties in doing this project are.

## Active reading

From *Scenario*, Ss have already known that they are expected to talk about how their lives and view of the world have been transformed by science and technology. The text describes the remarkable achievements China has made over the past few decades by placing science and innovation at the heart of its growth strategy. It can help Ss appreciate how life in China has improved since the implementation of the Reform and Opening-up policy in 1978. T can ask Ss to imagine what it was like to live in China half a century ago and compare it with the life they are enjoying now. T can also remind Ss about China's world-class infrastructure and an unrivaled transport system as well as the cashless society that few countries can match.

### Before you read

**Teaching suggestions**
China has made remarkable progress in modernizing its society and it's important for Ss to share the stories of China's achievements and progress. To help Ss understand the pace and extent of China's development in improving people's lives, T can ask Ss to talk to their parents or grandparents to know what it was like to live in China in the 1970s. T can also encourage Ss to conduct online research to learn more about the technological innovations and advancements taking place in China. Some examples would include space exploration, deep sea exploration, BeiDou Navigation Satellite System, quantum computing, new energy vehicles, etc.

**Reference answers**

1) Life in China in the 1970s was simple and austere. There were limited resources and merchandise. I would be stuck in life when there was barely enough to eat. There was no reliable power supply, and kerosene lamps were the main source of illumination. Bicycles would be the most common mode of transportation, laundry would be done by hand, and home appliances would be rare. I would also need coupons to get monthly necessities such as flour, rice, oil, meat, soap, and cloth.

2) One thing I would miss the most about modern life would be my smartphone. I wouldn't know how I can survive without a smartphone and Internet connection. How can I make video phone calls to my family? How can I find my way in strange places without a navigation app? How can I listen to my favorite music and order food? It's really difficult to imagine a life without smartphones.

## Living in China's age of technological miracles

### Text interpretation

本文介绍了当代中国在科技领域取得的惊人成就。通过对比七十年代和如今中国老百姓的生活，我们能看到科学技术对改善人民生活的重要作用。"从煤油灯到绿色照明"讲述了中国从电力无法得到基本保障到现在拥有全球领先的特高压输电技术的发展；"从邮递员到 5G 网络"介绍了中国在电信基础设施领域取得的进步；"从慢速列车到高速旅行"描绘了中国在高铁技术方面的飞速发展；"从粮票到人脸识别支付"更是反映出了中国人民生活发生了巨大的转变，从物资匮乏，需要通过粮票来分配有限资源到如今便捷、舒适的生活。

本文列出的科技成就都是当代中国学生能够切身体会到的。通过学习主课文，学生能更好地理解中国实施创新驱动发展战略，建设科技强国的决心和努力，也能充分意识到，要想实现中华民族伟大复兴的中国梦，必须具备强大的科技实力和创新能力。

本文采取了以小见大的写作手法，通过细节描写来展示中国在短短几十年间取得的巨大技术成就。通过回忆邢女士小时候家人在煤油灯旁围坐，以及刘先生在慢车上长时间站立，展现了过去中国人的生活，并与现在的生活形成了鲜明对比。教师可结合写作策略分析板块，引导学生理解和赏析这些写作手法，并鼓励他们在完成单元任务时积极使用这一写作方式。

### Background Information

**China's Innovation-driven Development Strategy**
China will pursue innovation-driven development and create new strengths for development, according to the draft outline of the 14th Five-Year Plan (2021-2025) for national economic and social development and the long-range objectives through the year 2035. Focusing on quantum information, photonics, micro and nanoelectronics, network communications, artificial intelligence, biomedicine, modern energy systems, and other major innovation areas, China will establish a group of national laboratories and reorganize national key laboratories, thus establishing a laboratory system with a rational structure and efficient operation.

### Language points

1 **If China were able to build a time machine and ..., that person would probably be subject to a life without electronics and illuminated by only kerosene lamps. (Para. 1)**

**be subject to sth.:** make sb. experience sth. unpleasant 使经受；使遭受
*Every spring, when the flowers bloom, my sister is subject to seasonal allergies.* **Notes:** Here "if China were able to ..." is a construction in the subjunctive mood that is used to present an imaginary scenario and its hypothetical outcome.

2 **... remained necessary alternatives for a long time in the face of constant power failures. (Para. 3)**

**in the face of sth.:** in a situation where there are many problems, difficulties, or dangers 面对（问题、困难或危险）
*People were greatly inspired by his courage in the face of adversity.*

3 **Fast-forward five decades, and a reliable electricity supply is no longer a problem in China. (Para. 4)**

**fast-forward:** v. move quickly to a later point in a story or stage of development 快速前进（到故事或发展的后来阶段）
*Fast-forward several decades and you will see how technology has dramatically changed the way we live our lives.*

4 **She says she's now got used to the presence of a wide range of smart electronics and home appliances. (Para. 4)**

**Notes:**
1) The phrase "get used to" is similar to "be used to." The difference is that "be used to" refers to the state of being familiar with, comfortable with, and accustomed to something, for example, *I'm used to waking up early because I've been doing it for years.* The phrase "get used to" refers to the process of becoming familiar with, comfortable with, and accustomed to something, for example, *It took me years to get used to the hot and humid climate in Shenzhen after moving from Xi'an.* Both phrases are followed by a noun, pronoun, or the *-ing* form of a verb.
2) "Smart electronics and devices" is used to refer to devices that are controlled by computers and are designed to react in a suitable way depending on the situation. These devices are equipped with advanced features, connectivity, and automation capabilities and are used to make life more convenient. The word "smart" is now frequently used to form phrases such as "smartphone", "smart watch", "smart device", "smart city", "smart building", "smart house", "smart missile", "smart vehicle", etc.

5 **This allows for increased transmission capacity, extended transmission distance, and reduced transmission loss. (Para. 5)**

**Meaning:** China's effort of shifting its power supply toward renewable energy enables higher transmission capacity, longer transmission distance, and fewer transmission loss.

6 **..., connecting its vast inland regions to its energy-thirsty coastal areas, ... (Para. 5)**

**Notes:** "Energy-thirsty" means having a high demand for energy. "Thirsty" can also mean having a strong desire for something. For example, "knowledge-thirsty" is marked by the eagerness to learn new knowledge and expand intellectual horizons.

7 **Another important technological miracle is the huge leap China has taken in its development of the 5G network. (Para. 6)**

**Meaning:** Another significant technological breakthrough is the remarkable progress that China has made in advancing its 5G network infrastructure.
**Notes:** Here the word "leap" is used metaphorically to describe an impressive advancement or progress.

8 **This accomplishment can be explained by the country's continued, forward-looking commitment to updating its telecom infrastructure. (Para. 6)**

**Meaning:** China has been modernizing its telecommunications facilities with a pioneering perspective, and therefore has made a lot of achievements.

9 **The eagerness with which people waited for the arrival of postmen so they could connect with friends or family members, so typical of the 1960s and 1970s, is also a thing of the past. (Para. 7)**

**Meaning:** During the 1960s and 1970s, it was common for people to make contact with friends or family members through letter writing. Since delivery took a long time, people would often get anxious. This has become history.

10 **With a one billion strong population of Internet users, China is at the forefront of mobile communication. (Para. 8)**

**strong:** used with a number to show how many people are in a group
*The staff, now 900 strong, has doubled in three years.*
**at the forefront (of sth.):** in an important and leading position among a group of people or organizations, esp. a group that is trying to achieve sth. 位于最前列；处于领先地位
*Now artificial intelligence technology is at the forefront of modern innovation and development.*

11 **Real-time video calls or conferences, mobile payments, online shopping, and car-hailing, among other activities, are all powered by the country's impressive mobile network. (Para. 8)**

**hail:** vt. make a signal to a car or bus so that it stops for you 打手势招呼（出租车或公共汽车）
*I hailed a taxi on the bustling street to quickly reach my destination.*
**among other things:** used to say that you are only mentioning one or two things from a much larger group of things 其中，包括（用于表示在众多人或物中只提到一两个）
*Jane was excited to start her new job, which promised, among other things, a generous vacation package.*

12 **Boasting the largest HSR network in the world, China now has more mileage of high-speed track than the rest of the world put together. (Para. 10)**

**boast:**
1) vt. have sth. good, often an attractive feature that other people admire 拥有（值得自豪的东西）
*They are building a new mall in the town that boasts over 200 shops.*
2) v. talk too proudly about your abilities, achievements, or possessions because you want to make other people admire you 夸耀；吹嘘
*The company boasts that its packaging is recyclable.*

13 **The economy is becoming more innovation-oriented ... (Para. 12)**

**Notes:** The suffix "-oriented" is added to nouns and adverbs to form adjectives that describe what someone or something is mainly interested in or concerned with. More examples include: "market-oriented economy", "environmentally-oriented product", "profit-oriented business", and "future-oriented mindset".

14 **... technological changes that have happened in China are nothing short of amazing. (Para. 14)**

**be nothing short of:** used to emphasize that sth. is very good, very surprising, etc. 毫不逊于……；简直是……（用于强调）
*The actor's performance in the film is nothing short of spectacular.*

**Additional activity**
This text uses several compound adjectives to produce powerful, concise descriptions. T can explain the form and usage of compound adjectives to Ss and ask them to do the additional exercises.

A compound adjective (also known as a "compound modifier") is created by combining two or more words that modify a single noun or a noun phrase. A hyphen is often used between the words to clarify that the words are working as a single modifying unit. This form is quite useful when we try to condense a clause into a phrase. For example, "the train moves slowly" can be condensed into "a slow-moving train." This helps produce better descriptions and can make your writing more concise.

Here are some common forms of compound adjectives.
- Compound adjectives starting with numbers
  We can combine a number with a noun to create a compound adjective to indicate certain types of measurement, quantity, or time. In this case, the noun appears in singular forms. For example:
  a 65-year-old retiree
  a 20-hour train ride
- Compound adjectives formed by nouns and adjectives
  Both "noun + adjective" and "adjective + noun" can be used jointly to modify another noun. For

（页底截断，接下页）

example:
high-speed rail
energy-thirsty coastal areas
tech-capable smartphone users
- Compound adjectives with adverbs
  Adverbs are often used in conjunction with adjectives to jointly modify a noun. For example:
  ultra-high voltage
  super-fast train
- Compound adjectives with present or past participles
  Past and present participles can be used to modify nouns in the same way as adjectives, and they can be combined with adjectives, nouns, and adverbs to form compounds. For example:
  wide-reaching network
  forward-looking commitments
  color-changing lights
  ticket-checking machines

**Rewrite each of the following sentences with a compound adjective.**
1 The smartphones allow us to make video calls in real time.
The smartphones allow us to make ______ video calls.
2 She took a bullet train for five hours from Beijing to Shanghai.
She took a ______ bullet train from Beijing to Shanghai.
3 Thanks to the HSR, traveling for long distances becomes very convenient.
Thanks to the HSR, ______ travels become very convenient.
4 The government is committed to making economic transitions that are oriented toward innovation.
The government is committed to making ______ economic transitions.
5 Some experts that can think forward are proposing reforms to the educational system.
Some ______ experts are proposing reforms to the educational system.

**Reference answers:**
1 real-time
2 five-hour
3 long-distance
4 innovation-oriented
5 forward-thinking

## Further exploration

Ss have learned the power of science and technology in improving people's lives in *Active reading* and understand science as the foundation upon which we foster economic, technological, and societal progress. The text in this part explains the role science plays in helping mankind respond to societal and environmental challenges and ways to better embrace science. The video is about science literacy. The speaker gives his views on science, curiosity, and what it means to be scientifically literate. T can guide Ss to come up with their own ways to embrace science and what they think about science literacy.

## How the world can better embrace science (Text)

### Language points

1 **It helps us provide for a growing and increasingly resource-hungry world population by improving the way we farm while reducing our carbon footprint. (Para. 1)**

**provide for sb.:** give sb. the things they need to live, such as money, food, etc. 供养……；抚养……
*Without work, how can I provide for my children?*
**Notes:** A carbon footprint is the total amount of greenhouse gases (including carbon dioxide and methane) that are generated by our actions.

2 **... we are transforming our model of development from a linear one based on unsustainable fossil fuels to a circular economy dependent on bio-based products and processes. (Para. 1)**

**Notes:** A circular economy is an economic system in which resources are used and reused in such a way that they remain in the economy for as long as possible. This contrasts with the linear economy, in which raw materials are used only once before being discarded.

3 **This transition is made possible through scientific endeavors that allow more energy-efficient inputs, and more innovative ways to recycle and reuse. (Para. 1)**

**Meaning:** Scientific efforts have created the necessary conditions to make the transition from a linear economy to a circular one happen by enabling the use of more energy-efficient inputs and more creative methods for recycling and reusing materials.

4 **... we must better embrace science by making it accessible to the general public. (Para. 2)**

**accessible:** a. easy to understand and enjoy 容易理解的；易懂的
*Good science journalists are specialists in making complex topics accessible to a general audience while adhering to scientific accuracy.*

5 **We need to give science a human face. (Para. 2)**

**Meaning:** We need to make science more understandable to ordinary people.

6 **... we can tell stories about it that people can relate to. (Para. 2)**

**relate to sb.:** be able to understand a situation or the way sb. feels and thinks 理解……；认同……
*I often wake up very early — it's something most readers over 50 can relate to.*

7 **We also need to step up cooperation in scientific research and development. (Para. 3)**

**step up:** increase the amount of an activity or the speed of a process in order to improve a situation 使增加；使上升；使加快
*Governments should step up support for high-quality enterprises.*

8 **Science can do great things if we draw on expertise from different fields. (Para. 3)**

**draw on / upon:** use information, experience, knowledge, etc., for a particular purpose 利用；凭借
*As a journalist, it's important to draw on reliable sources of information for accurate reporting.*

9 **By cultivating a curiosity for scientific know-how, we can foster a lifelong love of discovery among younger generations. (Para. 4)**

**know-how:** n. [U] *(infml.)* knowledge, practical ability, or skill to do sth. 知识；实用技能；窍门
*We need someone with the know-how to set up and oversee a new branch of the company.*

## Science literacy (Video)

### Script

1  When I think of science, I think of a truly human activity, something fundamental to our DNA, something that drives curiosity. When you're a kid, you're born a scientist. What does a scientist do? We look up and say, "I wonder what that is. Let me go find out. Let me poke it. Let me turn it around." This is what kids do! Kids that can barely walk, what are they doing? They're turning over rocks, they're plucking petals off a rose ... they're exploring their environment through experimentation. That's what we do as human beings.

2  So what do we do? We prevent that. We prevent these depths of curiosity from revealing themselves even within our own residences. You know, we spend a year teaching our kids to walk and talk, and the rest of their lives telling them to shut up and sit down. What kind of environment is that?

3  The goal here is not to make everybody a scientist, that's not the goal. What a boring world that would be! You want artists; you want musicians; you want novelists, poets; you want the rest of this. What matters is whether they're scientifically literate and maintain that literacy and that curiosity throughout their lives, no matter what becomes their profession.

4  People think science literacy is being able to recite facts, and it's not that. It's a part of it, but it's not the

（页底截断，接下页）

---

（接上页）main part. The main part is, how do you look at the world? What does the world look like through your lens? If you're scientifically literate, the world looks very different to you. It's not just a lot of mysterious things happening – there's a lot we understand out there, and that understanding empowers you to first, not be taken advantage of by others who do understand it, and second, there are issues that confront society that have science as their foundation.

5  You can't look at science (and math) as some separate subject. It's fundamental to what it is to be alive, because it is all around us.

### Background information

1) The video is an excerpt from a talk given by astrophysicist Neil deGrasse Tyson. The talk was given in a narrative and casual style, so some sentences may not be well-structured. This is common in spoken language. The speaker focused on getting his message across, rather than producing grammatically flawless sentences.

2) The speaker gives a more detailed illustration of scientific literacy in another interview. Here is the script.

The center line of science literacy is how you think. If someone comes up to you and says, "I have these crystals. If you rub them together, it will heal your ailments." I don't want you to say, "Oh, that's bunk (胡说八道)." No. Because extreme skepticism (极端怀疑), such as that, and extreme gullibility (极端轻信) are two equal ways of not having to think at all.

So what's your next thought when someone approaches you with the crystals? It should be, "How does that work? How do you know it works? By what mechanism does it work? How much does it cost? Where did you get the crystals? What evidence do you have that it would work on me?" Start asking questions. And people who are self-deluded will not have answers to those questions, because if they did, they wouldn't be trying to sell you your crystals.

Those aren't incredibly complex questions. It's just a matter of curiosity expressed in everyday life, as you encounter things that you've never seen before.

### Language points

1  **When I think of science, I think of a truly human activity, something fundamental to our DNA, something that drives curiosity. (Para. 1)**

**Meaning:** To me, science is a human endeavor and it is a core aspect of our character. It is deeply ingrained in our nature and it fuels curiosity. A similar expression is "Science is in (one's) DNA."

2  **When you're a kid, you're born a scientist. (Para. 1)**

**born:** *a.* used for saying that someone had a particular condition, personal quality, name, or social status at the time when they were born  生来就有的

*He was born a leader, rising from humble beginnings to become one of the most prominent scientists and inventors in American history.*

3  **We prevent these depths of curiosity from revealing themselves even within our own residences. (Para. 2)**

**Meaning:** We do not allow kids to show curiosity even at our own houses.

4  **What does the world look like through your lens? (Para. 4)**

**Meaning:** How do you perceive the world from your point of view?

---

**Reference answers**

**1**

1) drives curiosity
2) shut up
3) scientifically literate
4) profession
5) recite facts
6) very different
7) taken advantage of
8) all around us

**2**

1  They are both curious about the world and they are both exploring their environment through experimentation.

2  If we are not scientifically literate, we will think there are a lot of mysterious things happening in the world and we will be taken advantage of by others for the lack of understanding.

### Sharing your ideas

**Reference answers**

Acquiring science literacy allows me to evaluate the quality of information that is presented before me. I learned to look for scientific evidence behind the claims others made. There are people who deny that climate change is occurring at all or that it represents no significant threat to civilization. These people have no basic understanding of science and only make judgments based on intuitions, hearsay, or sensationalism. In other words, they don't have science literacy and cannot discern facts from opinions or myths.

To improve science literacy, we need to communicate science in ways the public can understand. For a long time, there was a disconnection between scientists and the general public. Now, we are seeing more experts bridging this gap and engaging in science popularization. In addition, we should engage in scientific collaborations in which experts from different fields can work together to solve the challenges we face. We also need to encourage rather than suppress the curiosity children show to the world, and foster a lifelong passion for discovery.

**Suggestions for evaluation**

For this activity, Ss' sharing can be evaluated according to the following criteria:
- whether Ss show a clear understanding of the meaning and importance of science literacy
- whether Ss can give some examples to illustrate the importance of science literacy in understanding the world
- whether Ss can offer suggestions for how to better embrace science

## Project

**Sample**

Science is key to human advancement. It has changed so many aspects of our lives, from the way we communicate to the way we study. Nowadays, I **have become accustomed to** the **ease and efficiency** of various means of communication. I can quickly gain access to a mobile phone or a computer, and connect with my family and friends with the use of the Internet and social media. Maintaining long-distance relationships

（页底截断，接下页）

---

（接上页）through expensive phone calls or sending letters that took days or even weeks to reach their destinations is **a thing of the past**.

**As an outcome of** the advancements in science and technology, my studying experience has been revolutionized. Now I have several study apps on my smartphone that allow me to learn English vocabulary, read literature, and attend MOOC courses. Enjoying the convenience of searching for information online, I often **recall a scene in the past when** accessing information required going to libraries or searching through encyclopedias. During the pandemic, there has been an **explosive growth** in online learning. **A growing number of** schools turned to online conference platforms that allowed us to finish our courses. Can you imagine how we could continue our courses if such technology was not available?

**In addition**, science is **fundamental to** shaping my understanding of the world. Through **cultivating** critical thinking, I have acquired the ability to question and objectively evaluate information. Science has taught me to **draw on** effective information, analyze evidence, draw logical conclusions, and **distinguish fact from fiction**. One experience I think all of you can **relate to** is that when encountering viral information online, I **get used to** checking reputable sources and reliable scientific backing to ensure its accuracy.

It's impossible to overstate how many aspects of modern life are impacted by scientific knowledge. We all need to **embrace** science by making it more accessible to the general public, cultivating curiosity, enhancing scientific understanding, and drawing on various expertise from various fields. By doing so, we can pave the way for **making our own contributions** to a better world in the future.

| Components | Criteria / Traits |
| --- | --- |
| **Content** | • Relevance: Addressing the assigned task by describing how advancements in science and technology have changed the presenter's life, how science shapes the presenter's understanding of the world, and how we can better embrace science<br>• Appeal: Sharing personal experience or insight and using interactive questions to add a personal touch |
| **Organization** | • Structure: Having effective opening and closing to make the presentation complete<br>• Coherence: Using transitional words and phrases to make the presentation cohesive and coherent<br>• Clarity: Arranging the presentation with a logical layout and highlighting the key points |
| **Language** | • Accuracy: Using expressions and sentence structures accurately and effectively<br>• Diversity: Using an adequate range of vocabulary and sentence structures<br>• Acquisition: Using as many as possible new words, expressions, and grammatical structures learned in the unit |
| **Delivery** | • Fluency: Delivering the presentation in a smooth and clear way with a natural, colloquial flow<br>• Engagement: Using interactive questions to engage the audience |

## Extended reading

In the previous parts, Ss have learned science is the key to human advancement. To embrace science and improve science literacy, it's necessary to let people know the working of the world in understandable ways. The text in *Extended reading* is a popular science article that explains the phenomena in everyday life that strikes us as bad luck. It demystifies the popular belief that bad things happen in threes. It helps readers understand probabilities and how the human perception of chance is often flawed.

Ask Ss where they learn about science in a non-technical way (popular science magazines, newspaper features, television programs, websites, and so on). Encourage Ss to talk about the ideas in the text and assist classmates who may be having difficulty.

## Can bad luck be explained?

### Culture notes

The proverb "bad things come in threes" is a superstitious belief that unfortunate events or situations almost always arrive in groups of threes. In Chinese, there is a similar expression "misfortunes never come singly (祸不单行)," which is often preceded by another phrase "good fortunes never come in pairs (福无双至)." People in different cultures seem to draw the same observation. Thanks to some scientists, there are now mathematical and psychological explanations for this seemingly weird phenomenon.

### Language points

1  **Have you heard about Murphy's Law? (Para. 1)**

**Notes:** Murphy's Law is generally credited to Captain Edward A. Murphy, Jr. It is a familiar adage that states that "things will go wrong in any given situation, if you give them a chance," or more generally, "whatever goes wrong will go wrong."

2  **Toast always lands butter side down. (Para. 1)**

**Meaning:** When a piece of toast falls on the ground, it is always the side with butter that touches the ground. This is thought to be bad luck because the butter will stain the ground or carpet and will be hard to clean.

3  **Even the most rational person can be convinced at times that there is a force out there making setbacks occur at the worst possible time. (Para. 1)**

**Meaning:** Sometimes even the most intelligent and reasonable person can be persuaded that there is a mysterious force at play, causing misfortunes to happen at the most inconvenient moments.

**Notes:** The word "possible" can be used with a superlative for emphasizing that something has the most or least of a particular quality

4  **This popular notion would be unlikely to stand the scrutiny of any scientific study ... (Para. 3)**

**Meaning:** This popular notion would not be able to stand or survive scientific examination.

**Notes:** Here the phrase "stand the scrutiny" means "be able to withstand or endure the careful examination or critical evaluation".

5  **So "badness" is much better represented as being part of a range rather than something which is there or not there. (Para. 5)**

**Meaning:** "Badness" is not an either / or quality, i.e., it is not something that either exists or does not exist. Instead, it is a matter of degree.

6  **... but this one bad event can remain alive and kicking for many months. (Para. 6)**

**alive and kicking:** still existing and not gone or forgotten, esp. when this is surprising  还存在的；不被忘掉的

*Romance is still alive and kicking for the couple who will be celebrating their 50th wedding anniversary this week.*

7  **The time period has been extended as long as is necessary to confirm the original forecast. (Para. 7)**

**Meaning:** The idea behind the sentence is that after one bad event, people will predict more bad events will occur, and they will wait until such bad events happen, so as to prove the prediction.

8  **But it is more likely that a friend will tell you, "Three bad things have happened, isn't that typical!" than "Only two bad things have happened, which proves the theory wrong." (Para. 8)**

**Meaning:** Sometimes after the second bad event, nothing happens for a long time (a third bad event does not follow). In this case, we could tell people that our experience shows that the idea that bad luck comes in threes is wrong. But actually, we do not tell them so. Instead, we wait for the third bad event and then say the old theory that bad luck comes in threes has been proved to be right.

10  **It is related to probability and independence. (Para. 9)**

**Meaning:** This is related to how likely it is that bad events will happen, and the fact that some unlucky events are usually connected to one another.

**Notes:** Probability (概率) and independence (独立性) are two concepts in statistics. Probability is the calculated chance that something will happen. Independence means one event's occurrence does not influence the probability that the other event will or will not occur.

11  **So while the probability of being made redundant or of becoming sick may be small, the chance of both occurring together is almost certainly higher than the product of the two probabilities. (Para. 9)**

**Meaning:** The likelihood of being fired is small, and the likelihood of becoming sick is also small. But because these two events are not independent of each other, being fired may increase the likelihood of becoming sick. Therefore, the chance of both events occurring simultaneously will be higher than the probability of being fired times the probability of becoming sick.

12  **So much for the general pattern of bad luck in life. (Para. 10)**

**so much for sth.:** used to indicate that you have finished talking about a subject  到此为止

*So much for the situation in Germany. Now we turn our attention to France.*

13  **Let's say you have six traffic lights on your journey. (Para. 10)**

**let's say:** used to introduce a suggestion or possible example of sth.  （用于引出建议或举例）假如说

*Let's say the journey takes three hours, then you'd be there by two o'clock.*

14  **The chance of six green lights is the same as the chance of a coin landing on the same side six times in a row. (Para. 10)**

**in a row:** one after another, without anything different happening in between 接连地；连续地

*Tom has won the chess championship five times in a row, showing his exceptional skills in the game.*

15  **Red lights come up just as often when the driver is not in a hurry; it's just that the disadvantage of the red light is considerably less if time is not critical. (Para. 11)**

**Meaning:** People have the feeling that they meet fewer red lights when they are not in a hurry. But that's not true. The frequency of encountering red lights remains consistent regardless of whether or not the driver is in a hurry. It is just that red light will not bring as much inconvenience when time is not that important.

**Reference answers**

a) 2
b) 7
c) 1
d) 6
e) 11
f) 8
g) 9
h) 5
i) 10
j) 7

## Translation of the texts

**Active reading**

**生活在中国的科技奇迹时代**

1  如果中国能制造出一台时间机器并将一位市民送回 70 年代末的农村地区，这个穿越者很可能会经历没有电子设备、只能靠煤油灯照明的日子。与亲朋好友的交流将完全依靠邮差：如果长途旅行去市区，他们将不得不在缓慢行驶的火车上坐上数个小时甚至是好几天；在城里，他们会发现去杂货店买东西需要不同产品的票证。

2  或许中国无法制造出时间机器，但回顾过去，有助于我们看到中国在科技方面的进步如何彻底改变了许多人现在习以为常的生活的基本方面。

**从煤油灯到特高压输电线路**

3  65 岁的退休人员邢女士回忆起自己年轻时的生活，20 世纪 70 年代典型的夜晚场景是一家人围坐在一盏煤油灯边。后来电灯开始普及。但是，面对不时的停电，煤油灯、蜡烛和手电筒在很长一段时间内仍然是必要的替代品。

4  快进 50 年，稳定的电力供应在中国已经不再是问题。与此同时，人工照明已经变得既实用又有趣。邢女士现在拥有几盏触觉感应变色灯，还能与音乐同步。她说自己现在已经习惯了各种各样的智能电子产品和家用电器的存在。

5  如今，中国的电力供应逐渐转向可再生能源，并拥有大范围的特高压（UHV）输电线路网。特高压输电能够增加输送容量，延长传输距离，还能减少输电损耗。中国已建成世界上最长的特高压输电线路，将广阔的内陆地区与能源匮乏的沿海地区相连接，使得中国跻身电力供应质量最高的 20 个国家之列。

**从邮递员到 5G 网络**

6  另一项重要的科技奇迹便是中国在发展 5G 网络方面取得的巨大飞跃。这样的成就源于中国能够富有前瞻性地不断致力于更新电信基础设施。

7  如今的人们早已习惯了现代通信手段的便捷和高效。只有在过去的电视剧或电影中，我们才能偶尔看见有人使用转盘拨号式公用电话。二十世纪六、七十年代常见的人们为了能与家人或朋友联系而翘首期盼邮递员到来的情形也已经不复存在。

8  中国有着多达十亿的互联网用户，处于移动通信方面的前沿。科技智能手机的使用者如今生活在一个由移动互联网连接的世界。实时视频通话或会议、移动支付、网购、网约车等功能全部都是由该国令人印象深刻的移动网络所提供。

**从慢速列车到高速旅行**

9  中国在科技方面奇迹般崛起还体现在高速铁路（HSR）技术和动车。得益于数代科学家、工程师和铁路工人的不懈努力，中国已成为这一领域的领先者。

10  中国拥有世界上最大的高速铁路网，高铁线路总里程更是比全球其他国家加起来还长。高铁已经成为中国的经济实力、快速现代化、日益提升的科技实力以及不断繁荣的象征。

11  经常出行的刘先生乘坐一辆超快、舒适的列车，花五个小时从上海去往北京。他回忆起以前的一幕，那时同样的旅程需要花费二十个小时。他得在火车上站整整一夜，忍耐着缓慢的行驶速度。当到达目的地时他的脚已经完全麻了。中国现代高铁技术给人们的生活带来了巨大的改变。

**从粮票到人脸识别支付**

12  过去十年，中国在人工智能方面取得的惊人进步无疑是另一项不容忽视的技术奇迹。经济变得更加以创新为导向，这也使得人工智能技术与大数据和云计算一起优先推动经济增长。

13  由于中国在人工智能技术方面取得了令人印象深刻的进步，越来越多的火车站现在都在使用面部识别检票机。此外，人脸识别支付也出现了爆炸式的增长。现在的大多数年轻人只需用手机扫一下脸就可以买到东西，他们无法想象半个世纪前，人们必须依靠食品券（中文称为粮票）购买大米和面粉的生活。

14  在过去的几十年里，中国发生的技术变革的速度及程度令人惊叹。随着中国对科技发展的进一步追求，中国人民的生活在未来将会更加先进。

**Further exploration**

**世界如何能更好地拥抱科学**

1  今天，我们不断受到社会和环境问题的挑战，如全球变暖、工业污染以及能源和食品短缺。科学是解决这些问题的关键。它通过改进我们的耕作方式，同时减少我们的碳足迹，帮助我们养活日益增长但资源日益匮乏的世界人口。随着科学带来的经济、技术和社会进步，我们正在将发展模式从基于不可持续的化石燃料的线性经济转变为依赖生物产品和工艺的循环经济。这一转变是通过科学努力实现的，这些努力可以实现更节能的投入，以及更创新的回收和再利用方式。

2  为了使世界更加富裕和可持续发展，我们必须更好地接受科学，使其为广大公众所理解。我们需要找到更好的方法来传播科学，并教导公众区分好的科学和哗众取宠的言论。我们需要使科学变得更为人性化。如果公众觉得科学太难理解，或者与他们的生活相距太远，我们可以讲一些人们可以理解的故事。科技创业者也可以作为科学的代言人，分享他们的智慧和激情，展示他们如何通过对科学的热爱来改善世界。

3  我们还需要加强科学研发方面的合作。科学的应用需要世界各地的政府、学界、公民社会和企业的合作，因为他们都能做出重要的贡献。如果能够利用不同领域的专业知识，那么科学一定会做出巨大贡献。在世界最大的公司以及最有名望的高校里，物理学家、工程师、化学家和生物学家们正在通力合作，旨在解决我们面临的挑战，并改善全世界人民的生活条件。

4  不仅如此，我们还需要吸引年轻人学习科学。我们应该从小教孩子们学习科学，鼓励他们将科学作为一种职业。通过培养对科学知识的好奇心，我们可以让年轻一代形成对探索的终生热爱。

5  科学将是对未来社会和环境挑战的基础。如果我们要实现我们的目标就必须通过交流、合作以及教育来建立科学的声望与地位。

**Extended reading**

**生活中的倒霉事能得到解释吗?**

1  你听说过墨菲定律吗？凡是可能出错的，必定会出错。掉到地上的吐司总是抹了黄油的那一面贴地。每逢节假日必定下雨。你买彩票从来没中过奖。但是你认识的人里似乎有人会中……你是不是觉得自己生来就是个倒霉蛋？即使是最理智的人有时候也会对此深信不疑，认为冥冥之中有一种力量让他们在最糟糕的时期里灾祸连连。

2  厄运的原因有一部分是数学问题，还有一部分是心理问题。的确，人们对厄运的感知和一些有意思的巧合之间有着紧密的联系。

3  就拿"坏事成三"这种看法来说吧，这种流传甚广的观念可能根本经不起科学的检验，但是它必定有一些经验基础，否则这个说法就不会存在。那么，怎样的解释才是合理的呢？

4  首先考虑的问题是"什么算是坏事？"

5  有些事情只是稍稍有点不好，比如火车晚点五分钟。有些事情则是糟糕透顶，比如考试不及格，或是被炒鱿鱼。所以我们更应该把坏事的好坏看成是一个程度的问题，而不是非好即坏。

6  谈到坏事成三的问题，其中最关键的因素是第一件倒霉事持续的时间有多久及给人的印象深不深。比如说，你外出度假期间家里的水管爆裂了。也许不到一个小时你的家就变成了一片汪洋，而在接下来的几个月中你的脑子会不停地想起这桩倒霉事，因为你要把房子清理干净，还要和保险公司就赔偿问题讨价还价，这些都会让你不断地想起此事。

7  第一件倒霉事困扰你的时间越长，你在之后遇到另外两件倒霉事的可能性就越大。说不定一个月之后，你的车被追尾了。又过了一个星期，你的结婚戒指不见了。出了第一件倒霉事，你本来情绪就很低落，此时便会迅速把接下来发生的事情联系到一起，看作是相互关联的一连串事件。即

（页底截断，接下页）

---

（接上页）使这几件事情的时间跨度可能长达两个月之久，那也不会改变你的看法。这个时间跨度可以任意延长，直至能够证明你之前的预言是对的。

8  倒霉的时候，人们总是倾向于寻找能证实"坏事成三"这一理论的事例，而忽视那些有出入的例子。孤立的倒霉事每时每刻都在发生。仅凭这一点就可以推翻这一理论了。坏事也可以是成双出现的。但是你的朋友更有可能对你说："我一连碰到了三件倒霉事，可真是应了那句俗话！"，而不是说："我只碰上了两件倒霉事，这不正好证明了'坏事成三'这个说法不成立吗？"毕竟，说后一种话是在自找麻烦！

9  不过，至少有一个合理的解释可以说明为什么坏事会扎堆。这涉及到概率和独立性的问题。并不是所有的倒霉事都互无关联。任何一个人在被解雇之后都会心情抑郁，这会降低他们身体的抵抗力，使他们更容易生病，而身体反应也不会像以前那么警觉灵敏。因此，虽然人们被裁员或生病的概率都很小，但是这两件事同时发生的可能性几乎肯定要高于这两种概率的乘积。

10  关于日常生活中出现倒霉事的常见模式就说到这里。我们接下来来看一个大家都遇到过的具体例子：你在赶时间，而交通灯总是红色的。为了便于理解，我们可以把交通灯看作是投掷一枚硬币，出现红灯和绿灯的概率各为 50%。如果你路上遇到六个交通灯，出现六个绿灯的机会与硬币连续六次落在同一面的概率相同，为六十四分之一。

11  司机在不赶时间的时候碰到的红灯其实和赶时间的时候一样多；只是如果时间不紧急，红灯带来的不便要小得多。认为红灯出现的次数比绿灯多其实是一种错觉。产生这种错觉的原因很简单，因为司机有更多的时间去想红灯。在绿灯的时候，车子几秒钟之内就疾驰而过了，而红灯却迫使司机改变驾驶行为，出现片刻的紧张，还要失去一分钟左右的自由。所以红灯会在司机的脑海中挥之不去，而绿灯则在转瞬间就被抛到了脑后。
